<!-- problem-1: body_md -->
\label{wenti-quanshoulian}
    Is it true that
    $$
        \mathrm{dom}H(B_X)=B_X
        \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
        X\cong\ell^1?
    $$
<!-- end problem-1 -->
<!-- problem-2: body_md -->
\label{wenti-bibaobaohan}

Let $O\subset X$ be a Reinhardt domain, and let
$\mathcal{F}(O)\subset H(O)$ contain the restrictions
to $O$ of all continuous polynomials on $X$.
Is it true that
$$
\mathrm{dom}\mathcal{F}(O)
\subset
\overline{\mathrm{span}\{e_j:j\in\mathbb{N}\}}^{\,X}?
$$
<!-- end problem-2 -->
<!-- problem-3: body_md -->
\label{wenti-jingdiankongjian}
Determine the monomial convergence sets
for holomorphic functions and homogeneous polynomials
on the classical Banach sequence spaces.
<!-- end problem-3 -->
<!-- lemma-1: body_md -->
\label{yinli-fenkuaixiajie}
If $\textbf{x}=\left( x_{i} \right)_{i\in \mathbb{N}}\in X\setminus\overline{\mathbb{C}^{(\mathbb{N})}}^{\,X}$,
there exist $\delta>0$ and integers
$0=n_0<n_1<n_2<\cdots$ such that
$$
\|(P_{n_k}-P_{n_{k-1}})\textbf{x}\|_X>\delta,
\qquad k\in\mathbb{N}.
$$
<!-- end lemma-1 -->
<!-- proof-1: body_md -->
If $\{P_n\textbf{x}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}_0}$ is a Cauchy sequence in $X$, then,
since $X$ is complete, there exists
$\textbf{y}=(y_i)_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\in X$ such that
$
\lim\limits_{n\to\infty}P_n\textbf{x}=\textbf{y}.
$
Since the coordinate functionals are continuous, we have
$$
x_i=\lim\limits_{n\to\infty}(P_n\textbf{x})_i=y_i,
\qquad i=1,2,\cdots .
$$
Thus $\textbf{x}=\textbf{y}\in
\overline{\mathbb{C}^{(\mathbb{N})}}^{\,X}$, which contradicts
$\textbf{x}\in X\setminus
\overline{\mathbb{C}^{(\mathbb{N})}}^{\,X}$. The sequence $(P_{n}\textbf{x} )_{n\in \mathbb{N}_{0}}$ is not Cauchy in $X$. Consequently, there is $\delta>0$ such that for every
$N\in\mathbb{N}_0$ there are $b>a\geqslant N$ with
$\|(P_b-P_a)\textbf{x}\|_X>\delta$.
Since
$$
|(P_b-P_a)\textbf{x}|
\leqslant|(P_b-P_N)\textbf{x}|,
$$
\eqref{shi-fanshudandiao} gives
$\|(P_b-P_N)\textbf{x}\|_X>\delta$.
Starting with $n_0=0$ and choosing $n_k=b$ for $N=n_{k-1}$
gives the required sequence.
<!-- end proof-1 -->
<!-- lemma-2: body_md -->
\label{yinli-xuliekongjian}
The following assertions hold.
\begin{enumerate}
\item\label{xiang-touyingxingzhi} For every $\textbf{z}\in X$ and $n\in\mathbb{N}_0$, the projections in \eqref{shi-shouweitouying} satisfy
        \begin{equation}
            \|P_n\textbf{z}\|_X\leqslant\|\textbf{z}\|_X,
            \qquad
            \|Q_n\textbf{z}\|_X\leqslant\|\textbf{z}\|_X.
            \label{shi-shouweisuosuo}
        \end{equation}
        Moreover, for every
        $\textbf{z}\in\overline{\mathbb{C}^{(\mathbb{N})}}^{\,X}$,
        \begin{equation}
            \lim\limits_{n\to\infty}\|P_n\textbf{z}-\textbf{z}\|_X
            =
            \lim\limits_{n\to\infty}\|Q_n\textbf{z}\|_X
            =
            0.
            \label{shi-weibushoulian}
        \end{equation}

        \item\label{xiang-bibaokongjian}
        The space $\overline{\mathbb{C}^{(\mathbb{N})}}^{\,X}$
        equipped with the norm inherited from $X$ is a complex Banach sequence space.
        \item\label{xiang-wutiaojianji} The sequence $(e_i)_{i\in\mathbb{N}}$ is a $1$-unconditional Schauder basis of $X$ if and only if
    $
        X=\overline{\mathbb{C}^{(\mathbb{N})}}^{\,X}.
    $
        \item\label{xiang-kefenxing} The space $X$ is separable if and only if
    $
        X=\overline{\mathbb{C}^{(\mathbb{N})}}^{\,X}.
    $
    \end{enumerate}
<!-- end lemma-2 -->
<!-- proof-2: body_md -->
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{Step 1}\par\nopagebreak[4]

    For every
$\textbf{z}\in X$,
    since
    $$
    |P_n\textbf{z}|\leqslant|\textbf{z}|,
    \qquad
    |Q_n\textbf{z}|\leqslant|\textbf{z}|,
    $$
    It follows from \eqref{shi-fanshudandiao} that \eqref{shi-shouweisuosuo} holds.

Fix $\textbf{z}\in\overline{\mathbb{C}^{(\mathbb{N})}}^{\,X}$ and
$\varepsilon>0$. Choose $\textbf{y}\in\mathbb{C}^{(\mathbb{N})}$ with
$\|\textbf{z}-\textbf{y}\|_X<\varepsilon$, and choose $N\in\mathbb{N}$
with $\mathrm{supp}\textbf{y}\subset\{1,\cdots,N\}$.
For $n\geqslant N$, one has $Q_n\textbf{y}=0$, so
\eqref{shi-shouweisuosuo} gives
$$
\|\textbf{z}-P_n\textbf{z}\|_X
=\|Q_n(\textbf{z}-\textbf{y})\|_X
\leqslant\|\textbf{z}-\textbf{y}\|_X<\varepsilon.
$$
This proves \eqref{shi-weibushoulian}.

\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{Step 2}\par\nopagebreak[4]

Let $\textbf{z}\in\overline{\mathbb{C}^{(\mathbb{N})}}^{\,X}$
and let $\textbf{w}\in\mathbb{C}^{\mathbb{N}}$ satisfy
$$
|\textbf{w}|\leqslant|\textbf{z}|.
$$
Since $X$ is a complex Banach sequence space, it follows that
$\textbf{w}\in X$. Moreover,
$$
|Q_n\textbf{w}|
\leqslant
|Q_n\textbf{z}|,
\qquad n=1,2,\cdots,
$$
and hence
$$
\|Q_n\textbf{w}\|_X
\leqslant
\|Q_n\textbf{z}\|_X,
\qquad n=1,2,\cdots.
$$
By \eqref{shi-weibushoulian}, we obtain
$$
\lim\limits_{n\to\infty}
\|P_n\textbf{w}-\textbf{w}\|_X
=
\lim\limits_{n\to\infty}
\|Q_n\textbf{w}\|_X
=
0.
$$
As $P_n\textbf{w}\in\mathrm{span}\{e_1,\cdots,e_n\}$ for every
$n\in\mathbb{N}$, it follows that
$
\textbf{w}\in
\overline{\mathbb{C}^{(\mathbb{N})}}^{\,X}.
$
Thus
$
\overline{\mathbb{C}^{(\mathbb{N})}}^{\,X}
$
equipped with the norm inherited from $X$ is a complex Banach sequence
space.

\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{Step 3}\par\nopagebreak[4]

The necessity of \hyperref[xiang-wutiaojianji]{\textup{(\ref*{xiang-wutiaojianji})}} is immediate. We prove the sufficiency. Assume that
$
X=\overline{\mathbb{C}^{(\mathbb{N})}}^{\,X}.
$
For any
$
\textbf{x}=(x_i)_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\in X, n\in\mathbb{N},
$
and any  $\lambda_1,\cdots,\lambda_n\in\mathbb{C}$ satisfying
$
|\lambda_i|\leqslant 1, i=1,\cdots,n,
$
we have
\begin{equation}
    \left\|
    \sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}\lambda_i x_i e_i
    \right\|_X
    \leqslant
    \left\|
    \sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}x_i e_i
    \right\|_X.\label{shi-zuobiaosuofang}
\end{equation}
On the other hand, by \eqref{shi-weibushoulian} and the assumption
$
X=\overline{\mathbb{C}^{(\mathbb{N})}}^{\,X},
$
it follows that $(e_i)_{i\in\mathbb{N}}$ is a Schauder basis of $X$. Together with the
 inequality \eqref{shi-zuobiaosuofang}, this shows that $(e_i)_{i\in\mathbb{N}}$ is a
$1$-unconditional Schauder basis of $X$. Thus the sufficiency of \hyperref[xiang-wutiaojianji]{\textup{(\ref*{xiang-wutiaojianji})}} follows.

\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{Step 4}\par\nopagebreak[4]

To prove \hyperref[xiang-kefenxing]{\textup{(\ref*{xiang-kefenxing})}}, suppose that $X$ is separable but
$\textbf{x}=\left( x_{j} \right)_{j\in \mathbb{N}}\in X\setminus\overline{\mathbb{C}^{(\mathbb{N})}}^{\,X}$.
Choose $\delta,(n_k)_{k\in \mathbb{N}_{0}}$ as in
Lemma~\ref{yinli-fenkuaixiajie}.
For each subset $A\subset\mathbb{N}$, define the sequence
$\textbf{x}^A=\left( x_{j}^{A} \right)_{j\in \mathbb{N}}$ by
$$
x_{j}^{A}=\begin{cases}x_{j},&j\in \bigcup\limits_{k\in A} \left\{ n_{k-1}+1,\cdots ,n_{k} \right\} ,\\ 0,&\text{otherwise} .\end{cases}
$$
Since $|\textbf{x}^A|\leqslant|\textbf{x}|$,
\eqref{shi-fanshudandiao} gives $\textbf{x}^A\in X$.
If $A\neq B$, choose a $k\in \mathbb{N}$ that belongs to exactly one of
$A$ and $B$.
It follows from Lemma~\ref{yinli-fenkuaixiajie} and \eqref{shi-fanshudandiao} that
$$
|(P_{n_k}-P_{n_{k-1}})\textbf{x}|
\leqslant|\textbf{x}^A-\textbf{x}^B|
\Rightarrow
\|\textbf{x}^A-\textbf{x}^B\|_X>\delta.
$$
Now $X$ contains an uncountable family of pairwise disjoint open sets
$
\left\{\textbf{x}^{A}+\frac{\delta}{3}B_X\right\}_{A\subset\mathbb{N}},
$
which contradicts the separability of $X$.

Conversely, suppose that
$
X=\overline{\mathbb{C}^{(\mathbb{N})}}^{\,X}.
$
Under the topology of $X$, since the countable set $\left( \mathbb{Q} +\mathrm{i}\mathbb{Q} \right)^{(\mathbb{N})}$
is dense in $\mathbb{C}^{(\mathbb{N})}$, it is also dense in $X$.
Therefore, $X$ is separable.
<!-- end proof-2 -->
<!-- proposition-1: body_md -->
\label{mingti-jishubiaoshi}
    Let $f\in H(O)$ and let
    $\textbf{z}_0\in O\cap\overline{\mathbb{C}^{(\mathbb{N})}}^{\,X}$ satisfy
    \begin{equation}
        \sum\limits_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_0^{(\mathbb{N})}}
        \left|c_{\alpha}(f)\textbf{z}_0^{\alpha}\right|<\infty.
        \label{shi-juduishoulianjiading}
    \end{equation}
    Then, for every $\textbf{z}\in\mathbb{C}^{\mathbb{N}}$ satisfying
    $|\textbf{z}|\leqslant|\textbf{z}_0|$, one has
    \begin{equation}
        \sum\limits_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_0^{(\mathbb{N})}}
        \left|c_{\alpha}(f)\textbf{z}^{\alpha}\right|<\infty,
        \label{shi-juduishoulian}
    \end{equation}
    and
    \begin{equation}
        f(\textbf{z})
        =
        \sum\limits_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_0^{(\mathbb{N})}}
        c_{\alpha}(f)\textbf{z}^{\alpha}.
        \label{shi-hanshubiaoshi}
    \end{equation}
<!-- end proposition-1 -->
<!-- proof-3: body_md -->
Fix $\textbf{z}\in\mathbb{C}^{\mathbb{N}}$ with
$|\textbf{z}|\leqslant|\textbf{z}_0|$. By Lemma~\ref{yinli-xuliekongjian}~\hyperref[xiang-bibaokongjian]{\textup{(\ref*{xiang-bibaokongjian})}},
$\textbf{z}\in\overline{\mathbb{C}^{(\mathbb{N})}}^{\,X}$.
The defining property of $O$ gives $\textbf{z}\in O$.
Since $|\textbf{z}^{\alpha}|\leqslant|\textbf{z}_0^{\alpha}|$ for
every multi-index $\alpha$, \eqref{shi-juduishoulianjiading} implies \eqref{shi-juduishoulian}.

For each $n\in\mathbb{N}$, the defining property of $O$ gives
$|P_n\textbf{z}|\in O$. Since $O$ is open, there exists
$\varepsilon>0$ such that
$
\sum\limits_{j=1}^n (|z_j|+\varepsilon)e_j\in O.
$
By the Reinhardt property of $O$, the polydisc
$$
\left\{
\sum\limits_{j=1}^n \zeta_j e_j:
|\zeta_j|<|z_j|+\varepsilon,\quad 1\leqslant j\leqslant n
\right\}
$$
is contained in $O$. Hence the Taylor series at the origin of the
restriction of $f$ to $\mathrm{span} \left\{ e_{1},e_{2},\cdots ,e_{n} \right\}$ converges absolutely at $P_n\textbf{z}$,
and therefore
\begin{equation}
    f(P_n\textbf{z})=
    \sum\limits_{\substack{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_0^{(\mathbb{N})}\\
            \mathrm{supp}\alpha\subset\{1,\cdots,n\}}}
    c_{\alpha}(f)\textbf{z}^{\alpha}.
    \label{shi-touyingzhankai}
\end{equation}
Here the finite-dimensional coefficients agree with $c_\alpha(f)$
by their definition; see also
\cite[Corollary~7.8, pp.~49--50]{Mu86}.
By \eqref{shi-weibushoulian} in Lemma~\ref{yinli-xuliekongjian}  and the continuity of $f$,
$f(P_n\textbf{z})\to f(\textbf{z})$.
On the other hand, absolute convergence in \eqref{shi-juduishoulian} gives
$$
\begin{aligned}
&\left|
\sum\limits_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_0^{(\mathbb{N})}}
 c_\alpha(f)\textbf{z}^{\alpha}
-\sum\limits_{\substack{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_0^{(\mathbb{N})}\\
\mathrm{supp}\alpha\subset\{1,\cdots,n\}}}
 c_\alpha(f)\textbf{z}^{\alpha}\right|\leqslant
\sum\limits_{\substack{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_0^{(\mathbb{N})}\\
\mathrm{supp}\alpha\not\subset\{1,\cdots,n\}}}
 |c_\alpha(f)\textbf{z}^{\alpha}|\longrightarrow0.
\end{aligned}
$$
Passing to the limit in \eqref{shi-touyingzhankai} proves
\eqref{shi-hanshubiaoshi}.
<!-- end proof-3 -->
<!-- lemma-3: body_md -->
\label{yinli-bituguji}
Let $O=X$ or $O=B_X$, and let
$\textbf{z}\in\mathrm{dom}H_b(O)$. There exist $A>0$ and $r>0$, with $r<1$ if $O=B_X$, such that for every
$f\in H_b(O)$,
\begin{equation}
	\sum\limits_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_0^{(\mathbb{N})}}
	|c_\alpha(f)\textbf{z}^\alpha|
	\leqslant A\|f\|_r .
	\label{shi-bituguji}
\end{equation}
In particular, if $O=X$, there is $K\geqslant1$ such that for every
$m\in\mathbb{N}, P\in\mathcal{P}^m(X)$,
\begin{equation}
\sum\limits_{|\alpha|=m}|c_\alpha(P)\textbf{z}^\alpha|
\leqslant K^m\|P\|_{\mathcal{P}^m(X)}.
\label{shi-dianchuzhishijie}
\end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-3 -->
<!-- proof-4: body_md -->
First, every coefficient functional $f\mapsto c_\alpha(f)$ is
continuous on $H_b(O)$. To see this, fix $s>0$, with $s<1$ when
$O=B_X$. Choose $n\geqslant1$ such that
$\mathrm{supp}\alpha\subset\{1,\cdots,n\}$.
Since, for every $\left| w_{j} \right| \leqslant \frac{s}{2n\| e_{j}\|_{X}} ,j=1,2,\cdots ,n$, we have:
$$
\left\|\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}w_je_j\right\|_X
\leqslant\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}\frac{s}{2n\| e_{j}\|_{X}}\|e_j\|_X=\frac{s}{2}.
$$
The Cauchy integral formula gives
$$
\begin{aligned}\left| c_{\alpha}(f) \right|&=\left| \frac{1}{(2\pi \mathrm{i})^{n}} \int\limits_{|w_{1}|=\frac{s}{2n\| e_{1}\|_{X}}} \cdots \int\limits_{|w_{n}|=\frac{s}{2n\| e_{n}\|_{X}}} \frac{f\left( \sum\limits_{j=1}^{n} w_{j}e_{j} \right)}{\prod\limits_{j=1}^{n} w_{j}^{\alpha_{j} +1}} \, \mathrm{d} w_{n}\cdots \mathrm{d} w_{1} \right|\\ &\leqslant \frac{1}{(2\pi )^{n}} \int\limits_{|w_{1}|=\frac{s}{2n\| e_{1}\|_{X}}} \cdots \int\limits_{|w_{n}|=\frac{s}{2n\| e_{n}\|_{X}}} \frac{\left| f\left( \sum\limits_{j=1}^{n} w_{j}e_{j} \right) \right|}{\prod\limits_{j=1}^{n} \left| w_{j} \right|^{\alpha_{j} +1}} \, \left| \mathrm{d} w_{n} \right| \cdots \left| \mathrm{d} w_{1} \right|\\ &\leqslant \frac{\|f\|_{s}}{\left( 2\pi \right)^{n}} \prod\limits_{j=1}^{n} \frac{2\pi \left( \frac{s}{2n\| e_{j}\|_{X}} \right)}{\left( \frac{s}{2n\| e_{j}\|_{X}} \right)^{\alpha_{j} +1}} =\|f\|_{s} \prod\limits_{j=1}^{n} \frac{1}{\left( \frac{s}{2n\| e_{j}\|_{X}} \right)^{\alpha_{j}}}.\end{aligned}
$$
Thus $f\mapsto c_\alpha(f)$ is continuous with respect to the
seminorm $\|\cdot\|_s$, and therefore continuous on $H_b(O)$.

The set $\mathbb{N}_0^{(\mathbb{N})}$ is countable. Let
$\ell^1(\mathbb{N}_0^{(\mathbb{N})})$ be the Banach space of
absolutely summable complex families indexed by this set, with norm
$\|(a_\alpha)_\alpha\|_1=\sum\limits_{\alpha\in \mathbb{N}_{0}^{(\mathbb{N})}}|a_\alpha|$.
 $\textbf{z}\in\mathrm{dom}H_b(O)$ makes
$$
T_{\textbf{z}}:H_b(O)\longrightarrow
\ell^1(\mathbb{N}_0^{(\mathbb{N})}),
\qquad
T_{\textbf{z}}f=(c_\alpha(f)\textbf{z}^\alpha)_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{(\mathbb{N})}},
$$
a well-defined linear map. Its graph is closed. Indeed, if
$f_j\to f$ in $H_b(O)$ and $T_{\textbf{z}}f_j\to(a_\alpha)_\alpha$
in $\ell^1(\mathbb{N}_{0}^{(\mathbb{N})})$. For every $\alpha \in \mathbb{N}_{0}^{(\mathbb{N})}$, we have
$$c_{\alpha}\left( f \right) \textbf{z}^{\alpha} =\lim\limits_{j\to \infty} c_{\alpha}\left( f_{j} \right) \textbf{z}^{\alpha} =\lim\limits_{j\to \infty} \left( T_{\textbf{z}}f_{j} \right)_{\alpha} =a_{\alpha}.$$

Both spaces are Fr\'echet. Hence the closed graph theorem \cite[52.10 and 52.12, p.~577]{KM97} implies that $T_{\textbf{z}}$ is continuous.

The seminorms in
\eqref{shi-banfanshu} form an increasing sequence. Hence, there exist
$A>0$ and $r>0$, with $r<1$ if $O=B_X$, such that
\eqref{shi-bituguji} holds.


Assume now that $O=X$ and let
$P\in\mathcal{P}^m(X)\subset H_{b}\left( X \right)$, where $m\in\mathbb{N}$. Since $P$ is
$m$-homogeneous, $c_\alpha(P)=0$ whenever $|\alpha|\neq m$, and
homogeneity gives
$\|P\|_r=r^m\|P\|_{\mathcal{P}^m(X)}$. Hence
$$
\sum\limits_{|\alpha|=m}|c_\alpha(P)\textbf{z}^{\alpha}|
\leqslant
Ar^m\|P\|_{\mathcal{P}^m(X)}.
$$
Set
$K\triangleq\max\{1,A\}\max\{1,r\}$. Since $m\geqslant1$,
$$
Ar^m
\leqslant
\max\{1,A\}^m\max\{1,r\}^m
=
K^m.
$$
Therefore
$$
\sum\limits_{|\alpha|=m}|c_\alpha(P)\textbf{z}^{\alpha}|
\leqslant
K^m\|P\|_{\mathcal{P}^m(X)},
$$
which proves
\eqref{shi-dianchuzhishijie}.
<!-- end proof-4 -->
<!-- theorem-1: body_md -->
\label{dingli-ercizhangai}
    If $\mathbb{C}^{(\mathbb{N})}$ is not dense in $X$, then  for each $\textbf{x} \in X\setminus \overline{\mathbb{C}^{(\mathbb{N})}}^{\,X}$, there exists
    $G\in\mathcal{P}^{2}(X)$ such that
    \begin{equation}
        \sum\limits_{\substack{
                \alpha\in\mathbb{N}_0^{(\mathbb{N})}\\
                |\alpha|=2
        }}
        \left|c_{\alpha}(G)\textbf{x}^{\alpha}\right|
        =+\infty.
        \label{shi-ercifasan}
    \end{equation}
<!-- end theorem-1 -->
<!-- proof-5: body_md -->
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{Step 1}\par\nopagebreak[4]

By Lemma~\ref{yinli-fenkuaixiajie}, choose $\delta>0$
and integers $0=n_0<n_1<n_2<\cdots$ such that
$$
\|(P_{n_k}-P_{n_{k-1}})\textbf{x}\|_X>\delta,
\qquad k\in\mathbb{N}.
$$

    For $k\in\mathbb{N}$, put
    $$
    E_k
    \triangleq
    \{n_{k-1}+1,\cdots,n_k\},
    \qquad
    P_{E_{k}}\textbf{u}
    \triangleq
    \sum\limits_{i\in E_k}u_i e_i.
    $$
    Then the sets $E_k$ are pairwise disjoint and
$
    \bigcup\limits_{k=1}^{\infty}E_k=\mathbb{N}.
$
    Moreover,
$
    \|P_{E_{k}}\textbf{u}\|_X
    \leqslant
    \|\textbf{u}\|_X,   \textbf{u}\in X,
$
    and
$
    \|P_{E_{k}}\textbf{x}\|_X>\delta.
    $

    By the complex Hahn--Banach theorem, for each $k\in\mathbb{N}$ there
    exists $\psi_k\in X^*$ such that
    $$
    \|\psi_k\|_{X^*}=1,
    \qquad
    \psi_k(P_{E_{k}}\textbf{x})
    =
    \|P_{E_{k}}\textbf{x}\|_X.
    $$

    Then, for every $\textbf{u}\in X$,
\begin{equation}
    \psi_{k} (P_{E_{k}}\textbf{u} )=\sum\limits_{i\in E_{k}} \psi_{k} \left( e_{i} \right) u_{i}.\label{shi-fenkuaifanhan}
\end{equation}
    In particular,
    \begin{equation}\label{shi-fenkuaixiajie}
    \sum\limits_{i\in E_{k}} |\psi_{k} \left( e_{i} \right) x_{i}|\geqslant \left| \sum\limits_{i\in E_{k}} \psi_{k} \left( e_{i} \right) x_{i} \right| =|\psi_{k} (P_{E_k}\textbf{x} )|>
        \delta,
        \qquad
        k\in\mathbb{N}.
    \end{equation}

    \par\medskip\noindent\textbf{Step 2}\par\nopagebreak[4]

    For $j\in\mathbb{N}$, set
    $$
h_{r,s}^{(j)}\triangleq e^{\frac{2\pi \mathrm{i} (r-1)(s-1)}{4^{j}}},\qquad 1\leqslant r,s\leqslant 4^{j}.
    $$
    Then
    $
    |h_{r,s}^{(j)}|=1
$
    and
    $$
    \sum\limits_{r=1}^{4^{j}} \overline{h_{r,s}^{(j)}} h_{r,t}^{(j)}=\begin{cases}4^{j},&s=t,\\ 0,&s\neq t.\end{cases}
    $$
    Consequently, for every
    $\xi_1,\cdots,\xi_{4^{j}}\in\mathbb{C}$,
    \begin{equation}\label{shi-zhengjiaohengdeng}
    \begin{aligned}\sum\limits_{r=1}^{4^{j}} \left| \sum\limits_{s=1}^{4^{j}} h_{r,s}^{(j)}\xi_{s} \right|^{2}&=\sum\limits_{r=1}^{4^{j}} \left( \sum\limits_{s=1}^{4^{j}} h_{r,s}^{(j)}\xi_{s} \sum\limits_{s^{\prime}=1}^{4^{j}} \overline{h_{r,s^{\prime}}^{(j)}} \cdot \overline{\xi_{s^{\prime}}} \right)\\ &=\sum\limits_{s=1}^{4^{j}} \sum\limits_{s^{\prime}=1}^{4^{j}} \sum\limits_{r=1}^{4^{j}} \left( h_{r,s}^{(j)}\overline{h_{r,s^{\prime}}^{(j)}} \xi_{s} \overline{\xi_{s^{\prime}}} \right)\\ &=\sum\limits_{s=1}^{4^{j}} \left( 4^{j}\xi_{s} \overline{\xi_{s}} \right) =4^{j}\sum\limits_{s=1}^{4^{j}} |\xi_{s} |^{2}.\end{aligned}
    \end{equation}

    Put
    $$
    d_0\triangleq0,
    \qquad
    d_j\triangleq d_{j-1}+2\cdot4^{j},
    \qquad
    j\in\mathbb{N}.
    $$
    For every $j\in\mathbb{N}$, define a bilinear form
    $A_j:X\times X\to\mathbb{C}$ by
    $$
A_{j}(\textbf{u} ,\textbf{v} )\triangleq \frac{1}{8^{j}} \sum\limits_{r,s=1}^{4^{j}} h_{r,s}^{(j)}\psi_{d_{j-1}+r} \left( P_{E_{d_{j-1}+r}}\textbf{u} \right) \psi_{d_{j-1}+4^{j}+s} \left( P_{E_{d_{j-1}+4^{j}+s}}\textbf{v} \right) .
    $$
    By the Cauchy--Schwarz inequality and
    \eqref{shi-zhengjiaohengdeng},
    $$
\begin{aligned}|A_{j}(\textbf{u} ,\textbf{v} )|&\leqslant \frac{1}{8^{j}} \left( \sum\limits_{r=1}^{4^{j}} |\psi_{d_{j-1}+r} \left( P_{E_{d_{j-1}+r}}\textbf{u} \right) |^{2} \right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\\ &\qquad \cdot \left( \sum\limits_{r=1}^{4^{j}} \left| \sum\limits_{s=1}^{4^{j}} h_{r,s}^{(j)}\psi_{d_{j-1}+4^{j}+s} \left( P_{E_{d_{j-1}+4^{j}+s}}\textbf{v} \right) \right|^{2} \right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\\ &=\frac{1}{4^{j}} \left( \sum\limits_{r=1}^{4^{j}} |\psi_{d_{j-1}+r} \left( P_{E_{d_{j-1}+r}}\textbf{u} \right) |^{2} \right)^{\frac{1}{2}} \left( \sum\limits_{s=1}^{4^{j}} |\psi_{d_{j-1}+4^{j}+s} \left( P_{E_{d_{j-1}+4^{j}+s}}\textbf{v} \right) |^{2} \right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\\ &\leqslant \| \textbf{u} \|_{X} \| \textbf{v} \|_{X} .\end{aligned}
    $$

    Hence the series
    $$
    A(\textbf{u},\textbf{v})
    \triangleq
    \sum\limits_{j=1}^{\infty}2^{-j}A_j(\textbf{u},\textbf{v})
    $$
    converges absolutely for every
    $(\textbf{u},\textbf{v})\in X\times X$, and
    $$
    |A(\textbf{u},\textbf{v})|
    \leqslant
    \|\textbf{u}\|_X\|\textbf{v}\|_X.
    $$
    Thus $A$ is a continuous bilinear form on $X\times X$.

    Define
$
    G(\textbf{u})
    \triangleq
    A(\textbf{u},\textbf{u}),   \textbf{u}\in X.
$
    Then
$
    G\in\mathcal{P}^{2}(X)
$
    and
    $$
    |G(\textbf{u})|
    \leqslant
    \|\textbf{u}\|_X^2,
    \qquad
    \textbf{u}\in X.
    $$

    \par\medskip\noindent\textbf{Step 3}\par\nopagebreak[4]

On every finite-dimensional coordinate subspace, only finitely many
summands in the definition of $G$ are nonzero. By \eqref{shi-fenkuaifanhan},

    $$
\begin{aligned}G\left( \textbf{u} \right)&=\sum\limits_{j=1}^{\infty} 2^{-j}A_{j}(\textbf{u} ,\textbf{u} )=\sum\limits_{j=1}^{\infty} \frac{1}{16^{j}} \sum\limits_{r,s=1}^{4^{j}} h_{r,s}^{(j)}\psi_{d_{j-1}+r} \left( P_{E_{d_{j-1}+r}}\textbf{u} \right) \psi_{d_{j-1}+4^{j}+s} \left( P_{E_{d_{j-1}+4^{j}+s}}\textbf{u} \right)\\ &=\sum\limits_{j=1}^{\infty} \frac{1}{16^{j}} \sum\limits_{r,s=1}^{4^{j}} h_{r,s}^{(j)}\sum\limits_{\begin{gathered}p\in E_{d_{j-1}+r}\\ q\in E_{d_{j-1}+4^{j}+s}\end{gathered}} \psi_{d_{j-1}+r} \left( e_{p} \right) \psi_{d_{j-1}+4^{j}+s} \left( e_{q} \right) u_{p}u_{q}.\end{aligned}
    $$

    Let $j\in\mathbb{N}$ and $1\leqslant r,s\leqslant 4^{j}$, and suppose that
    $$
    p\in E_{d_{j-1}+r},
    \qquad
    q\in E_{d_{j-1}+4^{j}+s}.
    $$
    Then $p<q$. Moreover, for every fixed pair $p<q,p,q\in \mathbb{N}$, there is at most one
    triple $(j,r,s)$ satisfying these conditions.  Indeed, let $k_p,k_q\in\mathbb{N}$ be uniquely determined by
    $p\in E_{k_p}$ and $q\in E_{k_q}$. If
    $$
    p\in E_{d_{j-1}+r},
    \qquad
    q\in E_{d_{j-1}+4^j+s},
    $$
    then
    $$
    k_p=d_{j-1}+r,
    \qquad
    k_q=d_{j-1}+4^j+s.
    $$
    Since the intervals
$
    \{d_{j-1}+1,\cdots,d_j\},j\in\mathbb{N},
  $
    are pairwise disjoint, the first equality determines $j$ and $r$ uniquely, and the
    second then determines $s$ uniquely. Hence the monomial
    $u_pu_q$ occurs in exactly one of the corresponding summands, and
    \begin{equation}\label{shi-fasanduoxiangshixishu}
    c_{e_{p}+e_{q}}(G)=\frac{1}{16^{j}} h_{r,s}^{(j)}\psi_{d_{j-1}+r} \left( e_{p} \right) \psi_{d_{j-1}+4^{j}+s} \left( e_{q} \right) .
    \end{equation}
    All the remaining monomial coefficients of degree $2$ are zero.

    Using \eqref{shi-fasanduoxiangshixishu} and
    \eqref{shi-fenkuaixiajie}, we obtain
    $$
    \begin{aligned}[b]
\sum\limits_{\substack{
                \alpha\in\mathbb{N}_0^{(\mathbb{N})}\\
                |\alpha|=2
        }}
        |c_\alpha(G)\textbf{x}^{\alpha}|
        &=\sum\limits_{j=1}^{\infty} \frac{1}{16^{j}} \sum\limits_{r,s=1}^{4^{j}} \left[ \sum\limits_{p\in E_{d_{j-1}+r}} \left| \psi_{d_{j-1}+r} \left( e_{p} \right) x_{p} \right| \cdot \sum\limits_{q\in E_{d_{j-1}+4^{j}+s}} \left| \psi_{d_{j-1}+4^{j}+s} \left( e_{q} \right) x_{q} \right| \right]
        \\&\geqslant
    \sum\limits_{j=1}^{\infty} \frac{1}{16^{j}} \sum\limits_{r,s=1}^{4^{j}} \delta^{2} =\sum\limits_{j=1}^{\infty} \delta^{2} =+\infty.
    \end{aligned}
    $$
<!-- end proof-5 -->
<!-- remark-1: body_md -->
\label{zhuji-bibaobaohan}
For every Reinhardt domain $O\subset X$, Theorem~\ref{dingli-ercizhangai} gives for each $\mathcal{P}^{2} \left( X \right) \subset \mathcal{F} \left( O \right) \subset H\left( O \right)$,
\begin{equation}
\mathrm{dom} \mathcal{F}(O)
\subset\overline{\mathrm{span}\{e_j:j\geqslant1\}}^{\,X}.
\label{shi-bibaobaohan}
\end{equation}
This gives an affirmative answer
to Problem~\ref{wenti-bibaobaohan}.
<!-- end remark-1 -->
<!-- lemma-4: body_md -->
\label{yinli-weibuguji}
	Suppose that $\overline{\mathbb{C}^{(\mathbb{N})}}^{\,X}=X$ and
	$\textbf{z}\in\mathrm{dom}H(X)$. For every $r>0$, there exist
	$N\in\mathbb{N}$ and $C>0$ such that for every $n\geqslant N, p\in\mathscr{P}_n$,
\begin{equation}
	\sum\limits_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_0^{(\mathbb{N})}}
	|c_\alpha(p)\textbf{z}^\alpha|
	\leqslant C\|p\|_r.
	\label{shi-weibutongyijie}
\end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-4 -->
<!-- proof-6: body_md -->
Fix $r>0$. For $p\in\mathscr{P}_0$, set
	$$
	S_{\textbf{z}}(p)\triangleq
	\sum\limits_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_0^{(\mathbb{N})}}
	|c_\alpha(p)\textbf{z}^{\alpha}|<\infty.
	$$

	Since $\mathscr{P}_{n+1}\subset\mathscr{P}_n$, it suffices to prove
	the estimate \eqref{shi-weibutongyijie} for one value of $n$.
	
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{Step 1}\par\nopagebreak[4]
	
	Suppose that no such estimate holds. Set $m_0=0$. For $j\in\mathbb{N}$, after $m_{j-1}$
	has been chosen, the failure of \eqref{shi-weibutongyijie} on
	$\mathscr{P}_{m_{j-1}}$ gives a nonzero 
	$q_j\in\mathscr{P}_{m_{j-1}}$ such that
	$$
	S_{\textbf{z}}(q_j)
	>
	(1+2^{j+1})\|q_j\|_r.
	$$
	Choose $m_j>m_{j-1}$ so that $q_j$ depends only on the coordinates
	$x_{m_{j-1}+1},\cdots ,x_{m_{j}}$, and define
	$$
	p_j\triangleq
	\frac{q_j-q_j(\textbf{0})}{2^{j+1}\|q_j\|_r}.
	$$
	Since $|q_j(\textbf{0})|\leqslant\|q_j\|_r$, we have
	\begin{equation}
		p_j(\textbf{0})=0,
		\qquad
		\|p_j\|_r\leqslant2^{-j},
		\qquad
		S_{\textbf{z}}(p_{j})=\frac{S_{\textbf{z}}(q_{j})-\left| q_{j}\left( \textbf{0} \right) \right|}{2^{j+1}\|q_j\|_r} >1.
		\label{shi-fasanxiangguifan}
	\end{equation}
	
	Consider the series
	\begin{equation}
		F(\textbf{x})\triangleq\sum\limits_{j=1}^{\infty}p_j(\textbf{x}).
		\label{shi-zhenghanshupinjie}
	\end{equation}
	Fix $\textbf{a}\in X$. By \eqref{shi-weibushoulian}, choose
	$N_0\in\mathbb{N}$ with $\|Q_{N_0}\textbf{a}\|_X<\frac{r}{3}$, and then
	choose $j_0$ so that $m_{j-1}\geqslant N_0$ for every $j\geqslant j_0$.
	If
	$\textbf{x}\in\textbf{a}+\frac{r}{3} B_X$ and $j\geqslant j_0$, then
	$$
	\begin{aligned}
		\|(P_{m_j}-P_{m_{j-1}})\textbf{x}\|_X
		&\leqslant\|Q_{N_0}\textbf{x}\|_X\leqslant\|Q_{N_0}\textbf{a}\|_X+\|\textbf{x}-\textbf{a}\|_X
		<\frac{2r}{3}.
	\end{aligned}
	$$
	The first inequality follows from $\left| ( P_{m_{j}}-P_{m_{j-1}}) \textbf{x} \right| \leqslant \left| Q_{N_{0}}\textbf{x} \right|$ and \eqref{shi-fanshudandiao}. Since $p_j$ depends only on the coordinates $x_{m_{j-1}+1},\cdots ,x_{m_{j}}$, \eqref{shi-fasanxiangguifan} gives
	$$
		|p_j(\textbf{x})|
		=
		\bigl|p_j((P_{m_j}-P_{m_{j-1}})\textbf{x})\bigr|
		\leqslant2^{-j},
		\quad
		\textbf{x}\in\textbf{a}+\frac{r}{3} B_X,\quad j\geqslant j_0.
	$$
	Thus \eqref{shi-zhenghanshupinjie} converges locally uniformly
	on $X$. Since every $p_j$ is a continuous polynomial,
	\eqref{shi-quanchunjixian} gives $F\in H(X)$.
	
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{Step 2}\par\nopagebreak[4]

We have
	$$
\begin{aligned}\sum\limits_{\alpha \in \mathbb{N}_{0}^{(\mathbb{N} )}} |c_{\alpha}(F)\textbf{z}^{\alpha} |&=\left| \sum\limits_{j=1}^{\infty} p_{j}\left( \textbf{0} \right) \right| +\sum\limits_{j=1}^{\infty} \left( S_{\textbf{z}}(p_{j})-\left| p_{j}\left( \textbf{0} \right) \right| \right)\\ &=\sum\limits_{j=1}^{\infty} S_{\textbf{z}}(p_{j})=+\infty .\end{aligned}
	$$
	This contradicts $\textbf{z}\in\mathrm{dom}H(X)$.
	Thus \eqref{shi-weibutongyijie} holds.
<!-- end proof-6 -->
<!-- proposition-2: body_md -->
\label{mingti-jubuhua}
Suppose that $\overline{\mathbb{C}^{(\mathbb{N})}}^{\,X}=X$.
For every Reinhardt domain $O\subset X$,
\begin{equation}
\mathrm{dom}H(O)=O\cap\mathrm{dom}H(X).
\label{shi-jubuhua}
\end{equation}
More precisely, for each $\textbf{z}\in O$ the following conditions
are equivalent:
\begin{enumerate}
\item\label{xiang-yuneishoulian} $\textbf{z}\in\mathrm{dom}H(O)$.
\item\label{xiang-zhenghanshushoulian} $\textbf{z}\in\mathrm{dom}H(X)$.
\item\label{xiang-weibupanju} For every $r>0$, there exist $N\in\mathbb{N}$ and $C>0$ such that
\begin{equation}
\sum\limits_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_0^{(\mathbb{N})}}
|c_\alpha(p)\textbf{z}^\alpha|
\leqslant C\|p\|_r,
\qquad p\in\mathscr{P}_N.
\label{shi-weibupanju}
\end{equation}
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end proposition-2 -->
<!-- lemma-5: body_md -->
\label{yinli-feijiebijin}
Let $\textbf{z}\in X$, $n\in\mathbb{N}_0$, and $r,C>0$.
Suppose that
\begin{equation}
	\sum\limits_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_0^{(\mathbb{N})}}
	|c_\alpha(p)\textbf{z}^{\alpha}|
	\leqslant C\|p\|_r,
	\qquad p\in\mathscr{P}_n.\label{shi-weibubijinjiading}
\end{equation}
Then
\begin{equation}
\sum\limits_{\beta\in\Lambda_n}
|c_\beta(g)(Q_n\textbf{z})^\beta|
\leqslant C\|g\|_\infty,
\qquad g\in H^\infty(rB_{Y_n}).
\label{shi-weibuquanchunguji}
\end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-5 -->
<!-- proof-7: body_md -->
For $m>n$ and $d\in\mathbb{N}_0$, put
$$
p_{m,d}(\textbf{x})
\triangleq
\sum\limits_{\substack{\beta\in\Lambda_n\\
\mathrm{supp}\beta\subset\{n+1,\cdots,m\}\\
|\beta|\leqslant d}}
\left(1-\frac{|\beta|}{d+1}\right)
c_\beta(g)\textbf{x}^\beta\in\mathscr{P}_n.
$$
Define
$$
K_d(\theta)
\triangleq
\frac{1}{d+1}\left|\sum\limits_{k=0}^{d}e^{\mathrm{i}k\theta}\right|^2,
\qquad \theta\in[0,2\pi].
$$
We have
\begin{equation}
    K_d(\theta)
    =
    \sum\limits_{\ell=-d}^{d}
    \left(1-\frac{|\ell|}{d+1}\right)e^{\mathrm{i}\ell\theta},
    \qquad
    K_d\geqslant0,
    \qquad
    \frac{1}{2\pi}\int\limits_0^{2\pi}K_d(\theta)\,\mathrm{d}\theta=1,\label{shi-feijiehe}
\end{equation}
and for each $k\in\mathbb{N}_0$, 
\begin{equation}\label{shi-feijiexishu}
    \begin{aligned}
        \frac{1}{2\pi}\int\limits_0^{2\pi}
        K_d(\theta)e^{\mathrm{i}k\theta}\,\mathrm{d}\theta
        &=\sum\limits_{\ell=-d}^{d}\left(1-\frac{|\ell|}{d+1}\right)
        \frac{1}{2\pi}\int\limits_0^{2\pi}
        e^{\mathrm{i}(\ell+k)\theta}\,\mathrm{d}\theta=
        \begin{cases}
            1-\frac{k}{d+1},&0\leqslant k\leqslant d,\\[2pt]
            0,&k>d.
        \end{cases}
    \end{aligned}
\end{equation}

Fix $g\in H^\infty(rB_{Y_n})$. For $\textbf{x}\in rB_X$, by \eqref{shi-feijiexishu}, we have
\begin{equation}\label{shi-feijiejifen}
	\begin{aligned}
		p_{m,d}(\textbf{x})
		&=
		\sum\limits_{\substack{\beta\in\Lambda_n\\
				\mathrm{supp}\beta\subset\{n+1,\cdots,m\}\\
				|\beta|\leqslant d}}
		\left(1-\frac{|\beta|}{d+1}\right)
		c_\beta(g)\textbf{x}^\beta\\
		&=
		\frac{1}{2\pi}
		\sum\limits_{\substack{\beta\in\Lambda_n\\
				\mathrm{supp}\beta\subset\{n+1,\cdots,m\}}}
		c_\beta(g)\textbf{x}^\beta
		\int\limits_0^{2\pi}
		K_d(\theta)e^{\mathrm{i}|\beta|\theta}\,\mathrm{d}\theta\\
		&=
		\frac{1}{2\pi}\int\limits_0^{2\pi}
		K_d(\theta)
		\sum\limits_{\substack{\beta\in\Lambda_n\\
				\mathrm{supp}\beta\subset\{n+1,\cdots,m\}}}
		c_\beta(g)
		\bigl(e^{\mathrm{i}\theta}(P_m-P_n)\textbf{x}\bigr)^\beta
		\,\mathrm{d}\theta\\
		&=
		\frac{1}{2\pi}\int\limits_0^{2\pi}
		K_d(\theta)\,
		g\bigl(e^{\mathrm{i}\theta}(P_m-P_n)\textbf{x}\bigr)
		\,\mathrm{d}\theta.
	\end{aligned}
\end{equation}
By \eqref{shi-feijiehe} and \eqref{shi-feijiejifen},
for every $\textbf{x}\in rB_X$,
$$
\begin{aligned}
|p_{m,d}(\textbf{x})|
&\leqslant\frac{1}{2\pi}\int\limits_0^{2\pi}
 K_d(\theta)
 \left|g\bigl(e^{\mathrm{i}\theta}(P_m-P_n)\textbf{x}\bigr)\right|
 \,\mathrm{d}\theta\leqslant\frac{\|g\|_\infty}{2\pi}
\int\limits_0^{2\pi}K_d(\theta)\,\mathrm{d}\theta
=\|g\|_\infty,
\end{aligned}
$$
which gives $\|p_{m,d}\|_r\leqslant\|g\|_\infty$. Thus
\eqref{shi-weibubijinjiading} gives
$$
\sum\limits_{\substack{\beta\in\Lambda_n\\
\mathrm{supp}\beta\subset\{n+1,\cdots,m\}\\
|\beta|\leqslant d}}
\left(1-\frac{|\beta|}{d+1}\right)
|c_\beta(g)(Q_n\textbf{z})^\beta|\leqslant C\| p_{m,d}\|_{r}
\leqslant C\|g\|_\infty.
$$
Letting $m,d\to\infty$ gives 
\eqref{shi-weibuquanchunguji}.
<!-- end proof-7 -->
<!-- proof-8: body_md -->
Restriction of an entire function to $O$ does not change its
coefficients, so \hyperref[xiang-yuneishoulian]{\textup{(\ref*{xiang-yuneishoulian})}} implies \hyperref[xiang-zhenghanshushoulian]{\textup{(\ref*{xiang-zhenghanshushoulian})}}. At $\textbf{z}=\textbf{0}$ all
three conditions hold. Fix a nonzero $\textbf{z}\in O$.
By Lemma~\ref{yinli-weibuguji},
\hyperref[xiang-zhenghanshushoulian]{\textup{(\ref*{xiang-zhenghanshushoulian})}} implies \hyperref[xiang-weibupanju]{\textup{(\ref*{xiang-weibupanju})}}.
Now suppose that \hyperref[xiang-weibupanju]{\textup{(\ref*{xiang-weibupanju})}} holds.
The finite-coordinate decomposition used below is inspired by the proofs of
\cite[Proposition~3.4, pp.~21--22]{DMP09} and
\cite[Proposition~5.4, pp.~961--962]{GMMS21}.


\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{Step 1}\par\nopagebreak[4]

Fix $f\in H(O)$, and define
$$
K\triangleq\{\textbf{x}\in X:|\textbf{x}|\leqslant|\textbf{z}|\}.
$$
The set $K$ is closed by continuity of the coordinate functionals.
For each $m\in\mathbb{N}$, we have
$$
P_mK=
\left\{\sum\limits_{j=1}^{m}w_je_j:
|w_j|\leqslant|z_j|\ (1\leqslant j\leqslant m)\right\}.
$$
Thus $P_mK$ is compact. Let $\left\{ \textbf{x}_{n} \right\}_{n\in \mathbb{N}} \subset K$ be arbitrary. By a diagonal argument, there exist a subsequence $\left\{ \textbf{x}_{n_{k}} \right\}_{k\in \mathbb{N}}$ and a sequence $\left\{ \textbf{x}^{\left( m \right)} \right\}_{m=1}^{\infty} \subset K$ such that
$$
\lim\limits_{k\to \infty} P_{m}\textbf{x}_{n_{k}} =\textbf{x}^{\left( m \right)} ,
\qquad m=1,2,\cdots.
$$
For $m_1,m_2\in \mathbb{N}$, we have
$$
\begin{aligned}
	\|\textbf{x}^{\left( m_{1} \right)} -\textbf{x}^{\left( m_{2} \right)}\|_{X}
	&=\lim\limits_{k\to \infty} \|P_{m_{1}}\textbf{x}_{n_{k}} -P_{m_{2}}\textbf{x}_{n_{k}}\|_{X}\\
	&=\lim\limits_{k\to \infty} \|Q_{m_{1}}\textbf{x}_{n_{k}} -Q_{m_{2}}\textbf{x}_{n_{k}}\|_{X}\\
	&\leqslant \|Q_{m_{1}}\textbf{z}\|_X
	+\|Q_{m_{2}}\textbf{z}\|_X.
\end{aligned}
$$
Hence, by \eqref{shi-weibushoulian} in Lemma~\ref{yinli-xuliekongjian}, $\left\{ \textbf{x}^{\left( m \right)} \right\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}$ is a Cauchy sequence in $X$. Since $X$ is complete, there exists $\textbf{x}\in K$ such that $\textbf{x}^{\left( m \right)}\to \textbf{x}$. For $k,m\in\mathbb{N}$, we have
$$
\begin{aligned}
	\|\textbf{x}_{n_{k}} -\textbf{x}\|_{X}
	&\leqslant \|\textbf{x}^{\left( m \right)} -\textbf{x}\|_{X}
	+\|\textbf{x}^{\left( m \right)} -P_{m}\textbf{x}_{n_{k}}\|_{X}
	+\|Q_{m}\textbf{x}_{n_{k}}\|_{X}\\
	&\leqslant \|\textbf{x}^{\left( m \right)} -\textbf{x}\|_{X}
	+\|\textbf{x}^{\left( m \right)} -P_{m}\textbf{x}_{n_{k}}\|_{X}
	+\|Q_{m}\textbf{z}\|_{X}.
\end{aligned}
$$
Therefore,
$$
0\leqslant \limsup\limits_{k\to \infty}
\|\textbf{x}_{n_{k}} -\textbf{x}\|_{X}
\leqslant
\|\textbf{x}^{\left( m \right)} -\textbf{x}\|_{X}
+\|Q_{m}\textbf{z}\|_{X}.
$$
Letting $m\to\infty$ and applying \eqref{shi-weibushoulian} in Lemma~\ref{yinli-xuliekongjian}, we obtain
$\textbf{x}_{n_{k}}\to \textbf{x}$. Therefore, $K$ is compact.


Choose $t>1$ with $t\textbf{z}\in O$, using openness of $O$.
The Reinhardt property gives $tK\subset O$.
For each $\textbf{a}\in tK$, openness of $O$ and continuity of $f$
give $\delta_{\textbf{a}},M_{\textbf{a}}>0$ such that
$$
\textbf{a}+3\delta_{\textbf{a}}B_X\subset O,
\qquad
|f|\leqslant M_{\textbf{a}}
\quad\text{on }\textbf{a}+3\delta_{\textbf{a}}B_X.
$$
By compactness, choose $\textbf{a}_1,\cdots,\textbf{a}_s\in tK$
whose balls $\textbf{a}_i+\delta_{\textbf{a}_i}B_X$ cover $tK$.
Set
$$
r\triangleq\frac{1}{2}\min\limits_{1\leqslant i\leqslant s}
\delta_{\textbf{a}_i},
\qquad
M\triangleq\max\limits_{1\leqslant i\leqslant s}M_{\textbf{a}_i}.
$$
If $\textbf{w}=\textbf{y}+\textbf{h}$ with $\textbf{y}\in tK$ and
$\|\textbf{h}\|_X<2r$, choose $i$ with
$\|\textbf{y}-\textbf{a}_i\|_X<\delta_{\textbf{a}_i}$. Then
$$
\|\textbf{w}-\textbf{a}_i\|_X
<\delta_{\textbf{a}_i}+2r
\leqslant2\delta_{\textbf{a}_i}<3\delta_{\textbf{a}_i}.
$$
Consequently, for each $\textbf{w}\in tK+2rB_X$, 
\begin{equation}
tK+2rB_X\subset O,
\qquad |f(\textbf{w})|\leqslant M.
\label{shi-jinlinyuyoujie}
\end{equation}

\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{Step 2}\par\nopagebreak[4]

Choose $N,C$ as in \eqref{shi-weibupanju} for the value of
$r$ in \eqref{shi-jinlinyuyoujie}. Since
$\mathscr{P}_n\subset\mathscr{P}_N$ for $n\geqslant N$, the same
estimate holds on every such $\mathscr{P}_n$. 
Choose $n\geqslant N$ so that
$$
I\triangleq\{i\in\{1,\cdots,n\}:z_i\neq0\}\neq\emptyset.
$$
Put
$$
\Gamma_I\triangleq\{\gamma\in\mathbb{N}_0^{(\mathbb{N})}:
\mathrm{supp}\gamma\subset I\}.
$$

For $\gamma=\left( \gamma_{i} \right)_{i\in \mathbb{N}}\in\Gamma_I$ and $\textbf{y}\in rB_{Y_n}$, define
\begin{equation}
g_\gamma(\textbf{y})\triangleq
\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{|I|}}\int\limits_{[0,2\pi]^I}
f(\sum\limits_{i\in I}tz_i e^{\mathrm{i}\theta_i}e_i+\textbf{y})
e^{-\mathrm{i}\sum\limits_{i\in I}\gamma_i\theta_i}\,\prod\limits_{i\in I}\mathrm{d}\theta_i.
\label{shi-toubuxishuhanshu}
\end{equation}
\eqref{shi-jinlinyuyoujie} gives
\begin{equation}
g_\gamma\in H^\infty(rB_{Y_n}),
\qquad\|g_\gamma\|_\infty\leqslant M.
\label{shi-toubuxishuhanshujie}
\end{equation}
For $\gamma\in\Gamma_I$ and $\beta\in\Lambda_n$, we claim that
\begin{equation}
	c_\beta(g_\gamma)
	=
	t^{|\gamma|}\textbf{z}^{\gamma}c_{\gamma+\beta}(f).
	\label{shi-xishutiqu}
\end{equation}
Put
$$
J\triangleq\mathrm{supp}\beta,
\Gamma_J\triangleq
\{\nu\in\mathbb{N}_0^{(\mathbb{N})}:
\mathrm{supp}\nu\subset J\}.
$$
Then $I\cap J=\emptyset$.
Choose $\left\{ \delta_{j} \right\}_{j\in J} \subset \left( 0,+\infty \right)$ such that
$\sum\limits_{j\in J}\delta_j\|e_j\|_X<r$.
We have
$$
\left\{
\sum\limits_{i\in I}u_i e_i+\sum\limits_{j\in J}v_j e_j:
|u_i|\leqslant t|z_i|, i\in I, \
|v_j|\leqslant\delta_j,\ j\in J
\right\}
\subset tK+rB_X\subset O.
$$
For $\textbf{v}=\sum\limits_{j\in J}v_j e_j$ with
$|v_j|\leqslant\delta_j$, the  Taylor expansion gives
$$
f(\sum\limits_{i\in I}tz_i e^{\mathrm{i}\theta_i}e_i+\textbf{v})
=
\sum\limits_{\eta\in\Gamma_I}
\sum\limits_{\nu\in\Gamma_J}
c_{\eta+\nu}(f)
t^{|\eta|}\textbf{z}^{\eta}\textbf{v}^{\nu}
e^{\mathrm{i}\sum\limits_{i\in I}\eta_i\theta_i}.
$$
We have

$$
\begin{aligned}
	g_\gamma(\textbf{v})
	&=
	\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{|I|}}
	\int\limits_{[0,2\pi]^I}
	f(\sum\limits_{i\in I}tz_i e^{\mathrm{i}\theta_i}e_i+\textbf{v})
	e^{-\mathrm{i}\sum\limits_{i\in I}\gamma_i\theta_i}\,\prod\limits_{i\in I}\mathrm{d}\theta_i\\
	&=
	\sum\limits_{\eta\in\Gamma_I}
	\sum\limits_{\nu\in\Gamma_J}
	c_{\eta+\nu}(f)t^{|\eta|}\textbf{z}^{\eta}\textbf{v}^{\nu}
	\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{|I|}}
	\int\limits_{[0,2\pi]^I}
	e^{\mathrm{i}\sum\limits_{i\in I}(\eta_i-\gamma_i)\theta_i}\,\prod\limits_{i\in I}\mathrm{d}\theta_i
\\&=
	t^{|\gamma|}\textbf{z}^{\gamma}
	\sum\limits_{\nu\in\Gamma_J}
	c_{\gamma+\nu}(f)\textbf{v}^{\nu}.
\end{aligned}
$$

Comparing the coefficients of $\textbf{v}^{\beta}$ proves
\eqref{shi-xishutiqu}.

Applying the \eqref{shi-weibuquanchunguji} of Lemma~\ref{yinli-feijiebijin} to
$g_\gamma$ and using
\eqref{shi-toubuxishuhanshujie} and
\eqref{shi-xishutiqu}, we obtain
\begin{equation}\label{shi-hunhexishujie}
	\begin{aligned}
		t^{|\gamma|}
		\sum\limits_{\beta\in\Lambda_n}
		|c_{\gamma+\beta}(f)\textbf{z}^{\gamma+\beta}|
		&=
		\sum\limits_{\beta\in\Lambda_n}
		|c_\beta(g_\gamma)(Q_n\textbf{z})^\beta|\leqslant CM.
	\end{aligned}
\end{equation}

If $\alpha_i>0$ for some
$i\in\{1,\cdots,n\}\setminus I$, then $\textbf{z}^{\alpha}=0$.
Every other multi-index $\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_0^{(\mathbb{N})}$
has a unique decomposition $\alpha=\gamma+\beta$ with
$\gamma\in\Gamma_I$ and $\beta\in\Lambda_n$.
Consequently, by \eqref{shi-hunhexishujie}, we have
\begin{equation}
\begin{aligned}
	&\sum\limits_{\alpha \in \mathbb{N}_{0}^{(\mathbb{N} )}} |c_{\alpha}(f)\textbf{z}^{\alpha} |=\sum\limits_{\gamma \in \Gamma_{I}} \sum\limits_{\beta \in \Lambda_{n}} |c_{\gamma +\beta}(f)\textbf{z}^{\gamma +\beta} |\\ &\quad \leqslant CM\sum\limits_{\gamma \in \Gamma_{I}} t^{-\left| \gamma \right|}=CM\prod\limits_{i\in I} \left( \sum\limits_{k=0}^{\infty} t^{-k} \right) =\frac{CM}{\left( 1-\frac{1}{t} \right)^{\left| I \right|}} <\infty ,\end{aligned}
\label{shi-jubuqiuhe}
\end{equation}which gives $\textbf{z}\in\mathrm{dom}H(O)$.
Thus \hyperref[xiang-weibupanju]{\textup{(\ref*{xiang-weibupanju})}} implies \hyperref[xiang-yuneishoulian]{\textup{(\ref*{xiang-yuneishoulian})}}, proving all three equivalences and
\eqref{shi-jubuhua}.
<!-- end proof-8 -->
<!-- corollary-1: body_md -->
\label{tuilun-quanyushoulian}
If
\begin{equation}
\mathrm{dom}H(B_X)=B_X,
\label{shi-qiushoulianjiading}
\end{equation}
then, for every Reinhardt domain $O\subset X$,
\begin{equation}
\mathrm{dom}H(O)=O.
\label{shi-quanyushoulian}
\end{equation}
<!-- end corollary-1 -->
<!-- proof-9: body_md -->
By \eqref{shi-qiushoulianjiading} and
Remark~\ref{zhuji-bibaobaohan},
$B_X\subset\overline{\mathbb{C}^{(\mathbb{N})}}^{\,X}$,
it follows that
$\overline{\mathbb{C}^{(\mathbb{N})}}^{\,X}=X$.


Fix $\textbf{x}\in X$ and $F\in H(X)$, and choose
$R>\|\textbf{x}\|_X$. Define
$$
F_R(\textbf{y})\triangleq F(R\textbf{y}),
\qquad\textbf{y}\in B_X.
$$
Then $F_R\in H(B_X)$, and 
$
c_\alpha(F_R)=R^{|\alpha|}c_\alpha(F),
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_0^{(\mathbb{N})}.
$
Since $\frac{\textbf{x}}{R}\in B_X$, assumption \eqref{shi-qiushoulianjiading} implies
$$
\sum\limits_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_0^{(\mathbb{N})}}
|c_\alpha(F)\textbf{x}^\alpha|
=
\sum\limits_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_0^{(\mathbb{N})}}
\left|c_\alpha(F_R)\left(\frac{\textbf{x}}{R}\right)^\alpha\right|
<\infty.
$$
Thus $\textbf{x}\in\mathrm{dom}H(X)$, and hence
$\mathrm{dom}H(X)=X$.
Proposition~\ref{mingti-jubuhua} now yields \eqref{shi-quanyushoulian}.
<!-- end proof-9 -->
<!-- theorem-2: body_md -->
\label{dingli-shoulianpanju}
One has
\begin{equation}
\mathrm{dom}H(X)=X
\label{shi-zhenghanshuquanshoulian}
\end{equation}
if and only if there exists $C\geqslant1$ such that, for every
$m,n\in\mathbb{N}$ and every $P\in\mathcal{P}^m(X)$ satisfying
$P=P\circ P_n$,
\begin{equation}
\sup\limits_{\textbf{z}\in B_X}
\sum\limits_{\substack{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_0^{(\mathbb{N})}\\|\alpha|=m}}
|c_\alpha(P)\textbf{z}^\alpha|
\leqslant C^m\sup\limits_{\textbf{z}\in B_X}|P(\textbf{z})|.
\label{shi-tongyizhishiguji}
\end{equation}
Here $P_n$ is given on all of $X$ by the formula in \eqref{shi-shouweitouying},
and $C$ is independent of $m$, $n$, and $P$.
<!-- end theorem-2 -->
<!-- remark-2: body_md -->
The relation between
\eqref{shi-tongyizhishiguji}
and Lempert's problem is discussed in
\cite[discussion following Theorem~2.21, p.~5870]{DGMM}.
<!-- end remark-2 -->
<!-- lemma-6: body_md -->
\label{yinli-gujituiguang}
Suppose that there is $C\geqslant1$ such that \eqref{shi-tongyizhishiguji} holds for every $m,n\in\mathbb{N}$ and every $P\in\mathcal{P}^m(X)$ satisfying $P=P\circ P_n$. Then, for every $m\in\mathbb{N}$, $P\in\mathcal{P}^m(X)$, and $\textbf{z}\in X$,
\begin{equation}
\sum\limits_{|\alpha|=m}|c_\alpha(P)\textbf{z}^\alpha|
\leqslant C^m\|\textbf{z}\|_X^m
\sup\limits_{\textbf{w}\in B_X}|P(\textbf{w})|.
\label{shi-yibanduoxiangshijie}
\end{equation}
Moreover, $\overline{\mathbb{C}^{(\mathbb{N})}}^{\,X}=X$.
<!-- end lemma-6 -->
<!-- proof-10: body_md -->
Assume that \eqref{shi-tongyizhishiguji} holds.  If $P\in\mathcal{P}^m(X)$ depends only on the
first $n$ coordinates, apply  \eqref{shi-tongyizhishiguji} to $\frac{\textbf{z}}{s}$, where
$s>\|\textbf{z}\|_X$. Multiplying by $s^m$ and letting
$s\downarrow\|\textbf{z}\|_X$ gives
\begin{equation}
\sum\limits_{|\alpha|=m}|c_\alpha(P)\textbf{z}^\alpha|
\leqslant C^m\|\textbf{z}\|_X^m
\sup\limits_{\textbf{w}\in B_X}|P(\textbf{w})|,
\qquad\textbf{z}\in X.
\label{shi-qicisuofang}
\end{equation}

Let $P\in\mathcal{P}^m(X)$ now be arbitrary. By \eqref{shi-qicisuofang}, we obtain
$$
\begin{aligned}
	&\sum\limits_{|\alpha|=m}
	\left|c_\alpha(P\circ P_n)\textbf{z}^\alpha\right|
	=
	\sum\limits_{\substack{|\alpha|=m\\
			\mathrm{supp}\alpha\subset\{1,2,\cdots,n\}}}
	\left|c_\alpha(P)\textbf{z}^\alpha\right|\\
	& \quad\leqslant
	C^m\|\textbf{z}\|_X^m
	\sup\limits_{\textbf{w}\in B_X}
	\left|P(P_n\textbf{w})\right|
\leqslant
	C^m\|\textbf{z}\|_X^m
	\sup\limits_{\textbf{w}\in B_X}
	\left|P(\textbf{w})\right|.
\end{aligned}
$$
Letting $n\to\infty$ gives \eqref{shi-yibanduoxiangshijie}.
Taking $m=2$ in \eqref{shi-yibanduoxiangshijie} and applying
Theorem~\ref{dingli-ercizhangai} gives
$\overline{\mathbb{C}^{(\mathbb{N})}}^{\,X}=X$.
<!-- end proof-10 -->
<!-- proof-11: body_md -->
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{Step 1}\par\nopagebreak[4]
	
	Assume that \eqref{shi-zhenghanshuquanshoulian} holds. For $\textbf{z}\in X$ and
	$P\in\mathscr{P}_0$, put
$
	S_{\textbf{z}}(P)
	\triangleq
	\sum\limits_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_0^{(\mathbb{N})}}
	|c_\alpha(P)\textbf{z}^\alpha|.
$
	For $k\in\mathbb{N}$, define
	\begin{equation}
		E_k
		\triangleq
		\bigcap\limits_{m,n\in\mathbb{N}}
		\bigcap\limits_{\substack{
				P\in\mathcal{P}^m(X)\\
				P=P\circ P_n
		}}
		\left\{
		\textbf{z}\in X:
		S_{\textbf{z}}(P)
		\leqslant
		k^m\|P\|_{\mathcal{P}^m(X)}
		\right\}.
		\label{shi-beierbiji}
	\end{equation}

Recall \eqref{shi-zuobiaoguji}, the coordinate functionals are continuous. Since $P$ depends on only finitely many coordinates, it follows from \eqref{shi-youxianzhankai} that the map
$\textbf{z}\mapsto S_{\textbf{z}}(P)$
is continuous on $X$. Hence $E_k$ is closed.

	
	For $\textbf{z}\in X$ and
	$\textbf{w}\in\mathbb{C}^{\mathbb{N}}$, \eqref{shi-fanshudandiao} yields
	$$
	|\textbf{w}|\leqslant|\textbf{z}|
	\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
	\textbf{w}\in X,\qquad
	S_{\textbf{w}}(P)\leqslant S_{\textbf{z}}(P)
	\quad(P\in\mathscr{P}_0),
	$$which gives
	\begin{equation}
		\textbf{z}\in E_k,\qquad
		|\textbf{w}|\leqslant|\textbf{z}|
		\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
		\textbf{w}\in E_k.
		\label{shi-bijiguxing}
	\end{equation}
	
	Fix $\textbf{z}\in X$. By the \eqref{shi-dianchuzhishijie} of Lemma~\ref{yinli-bituguji}, there exists
	$A_{\textbf{z}}\geqslant1$ such that for every $m\in\mathbb{N},
	P\in\mathcal{P}^m(X)$,
	$$
	S_{\textbf{z}}(P)
	\leqslant
	A_{\textbf{z}}^m\|P\|_{\mathcal{P}^m(X)}.
	$$
Notice that $\textbf{z}\in E_{\lceil A_{\textbf{z}}\rceil}$, hence
$X=\bigcup\limits_{k=1}^{\infty}E_k$.
	Since $X$ is Banach,  Baire's theorem
	(see \cite[52.12, p.~577]{KM97}) yields
	$K\in\mathbb{N}$, $\textbf{a}=\left( a_{j} \right)_{j\in \mathbb{N}}\in X$, and $\rho>0$ such that
	\begin{equation}
		\textbf{a}+\rho B_X\subseteq E_K.
		\label{shi-beierneidian}
	\end{equation}
	
	Fix $\textbf{h}\in\rho B_X$ and define
	$\textbf{u}=(u_j)_{j\in\mathbb{N}}$ by
	$$
	u_j
	\triangleq
	\begin{cases}
		\frac{a_j}{|a_j|}|h_j|,&a_j\neq0,\\[4pt]
		|h_j|,&a_j=0.
	\end{cases}
	$$
	By \eqref{shi-fanshudandiao},
	$$
	|\textbf{u}|=|\textbf{h}|,\qquad
	\textbf{u}\in X,\qquad
	\|\textbf{u}\|_X=\|\textbf{h}\|_X<\rho,\qquad	|\textbf{a}+\textbf{u}|
	=
	|\textbf{a}|+|\textbf{h}|
	\geqslant|\textbf{h}|.
	$$
	Combining \eqref{shi-beierneidian} and \eqref{shi-bijiguxing},
	we obtain 
	$$
	\textbf{a}+\textbf{u}
	\in\textbf{a}+\rho B_X
	\subseteq E_K\Rightarrow \textbf{h} \in E_{K}\Rightarrow\rho B_X\subseteq E_K.
	$$

	
	Set $C\triangleq\max\left\{1,\frac{K}{\rho}\right\}$.
	For $m,n\in\mathbb{N}$ and $P\in\mathcal{P}^m(X)$
	satisfying $P=P\circ P_n$, one has
	$$
\sup\limits_{\textbf{z} \in B_{X}} S_{\textbf{z}}(P)=\frac{1}{\rho^{m}} \sup\limits_{\textbf{z} \in B_{X}} S_{\rho \textbf{z}}(P)\leqslant \left( \frac{K}{\rho} \right)^{m} \| P\|_{\mathcal{P}^{m} (X)} \leqslant C^{m}\| P\|_{\mathcal{P}^{m} (X)} ,
	$$
	which proves \eqref{shi-tongyizhishiguji}.
	
	\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{Step 2}\par\nopagebreak[4]
	
	Assume that \eqref{shi-tongyizhishiguji} holds, with $C\geqslant1$ fixed.
	By Lemma~\ref{yinli-gujituiguang}, \eqref{shi-yibanduoxiangshijie} holds
	and $\overline{\mathbb{C}^{(\mathbb{N})}}^{\,X}=X$. 	Fix $\textbf{z}\in X$ and $r>0$. By  equation \eqref{shi-weibushoulian} in Lemma~\ref{yinli-xuliekongjian},
	choose $N\in\mathbb{N}$ such that
$
	C\|Q_N\textbf{z}\|_X<\frac{r}{2}.
$
	Let $p\in\mathscr{P}_N$. By the definition of $\mathscr{P}_N$,
	there exist $L\in\mathbb{N}$ and $d\in\mathbb{N}_0$, with $L>N$,
	such that
	$$
	p(\textbf{w})
	=
	\sum\limits_{m=0}^{d}
	\sum\limits_{\substack{
			|\alpha|=m\\
			\mathrm{supp}\alpha\subseteq\{N+1,\cdots,L\}
	}}
	c_\alpha(p)\textbf{w}^{\alpha},
	\qquad
	\textbf{w}\in X.
	$$
	For $m=0,\cdots,d$, define
	$$
	H_m(\textbf{w})
	\triangleq
	\sum\limits_{\substack{
			|\alpha|=m\\
			\mathrm{supp}\alpha\subseteq\{N+1,\cdots,L\}
	}}
	c_\alpha(p)\textbf{w}^{\alpha},
	\qquad
	\textbf{w}\in X.
	$$
By \eqref{shi-qicikexiguji},
	$$
	\|H_m\|_{\mathcal{P}^m(X)}
	\leqslant
	\frac{\sup\limits_{\textbf{w}\in rB_X}|p(\textbf{w})|}{r^m}
	=
	\frac{\|p\|_r}{r^m},
	\qquad
	0\leqslant m\leqslant d.
	$$
	Combining \eqref{shi-yibanduoxiangshijie} with
\eqref{shi-qicixishuyizhi}, we obtain
	$$
	\begin{aligned}
		S_{\textbf{z}}(p)
		&=
		\sum\limits_{m=0}^{d}
		\sum\limits_{|\alpha|=m}
		|c_\alpha(H_m)\textbf{z}^{\alpha}|=
		\sum\limits_{m=0}^{d}
		\sum\limits_{|\alpha|=m}
		|c_\alpha(H_m)(Q_N\textbf{z})^{\alpha}|\\
		&=
		|p(\textbf{0})|
		+
		\sum\limits_{m=1}^{d}
		\sum\limits_{|\alpha|=m}
		|c_\alpha(H_m)(Q_N\textbf{z})^{\alpha}|\\
		&\leqslant
		\|p\|_r
		+
		\sum\limits_{m=1}^{d}
		C^m\|Q_N\textbf{z}\|_X^m
		\|H_m\|_{\mathcal{P}^m(X)}\\
		&\leqslant
		\|p\|_r
		\sum\limits_{m=0}^{d}
		\left(
		\frac{C\|Q_N\textbf{z}\|_X}{r}
		\right)^m\leqslant
		\|p\|_r
		\sum\limits_{m=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{2^m}
		=
		2\|p\|_r.
	\end{aligned}
	$$
Thus, for every $r>0$, there exists $N\in\mathbb{N}$ such that
\eqref{shi-weibupanju} holds with constant $C=2$ for all $p\in\mathscr{P}_N$.
Applying Proposition~\ref{mingti-jubuhua}, we obtain
$\textbf{z}\in\mathrm{dom}H(X)$ which gives \eqref{shi-zhenghanshuquanshoulian}.
<!-- end proof-11 -->
<!-- definition-1: body_md -->
\label{dingyi-fanlikongjian}
    Define
    \begin{equation}
        a_0\triangleq 1,
        \qquad
        a_r\triangleq 2^{a_{r-1}+1},
        \qquad r=1,2,\cdots.
        \label{shi-cengjicanshu}
    \end{equation}
    For $r=1,2,\cdots$, set
    \begin{equation}
        \kappa(r)
        \triangleq
        \max\left\{
        j\in\mathbb{N}_0:a_j\leqslant r
        \right\}.
        \label{shi-cengjisuoyin}
    \end{equation}

Given a nondecreasing function
$
    \Psi:\mathbb{N}\longrightarrow[1,+\infty)
  $
    satisfying
$
    \lim\limits_{n\to\infty}\Psi(n)=+\infty,
$
    define inductively
    \begin{equation}
        t_0\triangleq 1, t_r
        \triangleq
        \min\left\{
        \left(1+\frac{1}{r}\right)t_{r-1},
        \,2t_{\kappa(r)},
        \,\Psi(a_{r-1})
        \right\},
        \qquad r=1,2,\cdots.
        \label{shi-huanmancanshu}
    \end{equation}

    For each $r\in\mathbb{N}_0$, define
    \begin{equation}
        \mathcal{G}_r
        \triangleq
        \left\{
        \frac{t_r}{a_r}
        \sum\limits_{j\in F}\epsilon_j e_j:
        F\subset\mathbb{N},
        \ |F|=a_r,
        \ \epsilon_j\in\{-1,1\}
        \ \text{for every }j\in F
        \right\},
        \qquad
        \mathcal{G}
        \triangleq
        \bigcup\limits_{r\in\mathbb{N}_0}\mathcal{G}_r.
        \label{shi-yuanziji}
    \end{equation}
    For $\textbf{z}\in\mathbb{C}^{(\mathbb{N})}$, define
    $$
    \|\textbf{z}\|_Y
    \triangleq
    \inf
    \left\{
    \sum\limits_{v=1}^{s}|\lambda_v|:
    \begin{array}{l}
        s\in\mathbb{N},\;
        \lambda_v\in\mathbb{R},\;
        \textbf{u}_v\in\mathcal{G},\quad v=1,\cdots,s,\\[2mm]
        |\textbf{z}|=\displaystyle\sum\limits_{v=1}^{s}\lambda_v\textbf{u}_v
    \end{array}
    \right\}.
    $$
<!-- end definition-1 -->
<!-- lemma-7: body_md -->
\label{yinli-canshuxingzhi}
The sequence $(t_r)_{r\in \mathbb{N}_{0}}$ defined by \eqref{shi-huanmancanshu} satisfies for every $r\in\mathbb{N}$,
\begin{align}
1&\leqslant t_{r-1}\leqslant t_r\leqslant r+1,
\label{shi-huanmancanshujie}\\
\frac{t_r}{a_r}&\leqslant\frac{1}{2}\frac{t_{r-1}}{a_{r-1}}\leqslant\frac{1}{2},
\label{shi-yuanzigaodu}\\
\lim\limits_{r\to +\infty} t_{r}&=+\infty,\label{shi-jixian}
\end{align}
<!-- end lemma-7 -->
<!-- proof-12: body_md -->
First, it follows immediately from $a_i>i$, $i=1,2,\cdots$, that
$$
0\leqslant \kappa(i)<i,\qquad i=1,2,\cdots,
$$
and hence $(t_r)_{r\in\mathbb{N}_0}$ is well defined. Clearly, $t_{1}\geqslant t_{0}\geqslant 1$, $\kappa$ is increasing and
$$
\lim\limits_{r\to+\infty}\kappa(r)=+\infty.
$$
We prove by induction that
$$
1\leqslant t_{r-1}\leqslant t_r.
$$

Suppose that $t_0,t_1,\cdots,t_{r-1}$ have already been constructed. Then, for $r\geqslant 2$, it follows from \eqref{shi-huanmancanshu} that
$$
t_{r-1}\leqslant 2t_{\kappa(r-1)}
\leqslant 2t_{\kappa(r)},
\qquad
t_{r-1}\leqslant \Psi(a_{r-2})
\leqslant \Psi(a_{r-1}),
$$
and hence
$$
t_r\geqslant t_{r-1}\geqslant 1.
$$
This proves that
$$
1\leqslant t_{r-1}\leqslant t_r
$$
for every $r\in\mathbb{N}$. Now, for $r\in\mathbb{N}$, \eqref{shi-huanmancanshu} gives
$$
t_r
=\prod\limits_{i=1}^{r}\frac{t_i}{t_{i-1}}
\leqslant
\prod\limits_{i=1}^{r}\left(1+\frac{1}{i}\right)
=r+1,
$$
which proves \eqref{shi-huanmancanshujie}.

By the evident inequality
$
a_i\geqslant 4a_{i-1},i=1,2,\cdots,
$
and \eqref{shi-huanmancanshu}, we obtain
$$
\frac{t_r}{a_r}
\leqslant
\frac{\left(1+\frac{1}{r}\right)t_{r-1}}{4a_{r-1}}
\leqslant
\frac{1}{2}\frac{t_{r-1}}{a_{r-1}}
\leqslant
\frac{1}{2},
$$
which proves \eqref{shi-yuanzigaodu}.

Suppose that
$$
\lim\limits_{r\to\infty}t_r=t<\infty.
$$
Then, by \eqref{shi-huanmancanshu} and
$$
\lim\limits_{r\to\infty}2t_{\kappa(r)}=2t,
\qquad
\lim\limits_{r\to\infty}\Psi(a_{r-1})=+\infty,
$$
we have, for all sufficiently large $r$,
$$
t_r=\left(1+\frac{1}{r}\right)t_{r-1}.
$$
This clearly implies
$$
\lim\limits_{r\to\infty}t_r=+\infty,
$$
a contradiction. Thus we have proved \eqref{shi-jixian}.
<!-- end proof-12 -->
<!-- lemma-8: body_md -->
\label{yinli-fanshugouzao}
The expression $\|\cdot\|_Y$ in Definition~\ref{dingyi-fanlikongjian} defines a norm on $\mathbb{C}^{(\mathbb{N})}$ satisfying
\begin{equation}
\|\textbf{z}\|_{\ell^\infty}\leqslant\|\textbf{z}\|_Y\leqslant\|\textbf{z}\|_{\ell^1},
\qquad \textbf{z}\in\mathbb{C}^{(\mathbb{N})}.
\label{shi-fanlifanshubijiao}
\end{equation}
Moreover, whenever $\textbf{x},\textbf{y}\in\mathbb{C}^{(\mathbb{N})}$ and $|\textbf{x}|\leqslant|\textbf{y}|$,
\begin{equation}
\|\textbf{x}\|_Y\leqslant\|\textbf{y}\|_Y.
\label{shi-fanlifanshudandiao}
\end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-8 -->
<!-- proof-13: body_md -->
$e_j\in\mathcal{G}_0$ for every $j\in\mathbb{N}$. By \eqref{shi-yuanzigaodu}, every
$\textbf{u}\in\mathcal{G}$ satisfies $\|\textbf{u}\|_{\ell^\infty}\leqslant 1$.
Thus, for each  representation
$|\textbf{z}|=\sum\limits_{v=1}^s\lambda_v\textbf{u}_v,\textbf{z}=\left( z_{j} \right)_{j\in \mathbb{N}} \in \mathbb{C}^{(\mathbb{N})}$,
$$
\| \textbf{z} \|_{\ell^{\infty}} \leqslant \sum\limits_{v=1}^{s} |\lambda_{v} |\| \textbf{u}_{v}\|_{\ell^{\infty}} \leqslant \sum\limits_{v=1}^{s} |\lambda_{v} |.
$$
Taking the infimum, we obtain $\|\textbf{z}\|_{\ell^{\infty}} \leqslant \|\textbf{z}\|_{Y}$. By $|\textbf{z}|=\sum\limits_{j\in\mathrm{supp}\textbf{z}}|z_j|e_j$, we have$$\|\textbf{z}\|_{Y} \leqslant \sum\limits_{j\in \mathrm{supp} \textbf{z}} \left| z_{j} \right| =\|\textbf{z}\|_{\ell^{1}},$$which gives \eqref{shi-fanlifanshubijiao}.

Let $r\in\mathbb{N}_0$ and
$
\textbf{u}=\frac{t_r}{a_r}\sum\limits_{j\in F}\epsilon_j e_j\in\mathcal{G}_r.
$
For any  $\theta_j\in[-1,1]$, $j\in F$, one has
\begin{equation}
    \frac{t_r}{a_r}\sum\limits_{j\in F}\theta_j\epsilon_j e_j
    =
    \sum\limits_{\delta=\left( \delta_{j} \right)_{j\in F}\in\{-1,1\}^{F}}
    \left(\prod\limits_{j\in F}\frac{1+\delta_j\theta_j}{2}\right)
    \left(\frac{t_r}{a_r}\sum\limits_{j\in F}\delta_j\epsilon_j e_j\right).\label{shi-fuhaopingjun}
\end{equation}
Indeed, we have $$
\begin{aligned}
    \sum\limits_{\delta\in\{-1,1\}^{F}}
    \prod\limits_{j\in F}\frac{1+\delta_j\theta_j}{2}
    &=
    \prod\limits_{j\in F}
    \left(
    \sum\limits_{\delta_j\in\{-1,1\}}
    \frac{1+\delta_j\theta_j}{2}
    \right) =
    \prod\limits_{j\in F}
    \left(
    \frac{1+\theta_j}{2}
    +
    \frac{1-\theta_j}{2}
    \right)
    =1.
\end{aligned}
$$
Furthermore, for every fixed $k\in F$,
$$
\begin{aligned}
    \sum\limits_{\delta\in\{-1,1\}^{F}}
    \delta_k
    \prod\limits_{j\in F}\frac{1+\delta_j\theta_j}{2}
    &=
    \left(
    \sum\limits_{\delta_k\in\{-1,1\}}
    \delta_k\frac{1+\delta_k\theta_k}{2}
    \right)
    \prod\limits_{\substack{j\in F\\ j\neq k}}
    \left(
    \sum\limits_{\delta_j\in\{-1,1\}}
    \frac{1+\delta_j\theta_j}{2}
    \right) \\
    &=
    \left(
    \frac{1+\theta_k}{2}
    -
    \frac{1-\theta_k}{2}
    \right)
    \prod\limits_{\substack{j\in F\\ j\neq k}}1 =
    \theta_k.
\end{aligned}
$$
Thus $$\begin{aligned}
    &\sum\limits_{\delta \in \{ -1,1\}^{F}} \left( \prod\limits_{j\in F} \frac{1+\delta_{j} \theta_{j}}{2} \right) \left( \frac{t_{r}}{a_{r}} \sum\limits_{j\in F} \delta_{j} \epsilon_{j} e_{j} \right)\\ &\  =\frac{t_{r}}{a_{r}} \sum\limits_{k\in F} \epsilon_{k} e_{k}\sum\limits_{\delta \in \{ -1,1\}^{F}} \left( \delta_{k} \prod\limits_{j\in F} \frac{1+\delta_{j} \theta_{j}}{2} \right)\\ &\  =\frac{t_{r}}{a_{r}} \sum\limits_{k\in F} \theta_{k} \epsilon_{k} e_{k},\end{aligned}$$
which proves \eqref{shi-fuhaopingjun}.

Now let $\textbf{x},\textbf{y}\in\mathbb{C}^{(\mathbb{N})}$ satisfy
$
|\textbf{x}|\leqslant|\textbf{y}|.
$ Put

$$
\theta_j\triangleq
\begin{cases}
    \frac{|x_j|}{|y_j|},&y_j\neq 0,\\[1mm]
    0,&y_j=0,
\end{cases}
\qquad j\in\mathbb{N}.
$$

Then $0\leqslant\theta_j\leqslant1$ and
$
|x_j|=\theta_j|y_j|, j\in\mathbb{N}.
$ Fix a representation

$$
|\textbf{y}|=\sum\limits_{v=1}^{s}\lambda_v\textbf{u}_v,
\qquad
\lambda_v\in\mathbb{R},\quad \textbf{u}_v\in\mathcal{G}.
$$

For each $v=1,2,\cdots,s$, write
$
\textbf{u}_v=
\frac{t_{r_v}}{a_{r_v}}
\sum\limits_{j\in F_v}\epsilon_{v,j}e_j.
$ By \eqref{shi-fuhaopingjun}, there exist nonnegative numbers
$\mu_{v,\delta}$, $\delta\in\{-1,1\}^{F_v}$, satisfying $
\sum\limits_{\delta\in\{-1,1\}^{F_v}}\mu_{v,\delta}=1,
$ such that
$$
\frac{t_{r_v}}{a_{r_v}}
\sum\limits_{j\in F_v}\theta_j\epsilon_{v,j}e_j
=
\sum\limits_{\delta\in\{-1,1\}^{F_v}}
\mu_{v,\delta}
\left(
\frac{t_{r_v}}{a_{r_v}}
\sum\limits_{j\in F_v}\delta_j\epsilon_{v,j}e_j
\right),
$$

where every sequence in parentheses belongs to
$\mathcal{G}_{r_v}\subset\mathcal{G}$. Consequently,
$$
\begin{aligned}
    |\textbf{x}|
    &=
    \sum\limits_{v=1}^{s}\lambda_v
    \frac{t_{r_v}}{a_{r_v}}
    \sum\limits_{j\in F_v}\theta_j\epsilon_{v,j}e_j\\
    &=
    \sum\limits_{v=1}^{s}
    \sum\limits_{\delta\in\{-1,1\}^{F_v}}
    \lambda_v\mu_{v,\delta}
    \left(
    \frac{t_{r_v}}{a_{r_v}}
    \sum\limits_{j\in F_v}\delta_j\epsilon_{v,j}e_j
    \right).
\end{aligned}
$$

Thus the last expression is a representation of
$|\textbf{x}|$, and the sum of the absolute values of its coefficients satisfies

$$
\begin{aligned}
    \sum\limits_{v=1}^{s}
    \sum\limits_{\delta\in\{-1,1\}^{F_v}}
    |\lambda_v|\mu_{v,\delta}
    &=
    \sum\limits_{v=1}^{s}
    |\lambda_v|
    \sum\limits_{\delta\in\{-1,1\}^{F_v}}
    \mu_{v,\delta}=
    \sum\limits_{v=1}^{s}|\lambda_v|.
\end{aligned}
$$

Hence $
\|\textbf{x}\|_Y
\leqslant
\sum\limits_{v=1}^{s}|\lambda_v|.
$ Since the  representation of $|\textbf{y}|$ was arbitrary, taking the infimum yields \eqref{shi-fanlifanshudandiao}.

Concatenating representations of $|\textbf{z}|$ and
$|\textbf{w}|$, and then applying
\eqref{shi-fanlifanshudandiao}, gives
$$
\|\textbf{z}+\textbf{w}\|_Y
\leqslant
\left\||\textbf{z}|+|\textbf{w}|\right\|_Y
\leqslant
\|\textbf{z}\|_Y+\|\textbf{w}\|_Y.
$$
Moreover, the definition gives
$\|\lambda\textbf{z}\|_Y=|\lambda|\|\textbf{z}\|_Y$
for $\lambda\in\mathbb{C}$, and positive definiteness follows from
\eqref{shi-fanlifanshubijiao}. Hence $\|\cdot\|_Y$ is a norm on
$\mathbb{C}^{(\mathbb{N})}$.
<!-- end proof-13 -->
<!-- definition-2: body_md -->
\label{dingyi-fanlikongjian-Y}
	Define
	$$
	Y
	\triangleq
	\left\{
	\textbf{z}\in c_0:
	(P_n\textbf{z})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
	\text{ is a Cauchy sequence in }
	(\mathbb{C}^{(\mathbb{N})},\|\cdot\|_{Y})
	\right\}.
	$$
	For $\textbf{z}\in Y$, put
	\begin{equation}
		\|\textbf{z}\|_Y
		\triangleq
		\lim\limits_{n\to\infty}\|P_n\textbf{z}\|_{Y}.
		\label{shi-fanlikongjianfanshu}
	\end{equation}
<!-- end definition-2 -->
<!-- proposition-3: body_md -->
\label{mingti-fanlixingzhi}
Let $Y$ be the space defined in Definition~\ref{dingyi-fanlikongjian-Y}. Then the following
assertions hold.
\begin{enumerate}
\item\label{xiang-fanlikongjian}
$Y$ is a complex Banach sequence space and
$\mathbb{C}^{(\mathbb{N})}$ is dense in $Y$.

\item\label{xiang-duichenji}
$(e_j)_{j\in\mathbb{N}}$ is a normalized $1$-symmetric Schauder basis
of $Y$. More precisely, $\|e_j\|_Y=1$ for every $j\in\mathbb{N}$, and
\begin{equation}
\left\|\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}\omega_jz_je_{\pi(j)}\right\|_Y
=
\left\|\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}z_je_j\right\|_Y
\label{shi-zhihuanxiangwei}
\end{equation}
for every $n\in\mathbb{N}$, every permutation $\pi$ of $\mathbb{N}$,
and all $z_j,\omega_j\in\mathbb{C}$ with $|\omega_j|=1$,
$j=1,\cdots,n$. 

\item\label{xiang-shibuyuxubu}
For every $\textbf{z}\in Y$,
\begin{equation}
|\textbf{z}|
\in Y\cap\mathbb{R}^{\mathbb{N}},
\qquad
\left\||\textbf{z}|\right\|_Y=\|\textbf{z}\|_Y.
\label{shi-qumobaofan}
\end{equation}
Moreover,
\begin{equation}
\left\{\textbf{x}\in Y\cap\mathbb{R}^{\mathbb{N}}:
\|\textbf{x}\|_Y\leqslant1\right\}
=
\overline{\mathrm{conv}\mathcal{G}}^{\,Y},
\label{shi-shiqiutubao}
\end{equation}
where $\mathrm{conv}\mathcal{G}$ denotes the set of finite
convex combinations of elements of $\mathcal{G}$.

\end{enumerate}
<!-- end proposition-3 -->
<!-- proof-14: body_md -->
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{Step 1}\par\nopagebreak[4]


First, it is easy to see that $Y$ is a complex linear space containing $\mathbb{C}^{(\mathbb{N})}$, and that $\|\cdot\|_Y$ is a seminorm on $Y$.

For every $\textbf{z}\in Y$ and $n\in\mathbb{N}$,
\eqref{shi-fanlifanshubijiao} gives
$$
\|P_n\textbf{z}\|_{\ell^\infty}
\leqslant
\|P_n\textbf{z}\|_{Y}.
$$
Since $\textbf{z}\in c_0$,
$
P_n\textbf{z}\to\textbf{z}
$
in $\ell^\infty$. Therefore,
\begin{equation}
	\|\textbf{z}\|_{\ell^\infty}
	\leqslant
	\|\textbf{z}\|_Y,
	\qquad
	\textbf{z}\in Y.
	\label{shi-fanliqianru}
\end{equation}
In particular, $\|\textbf{z}\|_Y=0$ implies
$\textbf{z}=\textbf{0}$, so $\|\cdot\|_Y$ is a norm on $Y$.

We next prove that $Y$ is complete. We first note that, for $m\geqslant n$, \eqref{shi-fanlifanshudandiao} yields
$$
\|P_n\textbf{z}\|_{Y}
\leqslant
\|P_m\textbf{z}\|_{Y}.
$$
Letting $m\to\infty$ proves \begin{equation}
\|P_n\textbf{z}\|_{Y}
\leqslant
\|\textbf{z}\|_{Y},n=1,2,\cdots.\label{shi-fanlitouyingsuosuo}
\end{equation}

Let
$(\textbf{z}^{(k)})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}$
be a Cauchy sequence in $Y$. By \eqref{shi-fanliqianru},
it is Cauchy in $c_0$. Since $c_0$ is complete, there exists
$\textbf{z}\in c_0$ such that
\begin{equation}
	\lim\limits_{k\to\infty}
	\|\textbf{z}^{(k)}-\textbf{z}\|_{\ell^\infty}=0.
	\label{shi-fanliclingjixian}
\end{equation}
Fix $\varepsilon>0$. Choose $K\in\mathbb{N}$ such that
\begin{equation}
	\|\textbf{z}^{(k)}-\textbf{z}^{(l)}\|_Y<\varepsilon,
	\qquad
	k,l\geqslant K.
	\label{shi-fanlibibeilie}
\end{equation}
By \eqref{shi-fanlitouyingsuosuo},
\begin{equation}
\|P_n(\textbf{z}^{(k)}-\textbf{z}^{(l)})\|_{Y}
<\varepsilon,
\qquad
n\in\mathbb{N},\quad k,l\geqslant K.\label{1}
\end{equation}
For fixed $n$ and $k\geqslant K$, \eqref{shi-fanlifanshubijiao} and
\eqref{shi-fanliclingjixian} give
$$
\begin{aligned}
	\|P_n(\textbf{z}^{(l)}-\textbf{z})\|_{Y}
	&\leqslant
	\|P_n(\textbf{z}^{(l)}-\textbf{z})\|_{\ell^1}\leqslant
	n\|\textbf{z}^{(l)}-\textbf{z}\|_{\ell^\infty}
	\longrightarrow0
	\qquad(l\to\infty).
\end{aligned}
$$
Consequently, letting $l\to\infty$ in \eqref{1} yields
\begin{equation}
	\|P_n(\textbf{z}^{(k)}-\textbf{z})\|_{Y}
	\leqslant\varepsilon,
	\qquad
	n\in\mathbb{N},\quad k\geqslant K.
	\label{shi-fanlijixianyoujiekongzhi}
\end{equation}

Fix $k\geqslant K$. For $n>m$, by
\eqref{shi-fanlifanshudandiao} and
\eqref{shi-fanlijixianyoujiekongzhi},
$$
\begin{aligned}
	\|(P_n-P_m)\textbf{z}\|_{Y}
	&\leqslant
	\|(P_n-P_m)(\textbf{z}-\textbf{z}^{(k)})\|_{Y}
	+
	\|(P_n-P_m)\textbf{z}^{(k)}\|_{Y}\\
	&\leqslant
	\|P_n(\textbf{z}-\textbf{z}^{(k)})\|_{Y}
	+
	\|(P_n-P_m)\textbf{z}^{(k)}\|_{Y}\\
	&\leqslant
	\varepsilon+
	\|(P_n-P_m)\textbf{z}^{(k)}\|_{Y}.
\end{aligned}
$$
Since $\textbf{z}^{(k)}\in Y$, the sequence
$(P_n\textbf{z}^{(k)})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}$ is Cauchy with respect to
$\|\cdot\|_{Y}$. Hence
$$
\limsup\limits_{m,n\to\infty}
\|(P_n-P_m)\textbf{z}\|_{Y}
\leqslant\varepsilon.
$$Hence
$(P_n\textbf{z})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}$ is Cauchy with respect to
$\|\cdot\|_{Y}$. Therefore $\textbf{z}\in Y$.

Now \eqref{shi-fanlijixianyoujiekongzhi} and the definition of
$\|\cdot\|_Y$ give
$$
\|\textbf{z}^{(k)}-\textbf{z}\|_Y
=
\lim\limits_{n\to\infty}
\|P_n(\textbf{z}^{(k)}-\textbf{z})\|_{Y}
\leqslant\varepsilon,
\qquad
k\geqslant K.
$$
Thus
$
\textbf{z}^{(k)}\to\textbf{z}
$
in $Y$, and $Y$ is complete.

We next verify the coordinatewise monotonicity. Let
$\textbf{z}\in Y$ and
$\textbf{w}\in\mathbb{C}^{\mathbb{N}}$ satisfy
$$
|\textbf{w}|\leqslant|\textbf{z}|.
$$
Since $\textbf{z}\in c_0$, one has $\textbf{w}\in c_0$. For $n>m$,  \eqref{shi-fanlifanshudandiao} gives
$$
\|(P_n-P_m)\textbf{w}\|_{Y}
\leqslant
\|(P_n-P_m)\textbf{z}\|_{Y}.
$$
Since $(P_n\textbf{z})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}$ is Cauchy with respect to
$\|\cdot\|_{Y}$, so is
$(P_n\textbf{w})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}$. Thus $\textbf{w}\in Y$.
Moreover,
$$
\|P_n\textbf{w}\|_{Y}
\leqslant
\|P_n\textbf{z}\|_{Y},
\qquad n\in\mathbb{N},
$$
and therefore
$
\|\textbf{w}\|_Y
\leqslant
\|\textbf{z}\|_Y.
$
Hence $Y$ satisfies \eqref{shi-fanshudandiao}.

Finally, let $\textbf{z}\in Y$. Since
$(P_n\textbf{z})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}$ is Cauchy with respect to
$\|\cdot\|_{Y}$,
$$
\begin{aligned}
	\|\textbf{z}-P_n\textbf{z}\|_Y
	&=
	\lim\limits_{m\to\infty}
	\|P_m(\textbf{z}-P_n\textbf{z})\|_{Y}=
	\lim\limits_{m\to\infty}
	\|P_m\textbf{z}-P_n\textbf{z}\|_{Y}
	\longrightarrow0
	\qquad(n\to\infty).
\end{aligned}
$$
Thus $\mathbb{C}^{(\mathbb{N})}$ is dense in $Y$. This proves
\hyperref[xiang-fanlikongjian]{\textup{(\ref*{xiang-fanlikongjian})}}.

\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{Step 2}\par\nopagebreak[4]

By \hyperref[xiang-fanlikongjian]{\textup{(\ref*{xiang-fanlikongjian})}} and Lemma~\ref{yinli-xuliekongjian}~\hyperref[xiang-wutiaojianji]{\textup{(\ref*{xiang-wutiaojianji})}}, the sequence
$(e_j)_{j\in\mathbb{N}}$ is a $1$-unconditional Schauder basis of $Y$.
By \eqref{shi-fanlifanshubijiao}, $\|e_j\|_Y=1$ for every
$j\in\mathbb{N}$. A coordinate permutation maps $\mathcal{G}$ onto
itself, and hence, by the definition of $\|\cdot\|_Y$, preserves the
norm. By \eqref{shi-fanlifanshudandiao},
multiplication of the coordinates by scalars of modulus $1$ also
preserves the norm, since it does not change the coordinatewise
absolute value. This proves  \hyperref[xiang-duichenji]{\textup{(\ref*{xiang-duichenji})}}.

\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{Step 3}\par\nopagebreak[4]
It is easy to see that \eqref{shi-qumobaofan} holds and $$\|\textbf{u}\|_{Y} \leqslant 1,\qquad \textbf{u}\in \mathcal{G}.$$

The set
$Y\cap\mathbb{R}^{\mathbb{N}}$ is closed in $Y$, because convergence
in $Y$ implies coordinatewise convergence. Hence
$$
\overline{\mathrm{conv}\mathcal{G}}^{\,Y}
\subset
\left\{\textbf{x}\in Y\cap\mathbb{R}^{\mathbb{N}}:
\|\textbf{x}\|_Y\leqslant1\right\}.
$$
To prove the reverse inclusion, first take 
$\textbf{x}\in \mathbb{R}^{(\mathbb{N})}$ with $\|\textbf{x}\|_Y\leqslant1$ and fix
$\varepsilon>0$. Choose a representation
$$
|\textbf{x}|=\sum\limits_{v=1}^{s}\lambda_v\textbf{u}_v,
\qquad
\sum\limits_{v=1}^{s}|\lambda_v|<1+\varepsilon.
$$
For every $j\in\mathbb{N}$, choose $\theta_j\in\{-1,1\}$ such that
$x_j=\theta_j|x_j|$, taking $\theta_j=1$ when $x_j=0$. For
$v=1,\cdots,s$, write $\textbf{u}_v=(u_{v,j})_{j\in\mathbb{N}}$ and define
$$
\textbf{w}_v\triangleq
\sum\limits_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\theta_ju_{v,j} e_j.
$$
One has
$\textbf{w}_v\in\mathcal{G}$. Now define
$$
\widetilde{\textbf{u}}_v\triangleq
\begin{cases}
	\textbf{w}_v,&\lambda_v\geqslant0,\\
	-\textbf{w}_v,&\lambda_v<0.
\end{cases}
$$
We have
$\widetilde{\textbf{u}}_v\in\mathcal{G}$. Moreover,
$$
\begin{aligned}
	\textbf{x}
	&=\sum\limits_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\theta_j|x_j|e_j=\sum\limits_{j\in\mathbb{N}}
	\theta_j
	\left(
	\sum\limits_{v=1}^{s}\lambda_vu_{v,j}
	\right)e_j\\
	&=\sum\limits_{v=1}^{s}\lambda_v
	\sum\limits_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\theta_ju_{v,j} e_j=\sum\limits_{v=1}^{s}\lambda_v \textbf{w}_v
	=\sum\limits_{v=1}^{s}|\lambda_v|\widetilde{\textbf{u}}_v.
\end{aligned}
$$
Note 
$$\frac{\textbf{x}}{1+\varepsilon} =\sum\limits_{v=1}^{s} \frac{|\lambda_{v} |}{1+\varepsilon} \widetilde{\textbf{u}}_{v} +\frac{1}{2} \left( 1-\frac{\sum\limits_{v=1}^{s} |\lambda_{v} |}{1+\varepsilon} \right) e_{1}+\frac{1}{2} \left( 1-\frac{\sum\limits_{v=1}^{s} |\lambda_{v} |}{1+\varepsilon} \right) \left( -e_{1} \right) \in \mathrm{conv}\mathcal{G}.$$
Letting $\varepsilon\to0$ proves
\begin{equation}
\textbf{x}\in\overline{\mathrm{conv}\mathcal{G}}^{\,Y},
\qquad
\textbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{(\mathbb{N})},\quad \|\textbf{x}\|_Y\leqslant1.
\label{shi-youxianshitubao}
\end{equation}
Finally, if $\textbf{x}\in Y\cap\mathbb{R}^{\mathbb{N}}$ and
$\|\textbf{x}\|_Y\leqslant1$, then each $P_n\textbf{x}$ satisfies \eqref{shi-youxianshitubao}. Moreover, since $P_{n}\textbf{x} \to \textbf{x}$, we have $\textbf{x} \in \overline{\mathrm{conv} \mathcal{G}}^{Y}$. This proves \eqref{shi-shiqiutubao}, and hence \hyperref[xiang-shibuyuxubu]{\textup{(\ref*{xiang-shibuyuxubu})}}.
<!-- end proof-14 -->
<!-- proposition-4: body_md -->
\label{mingti-jibenhanshu}\label{mingti-jiejinyikehe}
Define
$$
\varphi(n)\triangleq
\left\|\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}e_j\right\|_Y,
\qquad n\in\mathbb{N}.
$$
Then, for every nonempty finite set $F\subset\mathbb{N}$ and every
choice of $\omega_j \in \mathbb{C}$ with $|\omega_j|=1$, $j\in F$,
\begin{equation}
\left\|\sum\limits_{j\in F}\omega_je_j\right\|_Y=\varphi(|F|).
\label{shi-xiangweifanshu}
\end{equation}
Moreover,
\begin{equation}
\varphi(a_r)=\frac{a_r}{t_r},
\qquad r\in\mathbb{N}_0,
\label{shi-yuanzifanshu}
\end{equation}
and for all $n\geqslant a_{r-1},n,r\in\mathbb{N}$,
\begin{equation}
t_r\leqslant\frac{2r}{\varphi(r)},
\qquad
\varphi(n)\leqslant\frac{n}{t_{r-1}}.
\label{shi-canshuyujibenhanshu}
\end{equation}
For every
$n\in\mathbb{N}$ and every $\textbf{z}\in Y$ satisfying
$|\mathrm{supp}\textbf{z}|\leqslant n$, it holds that
\begin{equation}
\frac{1}{\Psi(n)}\|\textbf{z}\|_{\ell^1}
\leqslant\|\textbf{z}\|_Y\leqslant\|\textbf{z}\|_{\ell^1}.
\label{shi-youxianweibijiao}
\end{equation}
<!-- end proposition-4 -->
<!-- proof-15: body_md -->
We first establish the identity
\begin{equation}
\frac{n}{\varphi(n)}
=
\sup\limits_{\substack{\textbf{z}\in Y,\ \|\textbf{z}\|_Y\leqslant1\\
\mathrm{supp}\textbf{z}\subset\{1,\cdots,n\}}}
\|\textbf{z}\|_{\ell^1}
=
\sup\limits_{k\in\mathbb{N}_0}\frac{t_k}{a_k}\min\{n,a_k\},
\qquad n\in\mathbb{N}.
\label{shi-jibenhanshugongshi}
\end{equation}

Fix $n\in\mathbb{N}$ and let $\textbf{z} \in Y$ with $\mathrm{supp}\ \textbf{z}\subset\{1,2,\cdots,n\}$. For each permutation $\pi\in S_n$, set

$$
\textbf{z}^{\pi}
\triangleq
\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}|z_j|e_{\pi(j)}.
$$
We have
$$
\frac{1}{n!}\sum\limits_{\pi\in S_n}\textbf{z}^{\pi}
=
\frac{\|\textbf{z}\|_{\ell^1}}{n}
\sum\limits_{k=1}^{n}e_k.
$$

By \eqref{shi-zhihuanxiangwei}, for every $\pi\in S_n$,

$$
\|\textbf{z}^{\pi}\|_Y
=
\||\textbf{z}|\|_Y
=
\|\textbf{z}\|_Y.
$$

Consequently, using the triangle inequality and the definition of $\varphi(n)$,

$$
\begin{aligned}
    \frac{\varphi(n)}{n}\|\textbf{z}\|_{\ell^1}
    &=
    \left\|
    \frac{\|\textbf{z}\|_{\ell^1}}{n}
    \sum\limits_{k=1}^{n}e_k
    \right\|_Y=
    \left\|
    \frac{1}{n!}\sum\limits_{\pi\in S_n}\textbf{z}^{\pi}
    \right\|_Y\leqslant
    \frac{1}{n!}\sum\limits_{\pi\in S_n}\|\textbf{z}^{\pi}\|_Y=
    \|\textbf{z}\|_Y.
\end{aligned}
$$

Note that $$\left\| \frac{1}{\varphi (n)} \sum\limits_{j=1}^{n} e_{j} \right\|_{Y} =1,\left\| \frac{1}{\varphi (n)} \sum\limits_{j=1}^{n} e_{j} \right\|_{\ell^{1}} =\frac{n}{\varphi \left( n \right)}.$$Thus the first equality in
\eqref{shi-jibenhanshugongshi} follows. Hence

\begin{equation}
1\leqslant\frac{n}{\varphi(n)}
\leqslant\frac{n+1}{\varphi(n+1)},
\qquad n=1,2,\cdots.
\label{shi-jibenhanshudandiao}
\end{equation}
We now prove that\begin{equation}
	\frac{n}{\varphi(n)}
	=
	\sup\limits_{\textbf{u}=\left( u_{j} \right)_{j\in \mathbb{N}}\in\mathcal{G}}\left|\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}u_j\right|.\label{shi-1}
\end{equation}
For every $\textbf{x}=(x_i)_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\in\mathrm{conv}\mathcal{G}$, write
$$
\textbf{x}=\sum\limits_{v=1}^{s}\lambda_v \textbf{u}_v,
\qquad
\textbf{u}_v\in\mathcal{G},\quad
\lambda_v\geqslant0,\quad v=1,2,\cdots,s,
\qquad
\sum\limits_{v=1}^{s}\lambda_v=1.
$$
Then
$$
\begin{aligned}
	\left|\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}x_i\right|
	&=
	\left|\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}\sum\limits_{v=1}^{s}\lambda_vu_{v,i}\right|
	=
	\left|\sum\limits_{v=1}^{s}\lambda_v\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}u_{v,i}\right|\\
	&\leqslant
	\sum\limits_{v=1}^{s}\lambda_v
	\left|\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}u_{v,i}\right|
	\leqslant
	\sup\limits_{\textbf{u}=(u_j)_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\in\mathcal{G}}
	\left|\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}u_j\right|.
\end{aligned}
$$
Consequently,
$$
\sup\limits_{\textbf{x}=(x_j)_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\in\mathrm{conv}\mathcal{G}}
\left|\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}x_j\right|
=
\sup\limits_{\textbf{u}=(u_j)_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\in\mathcal{G}}
\left|\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}u_j\right|.
$$

The real linear functional
$\textbf{x}\mapsto\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}x_j$ is continuous, since \eqref{shi-fanliqianru} gives
$$
\left|\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}x_j\right|
\leqslant n\|\textbf{x}\|_{\ell^\infty}
\leqslant n\|\textbf{x}\|_Y.
$$
Combining this with \eqref{shi-shiqiutubao}, we obtain
$$
\sup\limits_{\substack{\textbf{x}\in Y\cap\mathbb{R}^{\mathbb{N}}\\
		\|\textbf{x}\|_Y\leqslant1}}
\left|\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}x_j\right|=\sup\limits_{\textbf{x}=(x_j)_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\in\overline{\mathrm{conv}\mathcal{G}}^{\,Y}}
\left|\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}x_j\right|
=
\sup\limits_{\textbf{u}=(u_j)_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\in\mathcal{G}}
\left|\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}u_j\right|.
$$
For every $\textbf{x}\in Y\cap\mathbb{R}^{\mathbb{N}}$ satisfying $\|\textbf{x}\|_{Y}\leqslant1$, we have
$$
\left|\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}x_j\right|
\leqslant
\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}|x_j|
=
\left\|P_n\textbf{x}\right\|_{\ell^1}
\leqslant
\frac{n}{\varphi(n)},
$$which gives \eqref{shi-1}.



For each $k\in\mathbb{N}_0$, a sequence in $\mathcal{G}_k$ has
$a_k$ nonzero coordinates, all of absolute value $\frac{t_k}{a_k}$.
Hence
$$
\sup\limits_{\textbf{u}\in\mathcal{G}_k}\left|\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}u_j\right|
=\frac{t_k}{a_k}\min\{n,a_k\}.
$$
Taking the supremum over $k$ proves the second
equality in \eqref{shi-jibenhanshugongshi}.

For $n=a_r$, if $k\leqslant r$, then
$$
\frac{t_k}{a_k}\min\{a_r,a_k\}=t_k\leqslant t_r.
$$
If $k>r$, then \eqref{shi-yuanzigaodu} gives
$$
\frac{t_k}{a_k}\min\{a_r,a_k\}
=\frac{t_k}{a_k}a_r\leqslant t_r.
$$
so
$\varphi(a_r)=\frac{a_r}{t_r}$, which proves
\eqref{shi-yuanzifanshu}. Formula \eqref{shi-xiangweifanshu} follows from
\eqref{shi-zhihuanxiangwei}.

Since $a_{\kappa(r)}\leqslant r$, the term $k=\kappa(r)$ in
\eqref{shi-jibenhanshugongshi} gives
$
\frac{r}{\varphi(r)}\geqslant t_{\kappa(r)}.
$
Combining this with $t_{r}\leqslant 2t_{\kappa \left( r \right)}$ yields
$t_r\leqslant\frac{2r}{\varphi(r)}$.
For every integer $n\geqslant a_{r-1}$,
\eqref{shi-jibenhanshudandiao} and
\eqref{shi-yuanzifanshu} give
$$
\frac{n}{\varphi(n)}
\geqslant\frac{a_{r-1}}{\varphi(a_{r-1})}=t_{r-1}.
$$
Thus \eqref{shi-canshuyujibenhanshu} holds.

Fix $n\in\mathbb{N}$.
Since $\left( a_{r} \right)_{r\in \mathbb{N}_0}$ is strictly increasing, $a_0=1$, and $a_r\to+\infty$,
there is a unique $r\in\mathbb{N}$ such that
$$
a_{r-1}\leqslant n<a_r.
$$
For $0\leqslant k\leqslant r-1$, we have
$$
\frac{t_k}{a_k}\min\{n,a_k\}=t_k\leqslant t_{r-1},
$$
with equality when $k=r-1$. For $k\geqslant r$,
\eqref{shi-yuanzigaodu} gives
$$
\frac{t_k}{a_k}\min\{n,a_k\}
=n\frac{t_k}{a_k}
\leqslant\frac{n}{a_r}t_r,
$$
with equality when $k=r$. Therefore
\eqref{shi-jibenhanshugongshi} yields
\begin{equation}
\frac{n}{\varphi(n)}
=\max\left\{t_{r-1},\frac{n}{a_r}t_r\right\}.
\label{shi-jibenhanshufenduan}
\end{equation}
For the choice \eqref{shi-huanmancanshu}, use $t_{r-1}\leqslant t_r$, $n<a_r$,
and the monotonicity of $\Psi$ to obtain

\begin{equation}
\frac{n}{\varphi(n)}
\leqslant t_r
\leqslant\Psi(a_{r-1})
\leqslant\Psi(n).
\label{shi-yudingjiejin}
\end{equation}

Let $\textbf{z}\in Y$ satisfy
$|\mathrm{supp}\textbf{z}|\leqslant n$.
If $\textbf{z}=\textbf{0}$, then
\eqref{shi-youxianweibijiao} is immediate.
Otherwise choose a permutation $\pi$ of $\mathbb{N}$ such that
$$
\mathrm{supp}\textbf{z}
\subset\{\pi(1),\cdots,\pi(n)\},
$$
and put
$$
\textbf{w}\triangleq\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}z_{\pi(j)}e_j.
$$
Applying \eqref{shi-jibenhanshugongshi} to
$\frac{\textbf{w}}{\|\textbf{w}\|_Y}$ and then using
\eqref{shi-yudingjiejin} and \eqref{shi-zhihuanxiangwei}, we obtain
$$
\|\textbf{z}\|_{\ell^1}
=\|\textbf{w}\|_{\ell^1}
\leqslant\frac{n}{\varphi(n)}\|\textbf{w}\|_Y
\leqslant\Psi(n)\|\textbf{z}\|_Y.
$$
Since $\textbf{z}$ has finite support, the opposite estimate
$\|\textbf{z}\|_Y\leqslant\|\textbf{z}\|_{\ell^1}$ follows from
\eqref{shi-fanlifanshubijiao}.
This proves \eqref{shi-youxianweibijiao}.
<!-- end proof-15 -->
<!-- corollary-2: body_md -->
\label{tuilun-yangeqianru}\label{tuilun-jiruoshoulian}
The inclusions
$$
\ell^1\subsetneq Y\subsetneq c_0
$$
are continuous, and
\begin{equation}
\|e_j\|_Y=1,\qquad j\in\mathbb{N},
\qquad
e_j\rightharpoonup0\quad\text{in }Y.
\label{shi-jiruoshoulian}
\end{equation}
<!-- end corollary-2 -->
<!-- proof-16: body_md -->
If $\textbf{z}\in\ell^1$, then
\eqref{shi-fanlifanshubijiao} implies
$$
\|P_{n}\textbf{z} -P_{m}\textbf{z}\|_{Y} =\left\|\sum\limits_{j=m+1}^n z_j e_j\right\|_Y
\leqslant\sum\limits_{j=m+1}^n|z_j|,
\qquad n>m,n,m\in \mathbb{N}_0.
$$
Hence $\textbf{z}\in Y$. It follows that $\ell^1\subset Y$ and
$$
\|\textbf{z}\|_Y\leqslant\|\textbf{z}\|_{\ell^1},
\qquad \textbf{z}\in\ell^1.
$$
Together with \eqref{shi-fanliqianru}, this proves that both
inclusions in Corollary~\ref{tuilun-yangeqianru} are continuous.

If $Y=\ell^1$ as sets, the inverse mapping theorem applied to the
continuous inclusion $\ell^1\hookrightarrow Y$ gives a constant
$C>0$ such that
$$
\|\textbf{z}\|_{\ell^1}\leqslant C\|\textbf{z}\|_Y,
\qquad \textbf{z}\in\ell^1.
$$
Taking $\textbf{z}=\sum\limits_{j=1}^{a_r}e_j$ and using
\eqref{shi-yuanzifanshu}, we obtain $t_r\leqslant C$ for every
$r\in\mathbb{N}_0$, contrary to $t_r\to+\infty$. Hence
$\ell^1\subsetneq Y$.

If $Y=c_0$ as sets, the inverse mapping theorem applied to the
continuous inclusion $Y\hookrightarrow c_0$ gives a constant $C>0$
such that
$$
\|\textbf{z}\|_Y\leqslant C\|\textbf{z}\|_{\ell^\infty},
\qquad \textbf{z}\in Y.
$$
Taking again $\textbf{z}=\sum\limits_{j=1}^{a_r}e_j$ gives
$\frac{a_r}{t_r}\leqslant C$. However, by \eqref{shi-yuanzigaodu}, 
$$
\lim\limits_{r\to +\infty} \frac{a_{r}}{t_{r}} \geqslant \lim\limits_{r\to +\infty} 2^{r}=+\infty.
$$
This contradiction proves $Y\subsetneq c_0$.

Let $\psi\in Y^*$. Suppose that $\psi(e_j)$ does not tend to
zero. Then there exist $\varepsilon>0$ and an infinite set
$J\subset\mathbb{N}$ such that
$|\psi(e_j)|\geqslant\varepsilon$ for every $j\in J$.
For each $r\in\mathbb{N}$, choose $F_r\subset J$ with
$|F_r|=a_r$. By \eqref{shi-xiangweifanshu} and \eqref{shi-yuanzifanshu},
$$
\left\|
\frac{t_r}{a_r}\sum\limits_{j\in F_r}
\frac{\overline{\psi(e_j)}}{|\psi(e_j)|}e_j
\right\|_Y=1.
$$
Therefore,
$$
\varepsilon t_r
\leqslant
\frac{t_r}{a_r}\sum\limits_{j\in F_r}|\psi(e_j)|
=
\left|\psi\left(
\frac{t_r}{a_r}\sum\limits_{j\in F_r}
\frac{\overline{\psi(e_j)}}{|\psi(e_j)|}e_j
\right)\right|
\leqslant\|\psi\|_{Y^*}.
$$
This contradicts $t_r\to+\infty$. Hence
$\psi(e_j)\to0$ for every $\psi\in Y^*$, and therefore
$e_j\rightharpoonup0$ in $Y$. Together with
$\|e_j\|_Y=1$ from \eqref{shi-fanlifanshubijiao}, this establishes
\eqref{shi-jiruoshoulian}.
<!-- end proof-16 -->
<!-- remark-3: body_md -->
\label{zhuji-butonggou}
    The space $\ell^1$ has the Schur property; that is, every weakly convergent
    sequence in $\ell^1$ converges in norm
    (see, for example, \cite[Theorem~2.3.6, p.~37]{AK16}).
    Consequently, \eqref{shi-jiruoshoulian} implies that $Y$ is not isomorphic to
    $\ell^1$.
<!-- end remark-3 -->
<!-- lemma-9: body_md -->
\label{yinli-zhichijishu}
