Let $m,d\in\mathbb{N},P\in \mathscr{P}_0\cap \mathcal{P}^{m} \left( Y\right)$ and let $\textbf{z}\in \mathbb{C}^{(\mathbb{N})}$ with $\left| \mathrm{supp} \textbf{z} \right| =d$. Then
\begin{equation}
P^{\#}(|\textbf{z}|)
\leqslant\binom{m+d-1}{m}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|P\|_{\mathcal{P}^m(Y)}\|\textbf{z}\|_Y^m.
\label{shi-zhichijishu}
\end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-9 -->
<!-- proof-17: body_md -->
Without loss of generality, assume that $\textbf{z} \neq \textbf{0}$ and let $\mathrm{supp}\textbf{z}=\{j_1,\cdots,j_d\}$.  Consider $\textbf{x}=\left( x_{i} \right)_{i\in \mathbb{N}}\triangleq\frac{|\textbf{z}|}{\|\textbf{z}\|_Y}$.  Clearly, \eqref{shi-zhichijishu} is equivalent to
\begin{equation}
	P^{\#}(\textbf{x})\leqslant\binom{m+d-1}{m}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|P\|_{\mathcal{P}^m(Y)}.\label{shi-11}
\end{equation}
Consider
\begin{equation}
	\mathcal{A}\triangleq
	\{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_0^{(\mathbb{N})}:|\alpha|=m,\ \mathrm{supp}\alpha\subset \mathrm{supp}\textbf{z}\},
	\qquad |\mathcal{A}|=\binom{m+d-1}{m}.
	\label{shi-danxiangshishu}
\end{equation}
We have
\begin{equation}
	\begin{aligned}
		&\frac{1}{(2\pi )^{d}} \int\limits_{[0,2\pi ]^{d}} \left| P\left( \sum\limits_{\ell =1}^{d} x_{j_{\ell}}e^{\mathrm{i} \theta_{\ell}}e_{j_{\ell}} \right) \right|^{2} \mathrm{d} \theta_{1} \cdots \mathrm{d} \theta_{d}\\ &\  =\frac{1}{(2\pi )^{d}} \int\limits_{[0,2\pi ]^{d}} \left| \sum\limits_{\alpha \in \mathcal{A}} c_{\alpha}(P)\textbf{x}^{\alpha} e^{\mathrm{i} \sum\limits_{\ell =1}^{d} \alpha_{j_{\ell}} \theta_{\ell}} \right|^{2} \mathrm{d} \theta_{1} \cdots \mathrm{d} \theta_{d}\\ &\  =\frac{1}{(2\pi )^{d}} \sum\limits_{\alpha ,\beta \in \mathcal{A}} c_{\alpha}(P)\overline{c_{\beta}(P)} \textbf{x}^{\alpha +\beta} \int\limits_{[0,2\pi ]^{d}} e^{\mathrm{i} \sum\limits_{\ell =1}^{d} (\alpha_{j_{\ell}} -\beta_{j_{\ell}} )\theta_{\ell}}\mathrm{d} \theta_{1} \cdots \mathrm{d} \theta_{d}\\ &\  =\frac{1}{(2\pi )^{d}} \sum\limits_{\alpha \in \mathcal{A}} \left| c_{\alpha}(P) \right|^{2} \textbf{x}^{2\alpha} \int\limits_{[0,2\pi ]^{d}} 1\mathrm{d} \theta_{1} \cdots \mathrm{d} \theta_{d} =\sum\limits_{\alpha \in \mathcal{A}} \left| c_{\alpha}(P) \right|^{2} \textbf{x}^{2\alpha}\\ &\  \leqslant \frac{1}{(2\pi )^{d}} \int\limits_{[0,2\pi ]^{d}} \|P\|_{\mathcal{P}^{m} \left( Y \right)}^{2} \cdot \left\| \sum\limits_{\ell =1}^{d} x_{j_{\ell}}e^{\mathrm{i} \theta_{\ell}}e_{j_{\ell}} \right\|_{Y}^{2m} \mathrm{d} \theta_{1} \cdots \mathrm{d} \theta_{d}\\ &\  =\frac{1}{(2\pi )^{d}} \int\limits_{[0,2\pi ]^{d}} \|P\|_{\mathcal{P}^{m} \left( Y \right)}^{2} \cdot \|\textbf{x}\|_{Y}^{2m} \mathrm{d} \theta_{1} \cdots \mathrm{d} \theta_{d} =\|P\|_{\mathcal{P}^{m} \left( Y \right)}^{2},\end{aligned}
	\label{shi-xishupingfanghe}
\end{equation}
where the penultimate equality follows from \eqref{shi-qumobaofan}.  Then the Cauchy--Schwarz inequality, \eqref{shi-danxiangshishu}, and \eqref{shi-xishupingfanghe} give
\begin{equation}
	\begin{aligned}
		P^{\#}(\textbf{x})
		&=\sum\limits_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}|c_\alpha(P)|\textbf{x}^\alpha
		\leqslant|\mathcal{A}|^{\frac{1}{2}}
		\left(\sum\limits_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}|c_\alpha(P)|^2\textbf{x}^{2\alpha}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leqslant\binom{m+d-1}{m}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|P\|_{\mathcal{P}^m(Y)},
	\end{aligned}
	\label{shi-xishuhepingfangjie}
\end{equation}
which gives \eqref{shi-zhichijishu}.
<!-- end proof-17 -->
<!-- lemma-10: body_md -->
\label{yinli-suijixishuhua}
Let $m\in\mathbb{N}$ and let $P\in \mathscr{P}_0\cap \mathcal{P}^{m} \left( Y\right)$. 
$\textbf{x}=\left( x_{i} \right)_{i\in \mathbb{N}}\in\mathbb{C}^{(\mathbb{N})}\cap[0,+\infty)^{\mathbb{N}}$
satisfies \begin{equation}
P^{\#}(\textbf{x})
\leqslant\frac{m^m}{m!}\binom{3m-1}{m}^{\frac{1}{2}}
\left(\|\textbf{x}\|_Y+
\frac{\varphi(m)}{m}\|\textbf{x}\|_{\ell^1}\right)^m
\|P\|_{\mathcal{P}^m(Y)}.
\label{shi-xishuhuaguji}
\end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-10 -->
<!-- proof-18: body_md -->
Without loss of generality, assume that $\textbf{x} \neq \textbf{0}$. Put
\begin{equation}
	I\triangleq
	\left\{j\in \mathbb{N}:x_j\geqslant\frac{\|\textbf{x}\|_{\ell^1}}{m}\right\},
	\qquad
	J\triangleq
	\left\{j\in \mathbb{N}:0<x_j<\frac{\|\textbf{x}\|_{\ell^1}}{m}\right\}.
	\label{shi-daxiaozuobiao}
\end{equation}
Then $I\cup J=\mathrm{supp}\textbf{x}$ is finite. Moreover,
$$
\frac{\|\textbf{x}\|_{\ell^1}}{m}|I|
\leqslant\sum\limits_{j\in I}x_j
\leqslant\|\textbf{x}\|_{\ell^1},
$$
and hence $|I|\leqslant m$. For
$(i_1,\cdots,i_m)\in(J\cup\{0\})^m$, define
\begin{equation}
	\begin{aligned}	\lambda_{i_1,\cdots,i_m}&\triangleq
		\left(
		1-\sum\limits_{j\in J}\frac{x_j}{\|\textbf{x}\|_{\ell^1}}
		\right)^{|\{1\leqslant \nu \leqslant m:i_\nu=0\}|}
		\prod\limits_{\substack{1\leqslant\nu\leqslant m\\ i_\nu\neq0}}
		\frac{x_{i_\nu}}{\|\textbf{x}\|_{\ell^1}},\\F_{i_1,\cdots,i_m}&	\triangleq\{i_\nu:1\leqslant\nu\leqslant m,\ i_\nu\neq0\},\end{aligned}
	\label{shi-quanzhongzhichi}
\end{equation}
and set
\begin{equation}
	\textbf{y}_{i_1,\cdots,i_m}
	\triangleq
	\sum\limits_{j\in I}x_je_j+
	\frac{\|\textbf{x}\|_{\ell^1}}{m}
	\sum\limits_{j\in F_{i_1,\cdots,i_m}}e_j.
	\label{shi-xishuxiangliang}
\end{equation}
Since
$
0\leqslant
\sum\limits_{j\in J}\frac{x_j}{\|\textbf{x}\|_{\ell^1}}
\leqslant1,
$
all $\lambda_{i_1,\cdots,i_m}$ are nonnegative, and 
\begin{equation}
	\begin{aligned}
		\sum\limits_{(i_1,\cdots,i_m)\in(J\cup\{0\})^m}
		\lambda_{i_1,\cdots,i_m}
		&=
		\left(
		1-\sum\limits_{j\in J}\frac{x_j}{\|\textbf{x}\|_{\ell^1}}
		+\sum\limits_{j\in J}\frac{x_j}{\|\textbf{x}\|_{\ell^1}}
		\right)^m
		=1.
	\end{aligned}
	\label{shi-quanzhongweiyi}
\end{equation}
For every $(i_1,\cdots,i_m)\in(J\cup\{0\})^m$, one has
$|F_{i_1,\cdots,i_m}|\leqslant m$. Thus, by \eqref{shi-fanshudandiao} and \eqref{shi-xiangweifanshu},
\begin{equation}
	\begin{aligned}
		|\mathrm{supp}\textbf{y}_{i_1,\cdots,i_m}|
		&\leqslant|I|+|F_{i_1,\cdots,i_m}|
		\leqslant2m,\\
		\|\textbf{y}_{i_1,\cdots,i_m}\|_Y
		&\leqslant
		\left\|\sum\limits_{j\in I}x_je_j\right\|_Y
		+\frac{\|\textbf{x}\|_{\ell^1}}{m}
		\left\|\sum\limits_{j\in F_{i_1,\cdots,i_m}}e_j\right\|_Y\\
		&\leqslant
		\|\textbf{x}\|_Y+
		\frac{\varphi(m)}{m}\|\textbf{x}\|_{\ell^1}.
	\end{aligned}
	\label{shi-xishufanshujie}
\end{equation}

Fix $\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_0^{(\mathbb{N})}$ with $|\alpha|=m$ and
$\textbf{x}^{\alpha}>0$, and put
$
S\triangleq J\cap\mathrm{supp}\alpha.
$
Define
$$
\mathcal I_S
\triangleq
\left\{
(i_1,\cdots,i_m)\in(J\cup\{0\})^m:
\left| \{ \nu \in \{ 1,\cdots ,m\} :i_{\nu}=j\} \right|=1
\ \text{for every }j\in S
\right\}.
$$
Then, by \eqref{shi-quanzhongzhichi},
\begin{align}
	\sum\limits_{(i_1,\cdots,i_m)\in\mathcal I_S}
	\lambda_{i_1,\cdots,i_m}
	&=
	\frac{m!}{(m-|S|)!}
	\prod\limits_{j\in S}
	\frac{x_j}{\|\textbf{x}\|_{\ell^1}}
	\nonumber\\
	&\quad\times
	\sum\limits_{\substack{
			(i_1,\cdots,i_{m-|S|})\\
			\in((J\setminus S)\cup\{0\})^{m-|S|}
	}}
	\prod\limits_{\substack{1\leqslant \nu\leqslant m-|S|\\ i_\nu=0}}
	\left(
	1-\sum\limits_{j\in J}
	\frac{x_j}{\|\textbf{x}\|_{\ell^1}}
	\right)
	\prod\limits_{\substack{1\leqslant \nu\leqslant m-|S|\\ i_\nu\neq0}}
	\frac{x_{i_\nu}}{\|\textbf{x}\|_{\ell^1}}
	\nonumber\\
	&=
	\frac{m!}{(m-|S|)!}
	\prod\limits_{j\in S}
	\frac{x_j}{\|\textbf{x}\|_{\ell^1}}
	\left(
	1-\sum\limits_{j\in J}
	\frac{x_j}{\|\textbf{x}\|_{\ell^1}}
	+
	\sum\limits_{j\in J\setminus S}
	\frac{x_j}{\|\textbf{x}\|_{\ell^1}}
	\right)^{m-|S|}
	\nonumber\\
	&=
	\frac{m!}{(m-|S|)!}
	\prod\limits_{j\in S}
	\frac{x_j}{\|\textbf{x}\|_{\ell^1}}
	\left(
	1-\sum\limits_{j\in S}
	\frac{x_j}{\|\textbf{x}\|_{\ell^1}}
	\right)^{m-|S|}.
	\label{shi-zhidingzuobiaoquan}
\end{align}
By \eqref{shi-daxiaozuobiao},
$
\sum\limits_{j\in S}\frac{x_j}{\|\textbf{x}\|_{\ell^1}}
\leqslant\frac{|S|}{m},
$
and therefore \eqref{shi-zhidingzuobiaoquan} is at least
\begin{equation}
	\begin{aligned}
		&\frac{m!}{(m-|S|)!}
		\prod\limits_{j\in S}\frac{x_j}{\|\textbf{x}\|_{\ell^1}}
		\left(\frac{m-|S|}{m}\right)^{m-|S|}\\
		&\qquad=
		\frac{m!}{m^m}
		\frac{(m-|S|)^{m-|S|}}{(m-|S|)!}
		\prod\limits_{j\in S}
		\frac{m x_j}{\|\textbf{x}\|_{\ell^1}}
		\geqslant
		\frac{m!}{m^m}
		\prod\limits_{j\in S}
		\frac{m x_j}{\|\textbf{x}\|_{\ell^1}}.
	\end{aligned}
	\label{shi-zhidingzuobiaoquanjie}
\end{equation}

It follows from \eqref{shi-zhidingzuobiaoquanjie} that
\begin{equation}
	\begin{aligned}
		&\sum\limits_{(i_1,\cdots,i_m)\in(J\cup\{0\})^m}
		\lambda_{i_1,\cdots,i_m}
		\textbf{y}_{i_1,\cdots,i_m}^{\alpha}\geqslant
		\sum\limits_{(i_1,\cdots,i_m)\in\mathcal I_S}
		\lambda_{i_1,\cdots,i_m}
		\textbf{y}_{i_1,\cdots,i_m}^{\alpha}
		\\&\qquad =
		\prod\limits_{j\in I}x_j^{\alpha_j}
		\prod\limits_{j\in S}
		\left(
		\frac{\|\textbf{x}\|_{\ell^1}}{m}
		\right)^{\alpha_j}
		\sum\limits_{(i_1,\cdots,i_m)\in\mathcal I_S}
		\lambda_{i_1,\cdots,i_m}
		\\
		&\qquad\geqslant
		\frac{m!}{m^m}
		\prod\limits_{j\in I}x_j^{\alpha_j}
		\prod\limits_{j\in S}
		\left(
		\frac{\|\textbf{x}\|_{\ell^1}}{m}
		\right)^{\alpha_j}
		\frac{m x_j}{\|\textbf{x}\|_{\ell^1}}
		\\
		&\qquad=
		\frac{m!}{m^m}
		\prod\limits_{j\in I}x_j^{\alpha_j}
		\prod\limits_{j\in S}
		\left(
		\frac{\|\textbf{x}\|_{\ell^1}}{m}
		\right)^{\alpha_j-1}x_j
		\\
		&\qquad\geqslant
		\frac{m!}{m^m}
		\prod\limits_{j\in I}x_j^{\alpha_j}
		\prod\limits_{j\in S}x_j^{\alpha_j}
		=
		\frac{m!}{m^m}\textbf{x}^{\alpha},
	\end{aligned}
	\label{shi-dandiaoxishuquanjie}
\end{equation}where the last inequality follows from $0<x_{j}<\frac{\|\textbf{x}\|_{\ell^{1}}}{m}, j\in S\subset J$. If
$\textbf{x}^{\alpha}=0$, \eqref{shi-dandiaoxishuquanjie} also holds. Multiplying
\eqref{shi-dandiaoxishuquanjie} by $|c_\alpha(P)|$ and summing over
$|\alpha|=m$ yields
\begin{equation}
	\frac{m!}{m^m}P^{\#}(\textbf{x})
	\leqslant
	\sum\limits_{(i_1,\cdots,i_m)\in(J\cup\{0\})^m}
	\lambda_{i_1,\cdots,i_m}
	P^{\#}(\textbf{y}_{i_1,\cdots,i_m}).
	\label{shi-jiaquanduoxiangshijie}
\end{equation}
By Lemma~\ref{yinli-zhichijishu} and \eqref{shi-xishufanshujie}, for every
$(i_1,\cdots,i_m)\in(J\cup\{0\})^m$,
$$
P^{\#}(\textbf{y}_{i_1,\cdots,i_m})
\leqslant
\binom{3m-1}{m}^{\frac{1}{2}}
\left(
\|\textbf{x}\|_Y+
\frac{\varphi(m)}{m}\|\textbf{x}\|_{\ell^1}
\right)^m
\|P\|_{\mathcal{P}^m(Y)}.
$$
Combining this estimate with \eqref{shi-quanzhongweiyi} and
\eqref{shi-jiaquanduoxiangshijie} gives
$$
P^{\#}(\textbf{x})
\leqslant
\frac{m^m}{m!}\binom{3m-1}{m}^{\frac{1}{2}}
\left(
\|\textbf{x}\|_Y+
\frac{\varphi(m)}{m}\|\textbf{x}\|_{\ell^1}
\right)^m
\|P\|_{\mathcal{P}^m(Y)},
$$
which is \eqref{shi-xishuhuaguji}.
<!-- end proof-18 -->
<!-- lemma-11: body_md -->
\label{yinli-yuanziqufan}
	Let $m\in\mathbb{N}$ and let $B\in\mathcal{L}^m(Y)$ depend on
	finitely many coordinates. Then
	\begin{equation}
		\|B^{\#}\|_{\mathcal{L}^m(Y)}
		=
		\sup\limits_{\textbf{u}^{(1)},\cdots,\textbf{u}^{(m)}\in\mathcal{G}^{+}}
		B^{\#}
		\left(
		\textbf{u}^{(1)},\cdots,\textbf{u}^{(m)}
		\right).
		\label{shi-yuanziqufan}
	\end{equation}
	If $B$ is symmetric and
	$P(\textbf{z})\triangleq B(\textbf{z},\cdots,\textbf{z})$, then
	\begin{equation}
		P^{\#}(\textbf{z})
		=
		B^{\#}(\textbf{z},\cdots,\textbf{z}),
		\qquad
		\|P\|_{\mathcal{P}^m(Y)}
		\leqslant
		\|B\|_{\mathcal{L}^m(Y)},
		\label{shi-duijiaoxishu}
	\end{equation}
	and, if
	$$
	\textbf{u}^{(1)},\cdots,\textbf{u}^{(m)}\in Y,
	\qquad
	\textbf{u}^{(j)}=|\textbf{u}^{(j)}|,
	\quad 1\leqslant j\leqslant m,
	$$
	then
	\begin{equation}
		B^{\#}
		\left(
		\textbf{u}^{(1)},\cdots,\textbf{u}^{(m)}
		\right)
		\leqslant
		\frac{m^m}{m!}
		P^{\#}
		\left(
		\frac{1}{m}
		\sum\limits_{j=1}^{m}\textbf{u}^{(j)}
		\right).
		\label{shi-duichenpingjun}
	\end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-11 -->
<!-- proof-19: body_md -->
Set
	$$
	K\triangleq
	\sup\limits_{\textbf{u}^{(1)},\cdots,\textbf{u}^{(m)}\in\mathcal{G}^{+}}
	B^{\#}\left(\textbf{u}^{(1)},\cdots,\textbf{u}^{(m)}\right).
	$$
	For $\textbf{u}^{(1)},\cdots,\textbf{u}^{(m)}\in\mathcal{G}$, by
	\eqref{shi-zhixishuxingshi},
	$$
	\left|
	B^{\#}\left(\textbf{u}^{(1)},\cdots,\textbf{u}^{(m)}\right)
	\right|
	\leqslant
	B^{\#}\left(
	|\textbf{u}^{(1)}|,\cdots,|\textbf{u}^{(m)}|
	\right)
	\leqslant K.
	$$
	If
	$$
	\textbf{x}^{(j)}
	=
	\sum\limits_{\nu=1}^{N_j}
	\lambda_{\nu}^{(j)}\textbf{u}_{\nu}^{(j)}
	\in\mathrm{conv}\mathcal{G},
	\qquad
	\lambda_{\nu}^{(j)}\geqslant0,
	\qquad
	\sum\limits_{\nu=1}^{N_j}\lambda_{\nu}^{(j)}=1,j=1,2,\cdots,m,
	$$
	then multilinearity gives
	\begin{align*}
		\left|
		B^{\#}\left(\textbf{x}^{(1)},\cdots,\textbf{x}^{(m)}\right)
		\right|
		&\leqslant
		\sum\limits_{\nu_1=1}^{N_1}\cdots
		\sum\limits_{\nu_m=1}^{N_m}
		\left(
		\prod\limits_{j=1}^{m}\lambda_{\nu_j}^{(j)}
		\right)
		\left|
		B^{\#}\left(
		\textbf{u}_{\nu_1}^{(1)},\cdots,
		\textbf{u}_{\nu_m}^{(m)}
		\right)
		\right|
		\\
		&\leqslant
		K
		\sum\limits_{\nu_1=1}^{N_1}\cdots
		\sum\limits_{\nu_m=1}^{N_m}
		\prod\limits_{j=1}^{m}\lambda_{\nu_j}^{(j)}
		=
		K.
	\end{align*}
	Thus, by \eqref{shi-shiqiutubao} and continuity,
	$$
	\left|
	B^{\#}\left(\textbf{x}^{(1)},\cdots,\textbf{x}^{(m)}\right)
	\right|
	\leqslant K
	$$
	whenever $\textbf{x}^{(j)}\in Y\cap\mathbb{R}^{\mathbb{N}}$ and
	$\|\textbf{x}^{(j)}\|_{Y}\leqslant1$, $j=1,\cdots,m$.
	
	Now let $\textbf{z}^{(j)}\in Y$ with
	$\|\textbf{z}^{(j)}\|_{Y}\leqslant1,j=1,2,\cdots,m$. By
	\eqref{shi-qumobaofan},
	$$
	|\textbf{z}^{(j)}|\in Y\cap\mathbb{R}^{\mathbb{N}},
	\qquad
	\left\||\textbf{z}^{(j)}|\right\|_{Y}
	=
	\|\textbf{z}^{(j)}\|_{Y}
	\leqslant1,
	$$
	and therefore
	$$
	\left|
	B^{\#}\left(\textbf{z}^{(1)},\cdots,\textbf{z}^{(m)}\right)
	\right|
	\leqslant
	B^{\#}\left(
	|\textbf{z}^{(1)}|,\cdots,|\textbf{z}^{(m)}|
	\right)
	\leqslant K.
	$$
	Hence
	$\|B^{\#}\|_{\mathcal{L}^{m}(Y)}\leqslant K$.
	Conversely, by \eqref{shi-shiqiutubao},
	$$
	\mathcal{G}^{+}
	\subset
	\mathcal{G}
	\subset
	\overline{\mathrm{conv}\mathcal{G}}^{\,Y}
	=
	\left\{
	\textbf{x}\in Y\cap\mathbb{R}^{\mathbb{N}}:
	\|\textbf{x}\|_{Y}\leqslant1
	\right\},
	$$
	so that $K\leqslant\|B^{\#}\|_{\mathcal{L}^{m}(Y)}$. This proves
	\eqref{shi-yuanziqufan}.
	
By the symmetry of $B$ and the definitions of $P^{\#}$ and $B^{\#}$, \eqref{shi-duijiaoxishu} follows immediately.
	
	Finally, let $\textbf{u}^{(j)}\in Y$ satisfy
	$\textbf{u}^{(j)}=|\textbf{u}^{(j)}|$, $j=1,\cdots,m$. Since
	$B^{\#}$ has nonnegative coefficients,
	$$
	B^{\#}\left(
	\textbf{u}^{(j_1)},\cdots,\textbf{u}^{(j_m)}
	\right)
	\geqslant0
	\qquad
	(1\leqslant j_1,\cdots,j_m\leqslant m).
	$$
	Hence, using symmetry,
	\begin{align*}
		&m^{m}
		P^{\#}\left(
		\frac{1}{m}\sum\limits_{j=1}^{m}\textbf{u}^{(j)}
		\right)
		=
		B^{\#}\left(
		\sum\limits_{j=1}^{m}\textbf{u}^{(j)},
		\cdots,
		\sum\limits_{j=1}^{m}\textbf{u}^{(j)}
		\right)
=
		\sum\limits_{j_1,\cdots,j_m=1}^{m}
		B^{\#}\left(
		\textbf{u}^{(j_1)},\cdots,\textbf{u}^{(j_m)}
		\right)
		\\
		&\qquad\geqslant
		\sum\limits_{\pi\in S_m}
		B^{\#}\left(
		\textbf{u}^{(\pi(1))},\cdots,\textbf{u}^{(\pi(m))}
		\right)=
		m!\,
		B^{\#}\left(
		\textbf{u}^{(1)},\cdots,\textbf{u}^{(m)}
		\right),
	\end{align*}
	which yields \eqref{shi-duichenpingjun}.
<!-- end proof-19 -->
<!-- lemma-12: body_md -->
\label{yinli-youxianwang}
Let $E$ be a complex normed space of dimension $d\geqslant1$, and
let $m\geqslant2$ be an integer. There is a finite set
$\mathcal{N}\subset\overline{B_E}$ such that
\begin{equation}
|\mathcal{N}|\leqslant(1+4m)^{2d},
\qquad
\|C\|_{\mathcal{L}^{m-1}(E)}
\leqslant\frac{2m}{m+1}
\max\limits_{\xi_{1} ,\cdots ,\xi_{m-1} \in \mathcal{N}} |C(\xi_{1} ,\cdots ,\xi_{m-1} )|
\label{shi-youxianwangguji}
\end{equation}
for every $C\in\mathcal{L}^{m-1}(E)$.
<!-- end lemma-12 -->
<!-- proof-20: body_md -->
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{Step 1}\par\nopagebreak[4]
	
	For $\textbf{a}\in E$ and $r>0$, write
	$$
	B_E(\textbf{a},r)
	\triangleq
	\left\{
	\textbf{x}\in E:
	\|\textbf{x}-\textbf{a}\|_E<r
	\right\}.
	$$
	Consider the largest $q\in\mathbb{N}$ for which there exist
	$\xi^{(1)},\cdots,\xi^{(q)}\in E$ satisfying
	$$
	\|\xi^{(j)}\|_E\leqslant1,
	\qquad
	1\leqslant j\leqslant q,
	$$
	and
	$$
	\|\xi^{(i)}-\xi^{(j)}\|_E>\frac{1}{2m},
	\qquad
	1\leqslant i<j\leqslant q.
	$$
	Such a largest $q$ exists since $\overline{B_E}$ is compact.
	
	The sets $\left\{ B_{E}\left( \xi^{(j)} ,\frac{1}{4m} \right) \right\}_{j=1}^{q}$are pairwise disjoint. Moreover, if
	$\textbf{x}\in B_E\left(\xi^{(j)},\frac{1}{4m}\right)$, then
	$$
	\|\textbf{x}\|_E
	\leqslant
	\|\textbf{x}-\xi^{(j)}\|_E
	+\|\xi^{(j)}\|_E
	<
	1+\frac{1}{4m}.
	$$
	Hence
	$$
	\bigcup\limits_{j=1}^{q}
	B_E\left(\xi^{(j)},\frac{1}{4m}\right)
	\subset
	\left(1+\frac{1}{4m}\right)B_E.
	$$
	
	Let $\mathrm{vol}_{2d}$ denote Lebesgue measure on the
	underlying real $2d$-dimensional vector space of $E$. We have
	$$
	\begin{aligned}
		&q\left(\frac{1}{4m}\right)^{2d}
		\mathrm{vol}_{2d}(B_E)
		=
		\sum\limits_{j=1}^{q}
		\mathrm{vol}_{2d}
		\left(
		B_E\left(\xi^{(j)},\frac{1}{4m}\right)
		\right)
		\\
		&\quad=
		\mathrm{vol}_{2d}
		\left(
		\bigcup\limits_{j=1}^{q}
		B_E\left(\xi^{(j)},\frac{1}{4m}\right)
		\right)
		\\
		&\quad\leqslant
		\mathrm{vol}_{2d}
		\left(
		\left(1+\frac{1}{4m}\right)B_E
		\right)
		=
		\left(1+\frac{1}{4m}\right)^{2d}
		\mathrm{vol}_{2d}(B_E).
	\end{aligned}
	$$
	Therefore
$
	q
	\leqslant
	\left(
	\frac{1+\frac{1}{4m}}
	{\frac{1}{4m}}
	\right)^{2d}
	=
	(1+4m)^{2d}.
$ Put
	$$
	\mathcal{N}
	\triangleq
	\left\{
	\xi^{(1)},\cdots,\xi^{(q)}
	\right\}.
	$$
	Then
$
	|\mathcal{N}|
	\leqslant
	(1+4m)^{2d}.
$

	\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{Step 2}\par\nopagebreak[4]
	
	Let $C\in\mathcal{L}^{m-1}(E)$, and put
$
	M_C
	\triangleq
\max\limits_{\xi_{1} ,\cdots ,\xi_{m-1} \in \mathcal{N}} |C(\xi_{1} ,\cdots ,\xi_{m-1} )|.
$
	Fix
	$\textbf{x}^{(1)},\cdots,\textbf{x}^{(m-1)}
	\in\overline{B_E}$.
		By the maximality of $q$, for every $1\leqslant j\leqslant m-1$ there exists
	$\xi^{(k_{j})}\in\mathcal{N}$ such that
	$$
	\|\textbf{x}^{(j)}-\xi^{(k_{j})}\|_E
	\leqslant
	\frac{1}{2m}.
	$$
	By multilinearity,
	$$
	\begin{aligned}
		&\left|
		C\left(
		\textbf{x}^{(1)},\cdots,\textbf{x}^{(m-1)}
		\right)
		-
		C\left(
		\xi^{(k_1)},\cdots,\xi^{(k_{m-1})}
		\right)
		\right|
		\\
		&\quad\leqslant
		\sum\limits_{j=1}^{m-1}
		\left|
		C\left(
		\xi^{(k_1)},\cdots,\xi^{(k_{j-1})},
		\textbf{x}^{(j)}-\xi^{(k_j)},
		\textbf{x}^{(j+1)},\cdots,\textbf{x}^{(m-1)}
		\right)
		\right|
		\\
		&\quad\leqslant
		\sum\limits_{j=1}^{m-1}
		\|C\|_{\mathcal{L}^{m-1}(E)}
		\left\|
		\textbf{x}^{(j)}-\xi^{(k_j)}
		\right\|_E\leqslant
		\frac{m-1}{2m}
		\|C\|_{\mathcal{L}^{m-1}(E)}.
	\end{aligned}
	$$
	It follows that
	$$
\begin{aligned}\left| C\left( \textbf{x}^{(1)} ,\cdots ,\textbf{x}^{(m-1)} \right) \right|&\leqslant \left| C\left( \xi^{(k_{1})} ,\cdots ,\xi^{(k_{m-1})} \right) \right| +\frac{m-1}{2m} \| C\|_{\mathcal{L}^{m-1} (E)}\\ &\leqslant M_{C}+\frac{m-1}{2m} \| C\|_{\mathcal{L}^{m-1} (E)} .\end{aligned}
	$$
Hence
	$$
	\|C\|_{\mathcal{L}^{m-1}(E)}
	\leqslant
	M_C
	+
	\frac{m-1}{2m}
	\|C\|_{\mathcal{L}^{m-1}(E)}.
	$$
	Therefore
	$$
	\|C\|_{\mathcal{L}^{m-1}(E)}
	\leqslant
	\frac{2m}{m+1}
	\max\limits_{\xi^{(1)},\cdots,\xi^{(m-1)}\in\mathcal{N}}
	\left|
	C\left(\xi^{(1)},\cdots,\xi^{(m-1)}\right)
	\right|.
	$$
	This proves \eqref{shi-youxianwangguji}.
<!-- end proof-20 -->
<!-- proposition-5: body_md -->
\label{mingti-duoxianxingguji}
For every $m\in\mathbb{N}$ and every symmetric
$B\in\mathcal{L}^m(Y)$ depending on finitely many coordinates,
\begin{equation}
\|B^{\#}\|_{\mathcal{L}^m(Y)}
\leqslant K_m\|B\|_{\mathcal{L}^m(Y)}.
\label{shi-duoxianxingguji}
\end{equation}
<!-- end proposition-5 -->
<!-- proof-21: body_md -->
The argument is inspired by
\cite[Lemma~4.2 and  Theorem~4.3, pp.~59--62]{DK05}.
We first verify that
\begin{equation}
K_m\geqslant4K_{m-1},\qquad m\geqslant2.
\label{shi-changshuzengzhang}
\end{equation}
Firstly,
$$K_{2}=4\sqrt{10} \theta_{2}^{2} \geqslant 4\sqrt{10} >4K_{1},$$Then for $m\geqslant 3$, since $\left( t_{r} \right)_{r\in \mathbb{N}_{0}} ,\left( \varphi \left( n \right) \right)_{n\in \mathbb{N}}$ are increasing, we have
$$
\begin{aligned}\theta_{m}&=1+\frac{\varphi \left( m \right)}{m} t_{m^{2}-1}\geqslant 1+\frac{\varphi \left( m-1 \right)}{m} t_{\left( m-1 \right)^{2} -1}\\ &=1+\frac{\left( \theta_{m-1} -1 \right) \left( m-1 \right)}{m} =\frac{1}{m} +\frac{m-1}{m} \theta_{m-1}.\end{aligned}
$$
Hence
$$\log \theta_{m} \geqslant \log \left( \frac{1}{m} \cdot 1+\left( 1-\frac{1}{m} \right) \theta_{m-1} \right) \geqslant \frac{1}{m} \log 1+\left( 1-\frac{1}{m} \right) \log \theta_{m-1},$$
that is, $\theta_m^m\geqslant\theta_{m-1}^{m-1}$. Moreover, for $m\geqslant 3$, we have
$$
\frac{\frac{m^{m}}{m!}}{\frac{\left( m-1 \right)^{m-1}}{\left( m-1 \right) !}} =\left( 1+\frac{1}{m-1} \right)^{m-1} \geqslant 2,\frac{\binom{3m-1}{m}}{\binom{3m-4}{m-1}} =\frac{3\left( 3m-1 \right) \left( 3m-2 \right)}{2m\left( 2m-1 \right)} >1 .
$$
These inequalities prove \eqref{shi-changshuzengzhang}.

We now argue by induction on $m$. For $m=1$, put
$$
B\left( \textbf{z} \right) = \sum\limits_{i=1}^{n} B\left( e_{i} \right) z_{i},\textbf{z} =\left( z_{i} \right)_{i\in \mathbb{N}} \in Y, \omega_j\triangleq
\begin{cases}
\frac{\overline{B(e_j)}}{|B(e_j)|},&B(e_j)\neq0,\\[2pt]
1,&B(e_j)=0,
\end{cases}j=1,2,\cdots.
$$For any $\textbf{z} \in Y$, by \eqref{shi-zhihuanxiangwei} and \eqref{shi-fanshudandiao}, we have
$$\begin{aligned}\left| B^{\#}\left( \textbf{z} \right) \right|&=\left| \sum\limits_{i=1}^{n} \left| B\left( e_{i} \right) \right| z_{i} \right| =\left| \sum\limits_{i=1}^{n} \omega_{i}B\left( e_{i} \right) z_{i} \right|\\ &\leqslant \|B\| \cdot \left\| \sum\limits_{i=1}^{n} \omega_{i}z_{i}e_{i} \right\|_{Y} =\|B\| \cdot \left\| \sum\limits_{i=1}^{n} z_{i}e_{i} \right\|_{Y}\\ &\leqslant \|B\| \cdot \|\textbf{z}\|_{Y},\end{aligned}$$
and hence $\|B^{\#}\|\leqslant\|B\|$. This proves \eqref{shi-duoxianxingguji} in the case $m=1$.
Assume that $m\geqslant 2$ and that \eqref{shi-duoxianxingguji} holds with $m$ replaced by $m-1$. Fix $B$ as in the statement and set
$P(\textbf{z})\triangleq B(\textbf{z},\cdots,\textbf{z})$.
By Lemma~\ref{yinli-yuanziqufan} \eqref{shi-yuanziqufan},
it suffices to estimate
$B^{\#}(\textbf{u}^{(1)},\cdots,\textbf{u}^{(m)})$ for vectors of the form
\begin{equation}
	\textbf{u}^{(j)}
	=
	\frac{t_{r_j}}{a_{r_j}}
	\sum\limits_{k\in F_j}e_k,
	\qquad
	r_j\in\mathbb{N}_0,\quad
	F_j\subset\mathbb{N},\quad
	|F_j|=a_{r_j},
	\qquad
	1\leqslant j\leqslant m.
	\label{shi-guinayuanzi}
\end{equation}
By Proposition~\ref{mingti-jiejinyikehe}
\begin{equation}
\|\textbf{u}^{(j)}\|_Y=1,
\qquad \|\textbf{u}^{(j)}\|_{\ell^1}=t_{r_j},\qquad j=1,2,\cdots,m.
\label{shi-yuanziliangfanshu}
\end{equation}
Put $r\triangleq\max\limits_{1\leqslant j\leqslant m}r_j$.

\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{Step 1}\par\nopagebreak[4]
If $r<m^2$, set $\textbf{x}\triangleq\frac{1}{m}\sum\limits_{j=1}^{m}\textbf{u}^{(j)}$.
Then
$\textbf{x}\in\mathbb{C}^{(\mathbb{N})}\cap[0,+\infty)^{\mathbb{N}}$.
By \eqref{shi-yuanziliangfanshu}, the triangle inequality, and
monotonicity of $(t_s)_{s\in\mathbb{N}_0}$, we have
$$
\begin{aligned}
	\|\textbf{x}\|_Y
	\leqslant\frac{1}{m}\sum\limits_{j=1}^{m}\|\textbf{u}^{(j)}\|_Y=1,\qquad	\|\textbf{x}\|_{\ell^1}
	=\frac{1}{m}\sum\limits_{j=1}^{m}t_{r_j}
	\leqslant t_r\leqslant t_{m^2-1}.
\end{aligned}
$$
Applying Lemma~\ref{yinli-suijixishuhua} \eqref{shi-xishuhuaguji} to $\textbf{x}$ and using
the definition of $\theta_m$ in \eqref{shi-guinachangshu}, we obtain
$$
\begin{aligned}
	P^{\#}(\textbf{x})
	&\leqslant
	\frac{m^m}{m!}\binom{3m-1}{m}^{\frac{1}{2}}
	\left(
	\|\textbf{x}\|_Y+
	\frac{\varphi(m)}{m}\|\textbf{x}\|_{\ell^1}
	\right)^m\|P\|\leqslant
	\frac{m^m}{m!}\binom{3m-1}{m}^{\frac{1}{2}}
	\theta_m^m\|P\|.
\end{aligned}
$$
Consequently, Lemma~\ref{yinli-yuanziqufan} \eqref{shi-duichenpingjun}, \eqref{shi-duijiaoxishu},
and \eqref{shi-guinachangshu} yield
\begin{equation}
	\begin{aligned}
		B^{\#}(\textbf{u}^{(1)},\cdots,\textbf{u}^{(m)})
		&\leqslant\frac{m^m}{m!}P^{\#}(\textbf{x})\leqslant
		\left(\frac{m^m}{m!}\right)^2
		\binom{3m-1}{m}^{\frac{1}{2}}\theta_m^m\|B\|
		=K_m\|B\|.
	\end{aligned}
	\label{shi-xiaocengguji}
\end{equation}

\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{Step 2}\par\nopagebreak[4]
Suppose that $r\geqslant m^2$. Without loss of generality, assume that $r_m=r$.
Since  $r-2\geqslant2$,
the defining inequalities \eqref{shi-huanmancanshu} for $\left( t_{r} \right)_{r\in \mathbb{N}_{0}}$ give
\begin{equation}
\frac{t_r}{t_{r-2}}
=\frac{t_r}{t_{r-1}}\frac{t_{r-1}}{t_{r-2}}
\leqslant\left(1+\frac{1}{r}\right)
\left(1+\frac{1}{r-1}\right)=\frac{r+1}{r-1}.
\label{shi-liangcengbizhi}
\end{equation}
Let $J\triangleq\{j\in\{1,\cdots,m-1\}:r_j>r-2\}$.
For $j\in J$,
\begin{equation}
	\begin{aligned}
		&\frac{t_{r_j}}{t_{r-2}}\binom{a_{r_j}}{a_{r-2}}^{-1}
		\sum\limits_{\substack{D\subset F_j\\ |D|=a_{r-2}}}
		\left(\frac{t_{r-2}}{a_{r-2}}\sum\limits_{k\in D}e_k\right)\\
		&\quad=
		\frac{t_{r_j}}{a_{r-2}}\binom{a_{r_j}}{a_{r-2}}^{-1}
		\sum\limits_{k\in F_j}
		\left|\left\{D\subset F_j:|D|=a_{r-2},\ k\in D\right\}\right|e_k\\
		&\quad=
		\frac{t_{r_j}}{a_{r-2}}\binom{a_{r_j}}{a_{r-2}}^{-1}
		\binom{a_{r_j}-1}{a_{r-2}-1}\sum\limits_{k\in F_j}e_k
		=
		\frac{t_{r_j}}{a_{r_j}}\sum\limits_{k\in F_j}e_k
		=
		\textbf{u}^{(j)}.
	\end{aligned}
	\label{shi-jiangcengpingjun}
\end{equation}
For $1\leqslant j<m$, define the finite nonempty set
$$
\mathcal E_j\triangleq
\begin{cases}
\left\{\frac{t_{r-2}}{a_{r-2}}\sum\limits_{k\in D}e_k:
D\subset F_j,\ |D|=a_{r-2}\right\},&j\in J,\\[2pt]
\{\textbf{u}^{(j)}\},&j\notin J,
\end{cases}
\qquad
\Lambda\triangleq\prod\limits_{j\in J}\frac{t_{r_j}}{t_{r-2}}.
$$
Note that $$
|\mathcal E_j|
=
\begin{cases}
	\displaystyle \binom{a_{r_j}}{a_{r-2}}, & j\in J,\\[6pt]
	1, & j\notin J.
\end{cases}$$
Choose $\textbf{v}^{(j)}\in\mathcal E_j$, $1\leqslant j<m$ such that $$
B^{\#}\left(
\textbf{v}^{(1)},\cdots,\textbf{v}^{(m-1)},\textbf{u}^{(m)}
\right)
= \max\limits_{\left(\textbf{w}^{(1)},\cdots,\textbf{w}^{(m-1)}\right)
	\in\prod\limits_{j=1}^{m-1}\mathcal E_j}
B^{\#}\left(
\textbf{w}^{(1)},\cdots,\textbf{w}^{(m-1)},\textbf{u}^{(m)}
\right).
$$ Set
$\textbf{v}^{(m)}=\textbf{u}^{(m)}$. Every $\textbf{v}^{(j)}$ with $j<m$ belongs to
$\mathcal G^+\cap\bigcup\limits_{k=0}^{r-2}\mathcal G_k$.
By multilinearity and \eqref{shi-jiangcengpingjun},
\begin{equation}
	\begin{aligned}
		B^{\#}(\textbf{u}^{(1)},\cdots,\textbf{u}^{(m)})
		&=
		\left(\prod\limits_{j\in J}\frac{t_{r_j}}{t_{r-2}}\right)
		B^{\#}\left(
		\frac{1}{|\mathcal E_1|}
		\sum\limits_{\textbf{w}^{(1)}\in\mathcal E_1}\textbf{w}^{(1)},
		\cdots,
		\frac{1}{|\mathcal E_{m-1}|}
		\sum\limits_{\textbf{w}^{(m-1)}\in\mathcal E_{m-1}}\textbf{w}^{(m-1)},
		\textbf{u}^{(m)}
		\right)\\
		&=
		\frac{\Lambda}{\prod\limits_{j=1}^{m-1}|\mathcal E_j|}
		\sum\limits_{\substack{\textbf{w}^{(j)}\in\mathcal E_j\\1\leqslant j<m}}
		B^{\#}\left(
		\textbf{w}^{(1)},\cdots,\textbf{w}^{(m-1)},\textbf{u}^{(m)}
		\right)\\
		&\leqslant
		\frac{\Lambda}{\prod\limits_{j=1}^{m-1}|\mathcal E_j|}
		\left(\prod\limits_{j=1}^{m-1}|\mathcal E_j|\right)
		B^{\#}\left(
		\textbf{v}^{(1)},\cdots,\textbf{v}^{(m)}
		\right)\\
		&=
		\Lambda B^{\#}\left(
		\textbf{v}^{(1)},\cdots,\textbf{v}^{(m)}
		\right).
	\end{aligned}
	\label{shi-jiangcengguji}
\end{equation}
Monotonicity of $\left( t_{r} \right)_{r\in \mathbb{N}_{0}}$ and \eqref{shi-liangcengbizhi} imply
\begin{equation}
1\leqslant\Lambda
\leqslant\left(\frac{t_r}{t_{r-2}}\right)^{|J|}
\leqslant\left(\frac{t_r}{t_{r-2}}\right)^{m-1}
\leqslant\left(\frac{r+1}{r-1}\right)^{m-1}.
\label{shi-jiangcengxishu}
\end{equation}
Put
\begin{equation}
U\triangleq\bigcup\limits_{j=1}^{m-1}\mathrm{supp}\textbf{v}^{(j)},
\qquad |U|\leqslant(m-1)a_{r-2}.
\label{shi-guinazhichi}
\end{equation}
The space $P_UY$ has complex dimension
$|U|\geqslant1$ and carries the
norm inherited from $Y$. For $k\in F_m$, define
\begin{equation}
B_k(\textbf{z}^{(1)},\cdots,\textbf{z}^{(m-1)})
\triangleq
B\left(P_U\textbf{z}^{(1)},\cdots,
P_U\textbf{z}^{(m-1)},e_k\right).
\label{shi-gudingzuobiao}
\end{equation}
Each $B_k$ is continuous, symmetric, and depends only on the
coordinates in $U$. By \eqref{shi-touyingsuosuo}, its norm on $Y$
equals the norm of its restriction to $\left(P_UY\right)^{m-1}$. Since
$\|\textbf{v}^{(j)}\|_Y=1$ by \eqref{shi-yuanzifanshu}, the coefficient
formula \eqref{shi-zhixishuxingshi} and the induction hypothesis give
\begin{equation}
\begin{aligned}
B^{\#}(\textbf{v}^{(1)},\cdots,\textbf{v}^{(m)})
&=\frac{t_r}{a_r}\sum\limits_{k\in F_m}
B_k^{\#}(\textbf{v}^{(1)},\cdots,\textbf{v}^{(m-1)})\\
&\leqslant\frac{t_r}{a_r}\sum\limits_{k\in F_m}\|B_k^{\#}\|
\leqslant\frac{t_r}{a_r}K_{m-1}
\sum\limits_{k\in F_m}\|B_k\|.
\end{aligned}
\label{shi-diciguina}
\end{equation}

\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{Step 3}\par\nopagebreak[4]

Choose $\mathcal N\subset\overline{B_{P_UY}}$ as in
Lemma~\ref{yinli-youxianwang}. For each $k\in F_m$, choose
$\Theta(k)\in\mathcal N^{m-1}$ at which $|B_k|$ attains its maximum
on this finite set. Equation \eqref{shi-youxianwangguji} gives
\begin{equation}
\|B_k\|\leqslant\frac{2m}{m+1}|B_k(\Theta(k))|.
\label{shi-wangdianqufan}
\end{equation}
Enumerate the distinct values of $\Theta$ as
$\Theta_\nu=(\xi_\nu^{(1)},\cdots,\xi_\nu^{(m-1)})$,
$1\leqslant\nu\leqslant M$, and put
$D_\nu\triangleq\{k\in F_m:\Theta(k)=\Theta_\nu\}$.
These sets are nonempty and form a partition of $F_m$. By
\eqref{shi-guinazhichi} and \eqref{shi-youxianwangguji},
\begin{equation}
\begin{aligned}
M\leqslant|\mathcal N|^{m-1}
\leqslant(1+4m)^{2|U|(m-1)}\leqslant(1+4m)^{2(m-1)^2a_{r-2}},\qquad
\sum\limits_{\nu=1}^{M}|D_\nu|=a_r.
\end{aligned}
\label{shi-wangdianfenzu}
\end{equation}
For $k\in D_\nu$, choose
$$
\omega_k\triangleq
\begin{cases}
\frac{\overline{B_k(\Theta_\nu)}}{|B_k(\Theta_\nu)|},
&B_k(\Theta_\nu)\neq0,\\[2pt]
1,&B_k(\Theta_\nu)=0,
\end{cases}
\qquad
\textbf{w}_\nu\triangleq\sum\limits_{k\in D_\nu}\omega_ke_k.
$$
Each $\xi_\nu^{(j)}$ belongs to $P_UY$ and has norm at most $1$.
By \eqref{shi-xiangweifanshu},
$\|\textbf{w}_\nu\|_Y=\varphi(|D_\nu|)$. Hence
\eqref{shi-gudingzuobiao}, \eqref{shi-wangdianqufan}, and multilinearity give
\begin{equation}
\begin{aligned}
\sum\limits_{k\in D_\nu}\|B_k\|
&\leqslant\frac{2m}{m+1}
\sum\limits_{k\in D_\nu}|B_k(\Theta_\nu)|=\frac{2m}{m+1}
\left|B(\xi_\nu^{(1)},\cdots,\xi_\nu^{(m-1)},\textbf{w}_\nu)\right|\\& \leqslant\frac{2m}{m+1}\|B\|\varphi(|D_\nu|).
\end{aligned}
\label{shi-fenzuxiangwei}
\end{equation}
 \eqref{shi-canshuyujibenhanshu} gives $\varphi(n)\leqslant\frac{n}{t_{r-2}}$ for $n\geqslant a_{r-2}$.
For $1\leqslant n<a_{r-2}$, \eqref{shi-jiruoshoulian} and the triangle
inequality give $\varphi(n)\leqslant n<a_{r-2}$. Thus
\begin{equation}
	\varphi(n)\leqslant\frac{n}{t_{r-2}}+a_{r-2},
	\qquad n\in\mathbb N.
	\label{shi-jibenhanshuyuxiang}
\end{equation}
Summing \eqref{shi-fenzuxiangwei}  over $\nu=1,2,\cdots,M$ and using
\eqref{shi-wangdianfenzu} and \eqref{shi-jibenhanshuyuxiang}, we obtain
\begin{equation}
	\begin{aligned}
		\sum\limits_{k\in F_m}\|B_k\|
		&\leqslant\frac{2m}{m+1}\|B\|
		\sum\limits_{\nu=1}^{M}\varphi(|D_\nu|)\leqslant\frac{2m}{m+1}\|B\|
		\left(\frac{a_r}{t_{r-2}}+Ma_{r-2}\right).
	\end{aligned}
	\label{shi-fenzuzonghe}
\end{equation}
By \eqref{shi-cengjicanshu},
\begin{equation}
	a_{r-1}=2^{a_{r-2}+1},\qquad a_r=2^{\,2^{a_{r-2}+1}+1}.
	\label{shi-liangcengditui}
\end{equation}
Define 
\begin{equation}
	\varepsilon_{m,r}\triangleq
	\frac{t_ra_{r-2}}{a_r}(1+4m)^{2(m-1)^2a_{r-2}}
	=\frac{t_ra_{r-2}(1+4m)^{2(m-1)^2a_{r-2}}}
	{2^{\,2^{a_{r-2}+1}+1}}.
	\label{shi-cengjiwucha}
\end{equation}
Then \eqref{shi-wangdianfenzu} implies
\begin{equation}
	\begin{aligned}
		\frac{t_r}{a_r}\left(\frac{a_r}{t_{r-2}}+Ma_{r-2}\right)
		&=\frac{t_r}{t_{r-2}}+\frac{t_rMa_{r-2}}{a_r}\leqslant\frac{t_r}{t_{r-2}}+\varepsilon_{m,r}
		\leqslant\frac{r+1}{r-1}+\varepsilon_{m,r}.
	\end{aligned}
	\label{shi-cengjixishu}
\end{equation}
Combining \eqref{shi-jiangcengguji}, \eqref{shi-diciguina},
\eqref{shi-fenzuzonghe}, and \eqref{shi-cengjixishu}, we obtain
\begin{equation}
	\begin{aligned}
		B^{\#}(\textbf{u}^{(1)},\cdots,\textbf{u}^{(m)})
		&\leqslant\frac{2m}{m+1}\Lambda
		\frac{t_r}{a_r}\left(\frac{a_r}{t_{r-2}}+Ma_{r-2}\right)
		K_{m-1}\|B\|\\
		&\leqslant\frac{2m}{m+1}\Lambda
		\left(\frac{t_r}{t_{r-2}}+\varepsilon_{m,r}\right)K_{m-1}\|B\|\\
		&\leqslant\frac{2m}{m+1}\left(\frac{r+1}{r-1}\right)^{m-1}
		\left(\frac{r+1}{r-1}+\varepsilon_{m,r}\right)K_{m-1}\|B\|,
	\end{aligned}
	\label{shi-dacengguji}
\end{equation}
where the last inequality uses \eqref{shi-jiangcengxishu}.

\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{Step 4}\par\nopagebreak[4]

It is easy to see that
$$
r+1\leqslant 2r^2\leqslant a_{r-2},r=4,5,\cdots,\qquad1+4m\leqslant2^{m^2}, m=2,3,\cdots.
$$
Since $r\geqslant m^2$, we obtain
\begin{equation}
(1+4m)^{2(m-1)^2a_{r-2}}
\leqslant2^{2m^2(m-1)^2a_{r-2}}
\leqslant2^{2r^2a_{r-2}}
\leqslant2^{a_{r-2}^2}.
\label{shi-wangdianbijiao}
\end{equation}
$2^{a_{r-2}+1}\geqslant2a_{r-2}^2$ and
$2^{a_{r-2}^2+1}\geqslant2a_{r-2}^4\geqslant16a_{r-2}^2$.
Together with $t_r\leqslant r+1$,
\eqref{shi-cengjiwucha}, and \eqref{shi-wangdianbijiao}, this yields
\begin{equation}
\begin{aligned}
0\leqslant\varepsilon_{m,r}
&\leqslant(r+1)a_{r-2}\,2^{a_{r-2}^2-2^{a_{r-2}+1}-1}\leqslant\frac{a_{r-2}^2}{2^{a_{r-2}^2+1}}
\leqslant\frac{1}{16}.
\end{aligned}
\label{shi-wuchajiexian}
\end{equation}
It remains to compare the coefficient in \eqref{shi-dacengguji}
with $\frac{K_m}{K_{m-1}}$. If $m=2$, then $r\geqslant4$ and
$\frac{r+1}{r-1}\leqslant\frac{5}{3}$, so
\begin{equation}
\frac{2m}{m+1}\left(\frac{r+1}{r-1}\right)^{m-1}\left(\frac{r+1}{r-1}+\varepsilon_{m,r}\right)
\leqslant\frac{4}{3}\frac{5}{3}
\left(\frac{5}{3}+\frac{1}{16}\right)
=\frac{415}{108}<4.
\label{shi-erciguinabijiao}
\end{equation}
Suppose $m\geqslant3$. For $0\leqslant x<1$,
$$
e^x=\sum\limits_{j=0}^{\infty}\frac{x^j}{j!}
\leqslant\sum\limits_{j=0}^{\infty}x^j
=\frac{1}{1-x}.
$$
Together with $\log(1+x)\leqslant x$ for $x\geqslant0$ and
$r\geqslant m^2$, this gives
\begin{equation}
\begin{aligned}
\left(\frac{r+1}{r-1}\right)^{m-1}
&=\left(1+\frac{2}{r-1}\right)^{m-1}
\leqslant e^{\frac{2(m-1)}{r-1}}\leqslant e^{\frac{2}{m+1}}
\leqslant\frac{m+1}{m-1},\\
\frac{2m}{m+1}\left(\frac{r+1}{r-1}\right)^{m-1}
&\leqslant\frac{2m}{m-1}\leqslant3,
\qquad
\frac{r+1}{r-1}\leqslant\frac{m^2+1}{m^2-1}\leqslant\frac{5}{4}.
\end{aligned}
\label{shi-gaociguinabijiao}
\end{equation}
Equations \eqref{shi-wuchajiexian} and \eqref{shi-gaociguinabijiao} imply
$$
\frac{2m}{m+1}\left(\frac{r+1}{r-1}\right)^{m-1}\left(\frac{r+1}{r-1}+\varepsilon_{m,r}\right)
\leqslant3\left(\frac{5}{4}+\frac{1}{16}\right)
=\frac{63}{16}<4.
$$
Together with \eqref{shi-erciguinabijiao} and
\eqref{shi-changshuzengzhang}, this shows that the coefficient in
\eqref{shi-dacengguji} is at most $\frac{K_m}{K_{m-1}}$. Consequently,
$B^{\#}(\textbf{u}^{(1)},\cdots,\textbf{u}^{(m)})\leqslant K_m\|B\|$ when
$r\geqslant m^2$. Combining this with \eqref{shi-xiaocengguji} and
taking the supremum in \eqref{shi-yuanziqufan} proves
\eqref{shi-duoxianxingguji} in degree $m$, completing the induction.
<!-- end proof-21 -->
<!-- proposition-6: body_md -->
\label{mingti-duoxiangshiguji}
For every $m\in\mathbb{N}_0$, every $P\in\mathcal{P}^m(Y)$, and every
$\textbf{z}\in Y$, one has
\begin{equation}
\sum\limits_{\substack{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_0^{(\mathbb{N})}\\|\alpha|=m}}
|c_\alpha(P)\textbf{z}^{\alpha}|
\leqslant C_m\|P\|_{\mathcal{P}^m(Y)}\|\textbf{z}\|_Y^m.
\label{shi-fanlixishuguji}
\end{equation}
If $P$ depends on finitely many coordinates, then
\begin{equation}
\|P^{\#}\|_{\mathcal{P}^m(Y)}\leqslant C_m\|P\|_{\mathcal{P}^m(Y)}.
\label{shi-youxianxishuguji}
\end{equation}
<!-- end proposition-6 -->
<!-- proof-22: body_md -->
For $m=0$, the polynomial $P$ is constant and both assertions follow
from $P^{\#}=|P|$. Let $m\geqslant1$, suppose first that $P$ depends
on finitely many coordinates.
The polarization formula in \cite[Lemma~7.13\,(1), pp.~84--85]{KM97}
gives
\begin{equation}
B(\textbf{x}^{(1)},\cdots,\textbf{x}^{(m)})
\triangleq\frac{1}{2^m m!}
\sum\limits_{\epsilon\in\{-1,1\}^m}
\left(\prod\limits_{j=1}^{m}\epsilon_j\right)
P\left(\sum\limits_{j=1}^{m}\epsilon_j\textbf{x}^{(j)}\right),P\left( \textbf{x} \right) =B\left( \textbf{x} ,\cdots ,\textbf{x} \right)
\label{shi-jihuagongshi}
\end{equation}
For $\|\textbf{x}^{(j)}\|_Y\leqslant1,j=1,2,\cdots,m$,
\eqref{shi-jihuagongshi} gives
$$
\begin{aligned}
|B(\textbf{x}^{(1)},\cdots,\textbf{x}^{(m)})|
&\leqslant\frac{\|P\|_{\mathcal{P}^m(Y)}}{2^m m!}
\sum\limits_{\epsilon\in\{-1,1\}^m}
\left\|\sum\limits_{j=1}^{m}\epsilon_j\textbf{x}^{(j)}\right\|_Y^m\leqslant\frac{m^m}{m!}\|P\|_{\mathcal{P}^m(Y)}.
\end{aligned}
$$
Hence
\begin{equation}
\|B\|_{\mathcal{L}^m(Y)}
\leqslant\frac{m^m}{m!}\|P\|_{\mathcal{P}^m(Y)}.
\label{shi-jihuafanshu}
\end{equation}
By \eqref{shi-duijiaoxishu}, \eqref{shi-duoxianxingguji}, and \eqref{shi-jihuafanshu},
$$
\begin{aligned}
\|P^{\#}\|_{\mathcal{P}^m(Y)}
&\leqslant\|B^{\#}\|_{\mathcal{L}^m(Y)}
\leqslant K_m\|B\|_{\mathcal{L}^m(Y)}\leqslant\frac{m^m}{m!}K_m\|P\|_{\mathcal{P}^m(Y)}
=C_m\|P\|_{\mathcal{P}^m(Y)}.
\end{aligned}
$$
This proves \eqref{shi-youxianxishuguji}. If $P\in\mathcal{P}^m(Y)$ , by \eqref{shi-youxianxishuguji} and $\|P\circ P_n\|\leqslant\|P\|$, it holds that
$$
\begin{aligned}
&\sum\limits_{\substack{|\alpha|=m\\
\mathrm{supp}\alpha\subset\{1,\cdots,n\}}}
|c_\alpha(P)\textbf{z}^\alpha|=
\sum\limits_{|\alpha|=m}
|c_\alpha(P\circ P_n)\textbf{z}^\alpha|=\left( P\circ P_{n} \right)^{\#} \left( \left| \textbf{z} \right| \right)
\leqslant C_m\|P\|\|\textbf{z}\|_Y^m.
\end{aligned}
$$
Letting $n\to\infty$ proves \eqref{shi-fanlixishuguji} with the same
explicit coefficient $C_m$.
<!-- end proof-22 -->
<!-- remark-4: body_md -->
The coefficients $C_m$ in \eqref{shi-duoxiangshichangshu} have at most
exponential growth. For $m\geqslant2$, $a_{m} > m^{2}-1$
gives $\kappa(m^2-1)\leqslant m-1$. By \eqref{shi-huanmancanshu},
\eqref{shi-huanmancanshujie}, and \eqref{shi-canshuyujibenhanshu},
$$
t_{m^2-1}\leqslant2t_{\kappa(m^2-1)}\leqslant2t_m
\leqslant\frac{4m}{\varphi(m)},
\qquad 1\leqslant \theta_{m} =1+\frac{\varphi \left( m \right)}{m} t_{m^{2}-1}\leqslant 1+\frac{\varphi \left( m \right)}{m} \frac{4m}{\varphi \left( m \right)} =5.
$$
Stirling's formula gives
\begin{equation}
	\begin{aligned}
		&\limsup\limits_{m\to \infty} C_{m}^{\frac{1}{m}}=\limsup\limits_{m\to \infty} \left[ \left( \frac{m^{m}}{m!} \right)^{\frac{3}{m}} \binom{3m-1}{m}^{\frac{1}{2m}} \theta_{m} \right]\\ &\  \leqslant 5\lim\limits_{m\to \infty} \left[ \left( \frac{m^{m}}{m!} \right)^{\frac{3}{m}} \binom{3m-1}{m}^{\frac{1}{2m}} \right] =\frac{15\sqrt{3}}{2} e^{3}\end{aligned}
	\label{shi-cishigenjie}
\end{equation}
<!-- end remark-4 -->
<!-- theorem-3: body_md -->
\label{dingli-fanliquanshoulian}
Let $Y$ be the complex Banach sequence space in Definition~\ref{dingyi-fanlikongjian-Y}.
Then

\begin{equation}
\mathrm{dom}H(O)=O
\qquad\text{for every Reinhardt domain }O\subset Y.
\label{shi-fanliquanyushoulian}
\end{equation}
For every $f\in H(O)$ and every $\textbf{z}\in O$,
\eqref{shi-juduishoulian} and \eqref{shi-hanshubiaoshi} hold.
In particular, $Y$ is a complex Banach sequence space
satisfying  \eqref{shi-fanliquanyushoulian}
which is not isomorphic to $\ell^1$.
<!-- end theorem-3 -->
<!-- theorem-4: body_md -->
\label{dingli-jingdianshoulian}
One has
\begin{equation}
\mathrm{dom}H(\ell^1)=\ell^1.
\label{shi-yikeheshoulian}
\end{equation}
For $1<p\leqslant2$,
\begin{equation}
\mathrm{dom}H(\ell^p)
=
\left\{\textbf{z}\in \ell^p:
\lim\limits_{n\to\infty}
\frac{\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}z_j^*}
{(\log(n+1))^{1-\frac{1}{p}}}=0\right\}.
\label{shi-xiaozhishushoulian}
\end{equation}
For $2<p<\infty$,
\begin{equation}
\mathrm{dom}H(\ell^p)
=
\left\{\textbf{z}\in \ell^p:
\lim\limits_{n\to\infty}
\frac{\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}(z_j^*)^{\frac{2p}{p+2}}}
{(\log(n+1))^{\frac{p}{p+2}}}=0\right\}.
\label{shi-dazhishushoulian}
\end{equation}
Moreover,
\begin{equation}
\mathrm{dom}H(c_0)
=
\left\{\textbf{z}\in c_0:
\lim\limits_{n\to\infty}
\frac{\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}(z_j^*)^2}{\log(n+1)}=0\right\}.
\label{shi-lingjixianshoulian}
\end{equation}
<!-- end theorem-4 -->
<!-- lemma-13: body_md -->
\label{yinli-chongpaixingzhi}
    For every $\textbf{z}\in c_0$ with $M(\textbf{z})<\infty$, one has
    $\textbf{z}\in X$ and
    \begin{equation}
        \|\textbf{z}\|_X\leqslant C M(\textbf{z}),
        \label{shi-chongpaiqianru}
    \end{equation}
    where $C\geqslant1$ depends only on $p$.
    Moreover, whenever $\textbf{z}\in c_0$ and $M(\textbf{z})<\infty$,
\begin{equation}
        M(\lambda\textbf{z})=|\lambda|M(\textbf{z}),\qquad
    M\bigl((z_{\pi(j)})_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\bigr)=M(\textbf{z}),\qquad
    M\bigl((\varepsilon_jz_j)_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\bigr)=M(\textbf{z}),\label{shi-chongpaiduichen}
\end{equation}
    for every $\lambda\in\mathbb{C}$, every bijection
    $\pi:\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{N}$, and every
    $(\varepsilon_j)_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\subset\mathbb{C}$ with
    $|\varepsilon_j|=1$ for all $j\in\mathbb{N}$. If
    $\textbf{w}\in c_0$ and $|\textbf{w}|\leqslant|\textbf{z}|$, then
    $M(\textbf{w})\leqslant M(\textbf{z})$.

    Furthermore, for every $\textbf{z}\in c_0$,
    \begin{equation}
        \lim\limits_{n\to\infty}
        \frac{\left(\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}(z_j^*)^q\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}}
        {(\log(n+1))^\sigma}=0
        \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
        \lim\limits_{N\to\infty}M(Q_N\textbf{z})=0.
        \label{shi-weibudengjia}
    \end{equation}
    Finally, for every $\textbf{z}\in c_0$, $N\in\mathbb{N}_0$, and
    $n\in\mathbb{N}$,
    \begin{equation}
        0\leqslant
        \left(\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}(z_j^*)^q\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}
        -\left(\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}
        \bigl((Q_N\textbf{z})_j^*\bigr)^q\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}
        \leqslant\|P_N\textbf{z}\|_{\ell^q}.
        \label{shi-shoubumodong}
    \end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-13 -->
<!-- proof-23: body_md -->
For every $\textbf{z}\in c_0$ and $n\in\mathbb{N}$,
    \begin{equation}
        \left(\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}(z_j^*)^q\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}
        =
        \sup\limits_{\substack{F\subset\mathbb{N}\\|F|=n}}
        \left(\sum\limits_{j\in F}|z_j|^q\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}.
        \label{shi-chongpaishangquejie}
    \end{equation}
    Hence \eqref{shi-chongpaishangquejie} yields \eqref{shi-chongpaiduichen}
    and
$
    |\textbf{w}|\leqslant|\textbf{z}|
    \Longrightarrow
    M(\textbf{w})\leqslant M(\textbf{z}).
$
    In particular,
    \begin{equation}
        M(P_N\textbf{z}),\ M(Q_N\textbf{z})
        \leqslant M(\textbf{z}).
        \label{shi-chongpaitouying}
    \end{equation}

    If $M(\textbf{z})<\infty$, then, for every $j\in\mathbb{N}$,
    $$
    j^{\frac{1}{q}}z_j^*
    \leqslant
    \left(\sum\limits_{k=1}^{j}(z_k^*)^q\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}
    \leqslant
    M(\textbf{z})(\log(j+1))^\sigma,
    $$
    and therefore
    \begin{equation}
        z_j^*\leqslant
        M(\textbf{z})
        \frac{(\log(j+1))^\sigma}{j^{\frac{1}{q}}}.
        \label{shi-chongpaidianjie}
    \end{equation}
    If $p<\infty$, then \eqref{shi-chongpaidianjie}
    and \eqref{shi-fenleizhishu} give
    $$
    \|\textbf{z}\|_X^p
    =
    \sum\limits_{j=1}^{\infty}(z_j^*)^p
    \leqslant
    M(\textbf{z})^p
    \sum\limits_{j=1}^{\infty}
    \frac{(\log(j+1))^{\sigma p}}{j^{\frac{p}{q}}}
    =
    M(\textbf{z})^p
    \sum\limits_{j=1}^{\infty}
    \frac{(\log(j+1))^{\sigma p}}{j^{1+\sigma p}}
    <\infty.
    $$
    Consequently,
    $$
    \|\textbf{z}\|_X
    \leqslant
    \left(
    \sum\limits_{j=1}^{\infty}
    \frac{(\log(j+1))^{\sigma p}}{j^{1+\sigma p}}
    \right)^{\frac{1}{p}}
    M(\textbf{z}).
    $$
    If $p=\infty$, then \eqref{shi-chongpaidianjie}
    gives
    $$
    \|\textbf{z}\|_X
    =z_1^*
    \leqslant
    (\log2)^\sigma M(\textbf{z})
    \leqslant M(\textbf{z}).
    $$
    Thus \eqref{shi-chongpaiqianru} follows.

    Let $N\in\mathbb{N}_0$ and $n\in\mathbb{N}$. For every
    $F\subset\mathbb{N}$ with $|F|=n$, Minkowski's inequality gives
    $$
    \left(\sum\limits_{j\in F}|z_j|^q\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}
    \leqslant
    \left(\sum\limits_{j\in F}|(P_N\textbf{z})_j|^q\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}
    +
    \left(\sum\limits_{j\in F}|(Q_N\textbf{z})_j|^q\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}
    $$
    and hence
    $$
    \left(\sum\limits_{j\in F}|z_j|^q\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}
    \leqslant
    \|P_N\textbf{z}\|_{\ell^q}
    +
    \left(\sum\limits_{j\in F}|(Q_N\textbf{z})_j|^q\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}.
    $$
    Taking the supremum over all such $F$ and using
    \eqref{shi-chongpaishangquejie}, we obtain
    $$
    \left(\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}(z_j^*)^q\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}
    \leqslant
    \|P_N\textbf{z}\|_{\ell^q}
    +
    \left(\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}
    \bigl((Q_N\textbf{z})_j^*\bigr)^q\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}.
    $$
    Moreover,
$
    |Q_N\textbf{z}|\leqslant|\textbf{z}|,
    $
    so \eqref{shi-chongpaishangquejie} gives
    $$
    \left(\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}
    \bigl((Q_N\textbf{z})_j^*\bigr)^q\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}
    \leqslant
    \left(\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}(z_j^*)^q\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}.
    $$
    Thus \eqref{shi-shoubumodong} follows.

    Suppose
$
    \lim\limits_{n\to\infty}
    \frac{\left(\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}(z_j^*)^q\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}}
    {(\log(n+1))^\sigma}
    =0.
$
    For every $\varepsilon>0$, there exists $n_0\geqslant2,n_0\in \mathbb{N}$ such that
    $$
    \frac{\left(\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}(z_j^*)^q\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}}
    {(\log(n+1))^\sigma}
    \leqslant\varepsilon,
    \qquad n\geqslant n_0.
    $$
    Since $|Q_N\textbf{z}|\leqslant|\textbf{z}|$,
    \eqref{shi-chongpaishangquejie} gives
    $$
    \frac{\left(\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}
        \bigl((Q_N\textbf{z})_j^*\bigr)^q\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}}
    {(\log(n+1))^\sigma}
    \leqslant\varepsilon,
    \qquad n\geqslant n_0.
    $$
    For $1\leqslant n<n_0$,
    $$
    \frac{\left(\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}
        \bigl((Q_N\textbf{z})_j^*\bigr)^q\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}}
    {(\log(n+1))^\sigma}
    \leqslant
    \frac{n^{\frac{1}{q}}}{(\log(n+1))^\sigma}
    \|Q_N\textbf{z}\|_{\ell^\infty}
    \leqslant
    \frac{n_0^{\frac{1}{q}}}{(\log2)^\sigma}
    \|Q_N\textbf{z}\|_{\ell^\infty}.
    $$
    Therefore
    $$
    M(Q_N\textbf{z})
    \leqslant
    \max\left\{
    \varepsilon,
    \frac{n_0^{\frac{1}{q}}}{(\log2)^\sigma}
    \|Q_N\textbf{z}\|_{\ell^\infty}
    \right\}.
    $$
    Since $\textbf{z}\in c_0$,
$
    \lim\limits_{N\to\infty}
    \|Q_N\textbf{z}\|_{\ell^\infty}=0,
$
    and hence
$
    \limsup\limits_{N\to\infty}M(Q_N\textbf{z})
    \leqslant\varepsilon.
$
    Letting $\varepsilon\to0$ gives
$
    \lim\limits_{N\to\infty}M(Q_N\textbf{z})=0.
$

Conversely, \eqref{shi-shoubumodong} gives, for every
    $N\in\mathbb{N}_0$ and $n\in\mathbb{N}$,
    $$
    \frac{\left(\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}(z_j^*)^q\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}}
    {(\log(n+1))^\sigma}
    \leqslant
    \frac{\|P_N\textbf{z}\|_{\ell^q}}{(\log(n+1))^\sigma}
    +
    \frac{\left(\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}
        \bigl((Q_N\textbf{z})_j^*\bigr)^q\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}}
    {(\log(n+1))^\sigma}.
    $$
    By \eqref{shi-chongpaoliang},
    $$
    \frac{\left(\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}(z_j^*)^q\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}}
    {(\log(n+1))^\sigma}
    \leqslant
    \frac{\|P_N\textbf{z}\|_{\ell^q}}{(\log(n+1))^\sigma}
    +
    M(Q_N\textbf{z}).
    $$
Therefore
    $$
    \limsup\limits_{n\to\infty}
    \frac{\left(\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}(z_j^*)^q\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}}
    {(\log(n+1))^\sigma}
    \leqslant M(Q_N\textbf{z}).
    $$
    Thus
$
    \lim\limits_{N\to\infty}M(Q_N\textbf{z})=0
$
    implies
    $$
    \lim\limits_{n\to\infty}
    \frac{\left(\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}(z_j^*)^q\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}}
    {(\log(n+1))^\sigma}=0.
    $$
    This proves \eqref{shi-weibudengjia}.
<!-- end proof-23 -->
<!-- lemma-14: body_md -->
\label{yinli-fenkuaiqiuhe}
For $X,p,q,\sigma$ as in \eqref{shi-fenleizhishu}, let
$C_0\geqslant1$ satisfy
\begin{equation}
\begin{aligned}
2^{\frac{1}{q}}(\log2)^\sigma&\leqslant C_0,
& e^2 2^{4\sigma+\frac{1}{p}}&\leqslant C_0,\\
u^\sigma(1+u)^{1-\frac{1}{q}}2^{-\frac{\sigma u}{2}}
&\leqslant C_0\quad(u\geqslant0),
&2^\sigma\max\left\{1,
\frac{2^{-\frac{\sigma}{2}}}{1-2^{-\frac{\sigma}{2}}}\right\}
&\leqslant C_0.
\end{aligned}
\label{shi-fuzhuchangshu}
\end{equation}
Let $1\leqslant k_0\leqslant K$ be integers and let
$\omega_{k_0},\cdots,\omega_K\geqslant0$ satisfy
\begin{equation}
\left(\sum\limits_{k=k_0}^{L}\omega_k^q\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}
\leqslant C_0L^\sigma,
\qquad k_0\leqslant L\leqslant K.
\label{shi-kuaibufenhe}
\end{equation}
Then, for every $d\in\mathbb{N}$ and $k_0\leqslant L\leqslant K$,
\begin{equation}
d^{\frac{d}{p}}
\sum\limits_{\substack{\beta_{k_0},\cdots,\beta_L\in\mathbb{N}_0\\
\beta_{k_0}+\cdots+\beta_L=d}}
\prod\limits_{k=k_0}^{L}\frac{\omega_k^{\beta_k}}{(\beta_k!)^{\frac{1}{q}}}
\leqslant(3C_0)^d\left(\frac{L}{d}\right)^{\sigma d}
\left(1+\frac{L}{d}\right)^{(1-\frac{1}{q})d}.
\label{shi-duocishuqiuhe}
\end{equation}
Moreover, for every $d\in\mathbb{N}$,
\begin{equation}
d^\sigma\sum\limits_{L=1}^{\infty}2^{-\sigma L}
\left(\frac{L}{d}\right)^{\sigma d}
\left(1+\frac{L}{d}\right)^{(1-\frac{1}{q})d}
\leqslant C_0^{2d}.
\label{shi-zhishishuaijian}
\end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-14 -->
<!-- proof-24: body_md -->
Fix $d\in\mathbb{N}$ and $k_0\leqslant L\leqslant K$. By \eqref{shi-fenleizhishu}, $1\leqslant q\leqslant2$ and $\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{q}=-\sigma<0$. We have
    \begin{equation}
        \#\left\{
        (\beta_{k_0},\cdots,\beta_L)\in\mathbb{N}_0^{L-k_0+1}:
        \sum\limits_{k=k_0}^{L}\beta_k=d
        \right\}
        =\binom{L-k_0+d}{d}\leqslant\binom{L+d}{d}.
        \label{shi-duocishujishu}
    \end{equation}
    By \eqref{shi-duocishujishu}, H\"older's inequality, and the multinomial theorem,
    \begin{equation}
        \begin{aligned}
            \sum\limits_{\substack{\beta_{k_0},\cdots,\beta_L\in\mathbb{N}_0\\
                    \beta_{k_0}+\cdots+\beta_L=d}}
            \prod\limits_{k=k_0}^{L}\frac{\omega_k^{\beta_k}}{(\beta_k!)^{\frac{1}{q}}}
            &\quad\leqslant
            \binom{L+d}{d}^{1-\frac{1}{q}}
            \left(
            \sum\limits_{\substack{\beta_{k_0},\cdots,\beta_L\in\mathbb{N}_0\\
                    \beta_{k_0}+\cdots+\beta_L=d}}
            \prod\limits_{k=k_0}^{L}\frac{\omega_k^{q\beta_k}}{\beta_k!}
            \right)^{\frac{1}{q}}\\
            &\quad=
            \binom{L+d}{d}^{1-\frac{1}{q}}
            \frac{\left(\sum\limits_{k=k_0}^{L}\omega_k^q\right)^{\frac{d}{q}}}
            {(d!)^{\frac{1}{q}}}.
        \end{aligned}
        \label{shi-duochongheguji}
    \end{equation}
    By \begin{equation}
  \begin{aligned}d!&=e^{\sum\limits_{k=1}^{d} \log k}\geqslant e^{\sum\limits_{k=2}^{d} \int\limits_{k-1}^{k} \log x\mathrm{d} x}=e^{d\log d-d+1}\geqslant e^{d\log d-\log 3\cdot d}=\left( \frac{d}{3} \right)^{d} ,\end{aligned}
    \end{equation}we have
    \begin{equation}
\binom{L+d}{d}\leqslant\frac{(L+d)^d}{d!}
\leqslant\left(3\left(1+\frac{L}{d}\right)\right)^d.
        \
        \label{shi-fenkuaijicheng}
    \end{equation}
    By \eqref{shi-duochongheguji}, \eqref{shi-fenkuaijicheng}, \eqref{shi-kuaibufenhe}, and \eqref{shi-fenleizhishu},
    $$
    \begin{aligned}
        &d^{\frac{d}{p}}
        \sum\limits_{\substack{\beta_{k_0},\cdots,\beta_L\in\mathbb{N}_0\\
                \beta_{k_0}+\cdots+\beta_L=d}}
        \prod\limits_{k=k_0}^{L}\frac{\omega_k^{\beta_k}}{(\beta_k!)^{\frac{1}{q}}}\leqslant
        d^{\frac{d}{p}}
        \left(3\left(1+\frac{L}{d}\right)\right)^{(1-\frac{1}{q})d}
        (C_0L^\sigma)^d\left(\frac{3}{d}\right)^{\frac{d}{q}}\\
        &\quad=
        (3C_0)^dL^{\sigma d}d^{(\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{q})d}
        \left(1+\frac{L}{d}\right)^{(1-\frac{1}{q})d}
        =
        (3C_0)^d\left(\frac{L}{d}\right)^{\sigma d}
        \left(1+\frac{L}{d}\right)^{(1-\frac{1}{q})d}.
    \end{aligned}
    $$
    This proves \eqref{shi-duocishuqiuhe}.

    Finally, $d\leqslant2^d$ and \eqref{shi-fuzhuchangshu} give
    $$
    \begin{aligned}
        &d^\sigma\sum\limits_{L=1}^{\infty}
        2^{-\sigma L}
        \left(\frac{L}{d}\right)^{\sigma d}
        \left(1+\frac{L}{d}\right)^{(1-\frac{1}{q})d}=
        d^\sigma\sum\limits_{L=1}^{\infty}2^{-\frac{\sigma L}{2}}
        \left[
        \left(\frac{L}{d}\right)^\sigma
        \left(1+\frac{L}{d}\right)^{1-\frac{1}{q}}
        2^{-\frac{\sigma L}{2d}}
        \right]^d\\
        &\quad\leqslant
        (2^\sigma C_0)^d
        \sum\limits_{L=1}^{\infty}2^{-\frac{\sigma L}{2}}=
        (2^\sigma C_0)^d
        \frac{2^{-\frac{\sigma}{2}}}{1-2^{-\frac{\sigma}{2}}}
\leqslant
        C_0^d\left[
        2^\sigma\max\left\{1,
        \frac{2^{-\frac{\sigma}{2}}}{1-2^{-\frac{\sigma}{2}}}
        \right\}
        \right]^d
        \leqslant C_0^{2d}.
    \end{aligned}
    $$
    This proves \eqref{shi-zhishishuaijian}.
<!-- end proof-24 -->
<!-- lemma-15: body_md -->
\label{yinli-chongpaixishuguji}
	For $X,p,q,\sigma$ as in \eqref{shi-fenleizhishu}, there exists
	a constant $C\geqslant1$, depending only on $p$, such that, for every
	$m\in\mathbb{N}$, every $G\in\mathcal{P}^m(X)$, and every
	$\textbf{z}\in c_0$ with $M(\textbf{z})<\infty$,
	\begin{equation}
		\sum\limits_{\substack{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_0^{(\mathbb{N})}\\
				|\alpha|=m}}
		|c_\alpha(G)\textbf{z}^\alpha|
		\leqslant
		C^m M(\textbf{z})^m\|G\|.
		\label{shi-chongpaixishuguji}
	\end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-15 -->
<!-- proof-25: body_md -->
Fix $p$ and choose $C_0\geqslant1$, depending only on $p$, such that \eqref{shi-fuzhuchangshu} holds and \eqref{shi-chongpaiqianru} holds with $C=C_0$. We first assume $G=G\circ P_n$. 

    \par\medskip\noindent\textbf{Step 1}\par\nopagebreak[4]
For $m\geqslant2$ and $\emptyset\neq A\subset\{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_0^{(\mathbb{N})}:|\alpha|=m,\ \mathrm{supp}\alpha\subset\{1,\cdots,n\}\}$, put
    \begin{equation}
        A^-\triangleq\{\alpha-e_{\max\mathrm{supp}\alpha}:\alpha\in A\}.
        \label{shi-shanjianzhiji}
    \end{equation}
    By \eqref{shi-duoxiangshifanshu} and \eqref{shi-shouweisuosuo}, $\|G\|=\sup\limits_{\|\textbf{u}\|_{\ell^p}\leqslant1}|G(\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}u_je_j)|$, where $\textbf{u}\in\mathbb{C}^n$. Moreover,
    \begin{equation}
        \begin{gathered}
            \alpha=\sum\limits_{\nu=1}^{m}e_{j_\nu}
            \quad\Longrightarrow\quad
            \alpha-e_{\max\mathrm{supp}\alpha}=\sum\limits_{\nu=1}^{m-1}e_{j_\nu},
            \qquad 1\leqslant j_1\leqslant\cdots\leqslant j_m\leqslant n,\\
            em\max\left\{e^{\frac{m-1}{p}},2^{\frac{m-1}{2}}\right\}
            \leqslant me^m\leqslant e^{2m}.
        \end{gathered}
        \label{shi-zhijiduiying}
    \end{equation}
    Thus \cite[Theorem~3.2]{BDS19} and \eqref{shi-zhijiduiying} give
    \begin{equation}
        \sum\limits_{\alpha\in A}|c_\alpha(G)\textbf{u}^\alpha|
        \leqslant e^{2m}|A^-|^\sigma\|\textbf{u}\|_X^m\|G\|,
        \qquad \textbf{u}\in X.
        \label{shi-zhijixishuguji}
    \end{equation}

    Take $\pi\in S_n$ with $|z_{\pi(1)}|\geqslant\cdots\geqslant|z_{\pi(n)}|$ and define
    \begin{equation}
        \begin{aligned}
            \varepsilon_j&\triangleq
            \begin{cases}
                \displaystyle\frac{\overline{z_{\pi(j)}}}{|z_{\pi(j)}|},&z_{\pi(j)}\neq0,\\
                1,&z_{\pi(j)}=0,
            \end{cases}
            \qquad 1\leqslant j\leqslant n,\\
            T\textbf{x}&\triangleq\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}\varepsilon_jx_{\pi(j)}e_j+Q_n\textbf{x},
            \qquad \textbf{x}\in X.
        \end{aligned}
        \label{shi-chongpaibianhuan}
    \end{equation}
    Then \eqref{shi-chongpaibianhuan} yields
    \begin{equation}
        \begin{gathered}
            T^{-1}\textbf{x}=\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}\overline{\varepsilon_j}x_je_{\pi(j)}+Q_n\textbf{x},
            \qquad \|T\textbf{x}\|_X=\|\textbf{x}\|_X,\\
            TP_n=P_nT,
            \qquad TP_n\textbf{z}=\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}|z_{\pi(j)}|e_j=(P_n\textbf{z})^*.
        \end{gathered}
        \label{shi-chongpaidengju}
    \end{equation}
    By \eqref{shi-chongpaidengju}, \eqref{shi-youxianzhankai}, \eqref{shi-chongpaiduichen}, and \eqref{shi-chongpaitouying},
    \begin{equation}
        \begin{gathered}
            \|G\circ T^{-1}\|=\|G\|,
            \qquad M(TP_n\textbf{z})=M(P_n\textbf{z})\leqslant M(\textbf{z}),\\
            \sum\limits_{|\alpha|=m}|c_\alpha(G)\textbf{z}^\alpha|
            =\sum\limits_{|\alpha|=m}|c_\alpha(G\circ T^{-1})(TP_n\textbf{z})^\alpha|.
        \end{gathered}
        \label{shi-xishuhebudong}
    \end{equation}
For proving \eqref{shi-chongpaixishuguji}, the cases
$G=0$ and $P_n\textbf{z}=\textbf{0}$ are immediate.
By \eqref{shi-xishuhebudong} and homogeneity,
it therefore suffices to assume
    \begin{equation}
        \|G\|=M(\textbf{z})=1,
        \qquad z_1\geqslant\cdots\geqslant z_n\geqslant0,
        \qquad z_j=0\quad(j>n).
        \label{shi-jingdianguifan}
    \end{equation}
    For $m=1$, \eqref{shi-youxianzhankai} and \eqref{shi-chongpaiqianru} give
    \begin{equation}
        \sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}|c_{e_j}(G)|z_j
        =\sup\limits_{|w_j|\leqslant z_j\ (j\in\mathbb{N})}|G(\textbf{w})|
        \leqslant\|G\|\|\textbf{z}\|_X\leqslant C_0.
        \label{shi-yicixishuguji}
    \end{equation}

    \par\medskip\noindent\textbf{Step 2}\par\nopagebreak[4]
Henceforth $m\geqslant2$. Put
    \begin{gather}
        k_0\triangleq\left\lceil\frac{\log m}{\log2}\right\rceil,
        \quad I_0\triangleq\{1,\cdots,2^{k_0}-1\},
        \quad I_k\triangleq\{2^k,\cdots,2^{k+1}-1\}\quad(k\geqslant k_0).
        \notag\\
        1\leqslant|I_0|<2m,
        \qquad |I_k|=2^k\geqslant m\quad(k\geqslant k_0).
        \label{shi-shuangjinfenkuai}
    \end{gather}
    Increasing $n$ if necessary, take $n=2^{K+1}-1$ with $K\geqslant k_0$. By \eqref{shi-fenleizhishu} and \eqref{shi-jingdianguifan},
    \begin{equation}
        \omega_k\triangleq2^{\sigma k}\|P_{I_k}\textbf{z}\|_X
        \leqslant2^{k(\sigma+\frac{1}{p})}z_{2^k}
        =2^{\frac{k}{q}}z_{2^k},
        \qquad k_0\leqslant k\leqslant K.
        \label{shi-kuaiquanzhong}
    \end{equation}
    For $k_0\leqslant L\leqslant K$, \eqref{shi-jingdianguifan}, \eqref{shi-kuaiquanzhong} and \eqref{shi-chongpaoliang} yield
    \begin{equation}
        \begin{aligned}
            \sum\limits_{k=k_0}^{L}\omega_k^q
            &\leqslant\sum\limits_{k=k_0}^{L}2^kz_{2^k}^q
            \leqslant2\sum\limits_{k=k_0}^{L}\sum\limits_{j=2^{k-1}}^{2^k-1}z_j^q\\
            &=2\sum\limits_{j=2^{k_0-1}}^{2^L-1}z_j^q
            \leqslant2\sum\limits_{j=1}^{2^L-1}z_j^q
            \leqslant2(L\log2)^{\sigma q}.
        \end{aligned}
        \label{shi-quanzhongbufenhe}
    \end{equation}
    By \eqref{shi-chongpaiqianru}, \eqref{shi-quanzhongbufenhe}, and \eqref{shi-fuzhuchangshu},
    \begin{equation}
        \|P_{I_0}\textbf{z}\|_X\leqslant C_0,
        \qquad
        \left(\sum\limits_{k=k_0}^{L}\omega_k^q\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}
        \leqslant2^{\frac{1}{q}}(\log2)^\sigma L^\sigma
        \leqslant C_0L^\sigma.
        \label{shi-kuaitongyijie}
    \end{equation}

    \par\medskip\noindent\textbf{Step 3}\par\nopagebreak[4]
For $\beta=(\beta_{k_0},\cdots,\beta_K)\in\mathbb{N}_0^{K-k_0+1}$, write $|\beta|\triangleq\sum\limits_{k=k_0}^{K}\beta_k$. For $h\in\mathbb{N}_0$ with $h+|\beta|=m$, set
    \begin{equation}
        A_{h,\beta}\triangleq\left\{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_0^{(\mathbb{N})}:
        \begin{gathered}
            |\alpha|=m,\quad\mathrm{supp}\alpha\subset\{1,\cdots,n\},\\
            \sum\limits_{j\in I_0}\alpha_j=h,\quad
            \sum\limits_{j\in I_k}\alpha_j=\beta_k\quad(k_0\leqslant k\leqslant K)
        \end{gathered}\right\}.
        \label{shi-fenkuaizhiji}
    \end{equation}
    By \eqref{shi-youxianzhankai} and \eqref{shi-fenkuaizhiji},
    \begin{equation}
        \sum\limits_{|\alpha|=m}|c_\alpha(G)\textbf{z}^\alpha|
        =\sum\limits_{h+|\beta|=m}\sum\limits_{\alpha\in A_{h,\beta}}
        |c_\alpha(G)\textbf{z}^\alpha|.
        \label{shi-xishuhefenjie}
    \end{equation}
    For $h<m$, put
    $$
        d\triangleq m-h=|\beta|\geqslant1,
        \qquad L\triangleq\max\{k:\beta_k>0\},
        \qquad t\triangleq\max I_L.
    $$
    For $\eta\in\mathbb{N}_0^{(\mathbb{N})}$, \eqref{shi-shanjianzhiji} and \eqref{shi-fenkuaizhiji} give
    \begin{equation}
        \begin{gathered}
            \alpha\in A_{h,\beta},\quad j=\max\mathrm{supp}\alpha
            \quad\Longrightarrow\quad j\in I_L,\quad\alpha-e_j+e_t\in A_{h,\beta},\\
            \eta+e_t\in A_{h,\beta}
            \quad\Longrightarrow\quad\max\mathrm{supp}(\eta+e_t)=t,\quad
            \eta=(\eta+e_t)-e_t\in A_{h,\beta}^-.
        \end{gathered}
        \label{shi-zuidazhibiao}
    \end{equation}
    Consequently, \eqref{shi-zuidazhibiao} gives
    \begin{equation}
        \begin{aligned}
            \eta\in A_{h,\beta}^-
            &\Longleftrightarrow\eta+e_t\in A_{h,\beta}\\
            &\Longleftrightarrow
            \begin{cases}
                \mathrm{supp}\eta\subset\{1,\cdots,t\},\\
                \sum\limits_{j\in I_0}\eta_j=h,\quad
                \sum\limits_{j\in I_L}\eta_j=\beta_L-1,\\
                \sum\limits_{j\in I_k}\eta_j=\beta_k\quad(k_0\leqslant k<L).
            \end{cases}
        \end{aligned}
        \label{shi-shanjianjikehua}
    \end{equation}
   By \eqref{shi-shanjianjikehua}, the coordinates of $\eta$ in the different blocks may be chosen independently. The numbers of nonnegative integer solutions on $I_0$, $I_k$ $(k_0\leqslant k<L)$, and $I_L$ are respectively
   $$
   \binom{|I_0|+h-1}{h},\qquad
   \binom{2^k+\beta_k-1}{\beta_k},\qquad
   \binom{2^L+\beta_L-2}{\beta_L-1}.
   $$
   Therefore,
   \begin{equation}
   	|A_{h,\beta}^-|
   	=\binom{|I_0|+h-1}{h}
   	\binom{2^L+\beta_L-2}{\beta_L-1}
   	\prod\limits_{k=k_0}^{L-1}
   	\binom{2^k+\beta_k-1}{\beta_k}.
   	\label{shi-shanjianjijishu}
   \end{equation}
    By \eqref{shi-shuangjinfenkuai},
    \begin{equation}
        \begin{aligned}
            \binom{|I_0|+h-1}{h}
            &\leqslant2^{|I_0|+h-1}\leqslant2^{3m},
            &&0\leqslant h\leqslant m,\\
            \binom{2^k+v-1}{v}
            &=\frac{\prod\limits_{\nu=0}^{v-1}(2^k+\nu)}{v!}
            \leqslant\frac{2^{(k+1)v}}{v!},
            &&k\geqslant k_0,\quad 0\leqslant v\leqslant m.
        \end{aligned}
        \label{shi-kuaizuhejie}
    \end{equation}
    Combining \eqref{shi-shanjianjijishu} and \eqref{shi-kuaizuhejie}, we obtain
    \begin{equation}
        \begin{aligned}
            |A_{h,\beta}^-|
            &\leqslant2^{3m}\frac{2^{(L+1)(\beta_L-1)}}{(\beta_L-1)!}
            \prod\limits_{k=k_0}^{L-1}\frac{2^{(k+1)\beta_k}}{\beta_k!}\\
            &=2^{3m+d-1}\frac{\beta_L}{2^L}
            \prod\limits_{k=k_0}^{L}\frac{2^{k\beta_k}}{\beta_k!}
            \leqslant2^{4m}\frac{\beta_L}{2^L}
            \prod\limits_{k=k_0}^{L}\frac{2^{k\beta_k}}{\beta_k!}.
        \end{aligned}
        \label{shi-shanjianjishangjie}
    \end{equation}

    \par\medskip\noindent\textbf{Step 4}\par\nopagebreak[4]
By \eqref{shi-fenkuaizhiji},
    \begin{equation}
        \|P_{I_0}\textbf{z}\|_X^h
        \prod\limits_{k=k_0}^{K}\|P_{I_k}\textbf{z}\|_X^{\beta_k}=0
        \quad\Longrightarrow\quad
        \sum\limits_{\alpha\in A_{h,\beta}}|c_\alpha(G)\textbf{z}^\alpha|=0.
        \label{shi-lingkuaichuli}
    \end{equation}
    Otherwise, define $\textbf{u}$ by
    \begin{equation}
        \begin{aligned}
            P_{I_0}\textbf{u}
            &\triangleq\left(\frac{h}{m}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}
            \frac{P_{I_0}\textbf{z}}{\|P_{I_0}\textbf{z}\|_X}
            &&\text{if }h>0,\\
            P_{I_k}\textbf{u}
            &\triangleq\left(\frac{\beta_k}{m}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}
            \frac{P_{I_k}\textbf{z}}{\|P_{I_k}\textbf{z}\|_X}
            &&\text{if }\beta_k>0,\quad k_0\leqslant k\leqslant K,
        \end{aligned}
        \label{shi-kuaiguifanhua}
    \end{equation}
    and set all remaining coordinates of $\textbf{u}$ equal to zero. By \eqref{shi-kuaiguifanhua},
    \begin{equation}
        \|\textbf{u}\|_X^p=\frac{h}{m}+\sum\limits_{k=k_0}^{K}\frac{\beta_k}{m}=1
        \quad(p<\infty),\qquad
        \|\textbf{u}\|_X=1\quad(p=\infty).
        \label{shi-kuaixiangliangfanshu}
    \end{equation}
    For $\alpha\in A_{h,\beta}$, \eqref{shi-fenkuaizhiji} and \eqref{shi-kuaiguifanhua} give
    \begin{equation}
        \textbf{z}^\alpha
        =\frac{m^{\frac{m}{p}}}{h^{\frac{h}{p}}}\|P_{I_0}\textbf{z}\|_X^h
        \prod\limits_{k=k_0}^{K}\frac{\|P_{I_k}\textbf{z}\|_X^{\beta_k}}{\beta_k^{\frac{\beta_k}{p}}}
        \,\textbf{u}^\alpha.
        \label{shi-kuaidanxiangshi}
    \end{equation}
    By \eqref{shi-zhijixishuguji}, \eqref{shi-kuaixiangliangfanshu}, and \eqref{shi-kuaidanxiangshi},
    \begin{equation}
        \begin{aligned}
            \sum\limits_{\alpha\in A_{h,\beta}}|c_\alpha(G)\textbf{z}^\alpha|
            &\leqslant e^{2m}|A_{h,\beta}^-|^\sigma
            \frac{m^{\frac{m}{p}}}{h^{\frac{h}{p}}}\|P_{I_0}\textbf{z}\|_X^h
            \prod\limits_{k=k_0}^{K}\frac{\|P_{I_k}\textbf{z}\|_X^{\beta_k}}{\beta_k^{\frac{\beta_k}{p}}}.
        \end{aligned}
        \label{shi-dankuaixishuguji}
    \end{equation}
    Since $h+d=m$, the identity $\max\limits_{0\leqslant u\leqslant1}\{-u\log u-(1-u)\log(1-u)\}=\log2$ gives
    \begin{equation}
        \frac{m^m}{h^hd^d}
        =e^{m\left(-\frac{h}{m}\log\frac{h}{m}-\frac{d}{m}\log\frac{d}{m}\right)}
        \leqslant2^m.
        \label{shi-eryuanshang}
    \end{equation}
    Also, $v!\leqslant v^v$ and \eqref{shi-fenleizhishu} give
    \begin{equation}
        \frac{1}{(v!)^\sigma v^{\frac{v}{p}}}
        \leqslant\frac{1}{(v!)^{\sigma+\frac{1}{p}}}
        =\frac{1}{(v!)^{\frac{1}{q}}},
        \qquad v\in\mathbb{N}_0.
        \label{shi-jichenghebing}
    \end{equation}
    Substitute \eqref{shi-shanjianjishangjie} and \eqref{shi-kuaiquanzhong} into \eqref{shi-dankuaixishuguji}. Using $\beta_L\leqslant d$, \eqref{shi-eryuanshang}, and \eqref{shi-jichenghebing}, we obtain
    \begin{equation}
        \begin{aligned}
            \sum\limits_{\alpha\in A_{h,\beta}}|c_\alpha(G)\textbf{z}^\alpha|
            &\leqslant e^{2m}2^{4\sigma m}\beta_L^\sigma2^{-\sigma L}
            \|P_{I_0}\textbf{z}\|_X^h
            \frac{m^{\frac{m}{p}}}{h^{\frac{h}{p}}}
            \prod\limits_{k=k_0}^{L}
            \frac{\omega_k^{\beta_k}}{(\beta_k!)^\sigma\beta_k^{\frac{\beta_k}{p}}}\\
            &\leqslant\left(e^2 2^{4\sigma+\frac{1}{p}}\right)^m
            d^\sigma2^{-\sigma L}\|P_{I_0}\textbf{z}\|_X^{m-d}d^{\frac{d}{p}}
            \prod\limits_{k=k_0}^{L}\frac{\omega_k^{\beta_k}}{(\beta_k!)^{\frac{1}{q}}}\\
            &\leqslant C_0^m d^\sigma2^{-\sigma L}
            \|P_{I_0}\textbf{z}\|_X^{m-d}d^{\frac{d}{p}}
            \prod\limits_{k=k_0}^{L}\frac{\omega_k^{\beta_k}}{(\beta_k!)^{\frac{1}{q}}},
        \end{aligned}
        \label{shi-kuaishuaijianguji}
    \end{equation}
    where the last inequality follows from \eqref{shi-fuzhuchangshu}. By \eqref{shi-lingkuaichuli}, \eqref{shi-kuaishuaijianguji} also holds if $h>0$ and $\| P_{I_0}\textbf z\|_X=0$, or if $\beta_k>0$ and $\| P_{I_k}\textbf z\|_X=0$ for some $k_0\leqslant k\leqslant L$.

    \par\medskip\noindent\textbf{Step 5}\par\nopagebreak[4]
By \eqref{shi-kuaitongyijie}, Lemma~\ref{yinli-fenkuaiqiuhe} applies to $(\omega_k)_{k=k_0}^{K}$. For $1\leqslant d\leqslant m$, \eqref{shi-kuaishuaijianguji} gives
    \begin{equation}
        \begin{aligned}
            \sum\limits_{|\beta|=d}\sum\limits_{\alpha\in A_{m-d,\beta}}
            |c_\alpha(G)\textbf{z}^\alpha|
            &=\sum\limits_{L=k_0}^{K}
            \sum\limits_{\substack{|\beta|=d\\\max\{k:\beta_k>0\}=L}}
            \sum\limits_{\alpha\in A_{m-d,\beta}}|c_\alpha(G)\textbf{z}^\alpha|\\
            &\leqslant C_0^m\|P_{I_0}\textbf{z}\|_X^{m-d}d^\sigma
            \sum\limits_{L=k_0}^{K}2^{-\sigma L}d^{\frac{d}{p}}
            \sum\limits_{\substack{\beta_{k_0},\cdots,\beta_L\in\mathbb{N}_0\\
                    \beta_{k_0}+\cdots+\beta_L=d}}
            \prod\limits_{k=k_0}^{L}\frac{\omega_k^{\beta_k}}{(\beta_k!)^{\frac{1}{q}}}\\
            &\leqslant C_0^m\|P_{I_0}\textbf{z}\|_X^{m-d}(3C_0)^d d^\sigma
            \sum\limits_{L=k_0}^{K}2^{-\sigma L}
            \left(\frac{L}{d}\right)^{\sigma d}
            \left(1+\frac{L}{d}\right)^{(1-\frac{1}{q})d}\\
            &\leqslant3^dC_0^{2m}d^\sigma\sum\limits_{L=1}^{\infty}2^{-\sigma L}
            \left(\frac{L}{d}\right)^{\sigma d}
            \left(1+\frac{L}{d}\right)^{(1-\frac{1}{q})d}\\
            &\leqslant3^dC_0^{2m+2d},
        \end{aligned}
        \label{shi-tongciweikuaiqiuhe}
    \end{equation}
    by \eqref{shi-duocishuqiuhe}, \eqref{shi-kuaitongyijie}, and \eqref{shi-zhishishuaijian}.

    For $d=0$, $\beta=0$. By \eqref{shi-shanjianzhiji}, \eqref{shi-fenkuaizhiji}, and \eqref{shi-shuangjinfenkuai},
    \begin{equation}
        \begin{aligned}
            A_{m,0}^-&=\{\eta\in\mathbb{N}_0^{(\mathbb{N})}:|\eta|=m-1,\ \mathrm{supp}\eta\subset I_0\},\\
            |A_{m,0}^-|&=\binom{|I_0|+m-2}{m-1}
            \leqslant2^{|I_0|+m-2}\leqslant2^{3m}.
        \end{aligned}
        \label{shi-shoukuaizhijishu}
    \end{equation}
    Apply \eqref{shi-zhijixishuguji} with $\textbf{u}=P_{I_0}\textbf{z}$. By \eqref{shi-shoukuaizhijishu}, \eqref{shi-kuaitongyijie}, and \eqref{shi-fuzhuchangshu},
    \begin{equation}
        \begin{aligned}
            \sum\limits_{\alpha\in A_{m,0}}|c_\alpha(G)\textbf{z}^\alpha|
            &\leqslant e^{2m}|A_{m,0}^-|^\sigma\|P_{I_0}\textbf{z}\|_X^m\|G\|\leqslant e^{2m}2^{3\sigma m}C_0^m\leqslant C_0^{2m}.
        \end{aligned}
        \label{shi-shoukuaixishuguji}
    \end{equation}
    Combining \eqref{shi-xishuhefenjie}, \eqref{shi-tongciweikuaiqiuhe}, and \eqref{shi-shoukuaixishuguji}, we obtain
    \begin{equation}
        \begin{aligned}
            \sum\limits_{|\alpha|=m}|c_\alpha(G)\textbf{z}^\alpha|
            &\leqslant C_0^{2m}+\sum\limits_{d=1}^{m}3^dC_0^{2m+2d}\leqslant(m+1)3^mC_0^{4m}\leqslant(6C_0^4)^m.
        \end{aligned}
        \label{shi-jingdianqiuhe}
    \end{equation}
    Put $C\triangleq6C_0^4$. By \eqref{shi-xishuhebudong}, \eqref{shi-yicixishuguji}, \eqref{shi-jingdianqiuhe}, and homogeneity, for every $m,n\in\mathbb{N}$ and $G=G\circ P_n$,
    \begin{equation}
        \sum\limits_{|\alpha|=m}|c_\alpha(G)\textbf{z}^\alpha|
        \leqslant C^mM(P_n\textbf{z})^m\|G\|
        \leqslant C^mM(\textbf{z})^m\|G\|.
        \label{shi-youxianchongpaiguji}
    \end{equation}
    Finally, for arbitrary $G\in\mathcal{P}^m(X)$, \eqref{shi-shouweisuosuo}, and \eqref{shi-youxianchongpaiguji} yield
    $$
    \begin{aligned}
        \sum\limits_{|\alpha|=m}|c_\alpha(G)\textbf{z}^\alpha|
        &=\lim\limits_{n\to\infty}
        \sum\limits_{\substack{|\alpha|=m\\\mathrm{supp}\alpha\subset\{1,\cdots,n\}}}
        |c_\alpha(G)\textbf{z}^\alpha|=\lim\limits_{n\to\infty}
        \sum\limits_{|\alpha|=m}|c_\alpha(G\circ P_n)\textbf{z}^\alpha|\\
        &\leqslant C^mM(\textbf{z})^m\sup\limits_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\|G\circ P_n\|
        \leqslant C^mM(\textbf{z})^m\|G\|.
    \end{aligned}
    $$
    This proves \eqref{shi-chongpaixishuguji}.
<!-- end proof-25 -->
<!-- lemma-16: body_md -->
\label{yinli-duoxiangshiwang}
    Let $n,m\in\mathbb{N}$ with $m\geqslant2$. There exists a finite set
    $\mathcal{N}\subset\{\textbf{u}\in\mathbb{C}^n:
    \|\textbf{u}\|_{\ell^2}\leqslant1\}$ with
    $|\mathcal{N}|\leqslant(1+12m)^{2n}$ such that
    $$
    \sup\limits_{\|\textbf{u}\|_{\ell^2}\leqslant1}|P(\textbf{u})|
    \leqslant2\max\limits_{\textbf{u}\in\mathcal{N}}|P(\textbf{u})|
    $$
    for every $m$-homogeneous polynomial $P$ on $\mathbb{C}^n$.
<!-- end lemma-16 -->
<!-- proof-26: body_md -->
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{Step 1}\par\nopagebreak[4]

    Set $\delta=\frac{1}{6m}$. For $\textbf{a}\in\mathbb{C}^n$
    and $r>0$, write
    $$
    B(\textbf{a},r)
    \triangleq
    \{\textbf{w}\in\mathbb{C}^n:
    \|\textbf{w}-\textbf{a}\|_{\ell^2}<r\}.
    $$
    Consider the largest $q\in\mathbb{N}$ for which there exist
    $\textbf{u}^{(1)},\cdots,\textbf{u}^{(q)}\in\mathbb{C}^n$
    such that
    $$
    \|\textbf{u}^{(j)}\|_{\ell^2}\leqslant1,
    \qquad 1\leqslant j\leqslant q,
    $$
    and
    $$
    \|\textbf{u}^{(i)}-\textbf{u}^{(j)}\|_{\ell^2}>\delta,
    \qquad 1\leqslant i<j\leqslant q.
    $$
    The balls
    $B(\textbf{u}^{(j)},\frac{\delta}{2})$,
    $1\leqslant j\leqslant q$, are pairwise disjoint. For
    $\textbf{w}\in B(\textbf{u}^{(j)},\frac{\delta}{2})$,
    $$
    \|\textbf{w}\|_{\ell^2}
    \leqslant
    \|\textbf{w}-\textbf{u}^{(j)}\|_{\ell^2}
    +\|\textbf{u}^{(j)}\|_{\ell^2}
    <\frac{\delta}{2}+1.
    $$
    Thus
    $$
    \bigcup\limits_{j=1}^q
    B\left(\textbf{u}^{(j)},\frac{\delta}{2}\right)
    \subset
    B\left(\textbf{0},1+\frac{\delta}{2}\right).
    $$

    Let $\mathrm{vol}_{2n}$ denote real $2n$-dimensional
    Lebesgue measure on $\mathbb{C}^n$. We have
    $$
    \begin{aligned}
        &q\left(\frac{\delta}{2}\right)^{2n}
        \mathrm{vol}_{2n}(B(\textbf{0},1))=
        \sum\limits_{j=1}^q
        \mathrm{vol}_{2n}
        \left(B\left(\textbf{u}^{(j)},\frac{\delta}{2}\right)\right)\\
        &\quad=
        \mathrm{vol}_{2n}
        \left(
        \bigcup\limits_{j=1}^q
        B\left(\textbf{u}^{(j)},\frac{\delta}{2}\right)
        \right)\leqslant
        \mathrm{vol}_{2n}
        \left(B\left(\textbf{0},1+\frac{\delta}{2}\right)\right)\\
        &\quad=
        \left(1+\frac{\delta}{2}\right)^{2n}
        \mathrm{vol}_{2n}(B(\textbf{0},1)).
    \end{aligned}
    $$

    We conclude that
    $$
    q
    \leqslant
    \left(
    \frac{1+\frac{\delta}{2}}{\frac{\delta}{2}}
    \right)^{2n}
    =\left(1+\frac{2}{\delta}\right)^{2n}
    =(1+12m)^{2n}.
    $$

    Let $$\mathcal{N} \triangleq \left\{ \textbf{u}^{(1)} ,\cdots ,\textbf{u}^{(q)} \right\}.$$In particular,
    $$
    |\mathcal{N}|\leqslant(1+12m)^{2n}.
    $$

    \par\medskip\noindent\textbf{Step 2}\par\nopagebreak[4]

    Let $P$ be any $m$-homogeneous polynomial on $\mathbb{C}^n$, and put
$
    K\triangleq
    \sup\limits_{\|\textbf{u}\|_{\ell^2}\leqslant1}|P(\textbf{u})|<\infty.
$   Fix $\textbf{u},\textbf{v}\in\mathbb{C}^n$ with
    $\|\textbf{u}\|_{\ell^2}\leqslant1$ and
    $\|\textbf{v}\|_{\ell^2}=1$. For every
    $\lambda\in\mathbb{C}$ with $|\lambda|=\frac{1}{m}$,
    $$
    \left\|
    \frac{\textbf{u}+\lambda\textbf{v}}{1+\frac{1}{m}}
    \right\|_{\ell^2}
    \leqslant
    \frac{\|\textbf{u}\|_{\ell^2}
        +|\lambda|\|\textbf{v}\|_{\ell^2}}
    {1+\frac{1}{m}}
    \leqslant1.
    $$
    Consequently, the homogeneity of $P$ gives
    $$
    \begin{aligned}
        |P(\textbf{u}+\lambda\textbf{v})|
        &=\left(1+\frac{1}{m}\right)^m
        \left|
        P\left(
        \frac{\textbf{u}+\lambda\textbf{v}}{1+\frac{1}{m}}
        \right)
        \right|\leqslant\left(1+\frac{1}{m}\right)^mK.
    \end{aligned}
    $$
    Applying the Cauchy formula to the entire function
    $\lambda\mapsto P(\textbf{u}+\lambda\textbf{v})$, we obtain
    $$
    DP(\textbf{u})[\textbf{v}]
    =\frac{1}{2\pi \mathrm{i}}
    \int\limits_{|\lambda|=\frac{1}{m}}
    \frac{P(\textbf{u}+\lambda\textbf{v})}{\lambda^2}
    \,\mathrm{d}\lambda,
    $$
 Hence
    $$
    \begin{aligned}
    &|DP(\textbf{u})[\textbf{v}]|
        \leqslant
        \frac{1}{2\pi}
        \int\limits_{|\lambda|=\frac{1}{m}}
        \frac{|P(\textbf{u}+\lambda\textbf{v})|}{|\lambda|^2}
        \,|\mathrm{d}\lambda|\\
        &\quad \leqslant
        \frac{1}{2\pi}\frac{2\pi}{m}\,
        m^2\left(1+\frac{1}{m}\right)^mK=m\left(1+\frac{1}{m}\right)^mK
        \leqslant3mK.
    \end{aligned}
    $$
 By the linearity of
    $DP(\textbf{u})$, for every nonzero $\textbf{h}\in\mathbb{C}^n$,
    $$
    \begin{aligned}
        |DP(\textbf{u})[\textbf{h}]|
        &=\|\textbf{h}\|_{\ell^2}
        \left|
        DP(\textbf{u})
        \left[
        \frac{\textbf{h}}{\|\textbf{h}\|_{\ell^2}}
        \right]
        \right|\leqslant3mK\|\textbf{h}\|_{\ell^2}.
    \end{aligned}
    $$
    The same inequality holds for $\textbf{h}=\textbf{0}$.

    Now let $\textbf{u},\textbf{v}\in\mathbb{C}^n$ satisfy
    $\|\textbf{u}\|_{\ell^2},\|\textbf{v}\|_{\ell^2}\leqslant1$, we have
    \begin{equation}
        \begin{aligned}
            |P(\textbf{u})-P(\textbf{v})|
            &\leqslant 3mK\|\textbf{u}-\textbf{v}\|_{\ell^2}.
        \end{aligned}
        \label{shi-duoxiangshicha}
    \end{equation}

    Hence, for every
$\textbf{u}\in\mathbb{C}^n$ with
$\|\textbf{u}\|_{\ell^2}\leqslant1$, there exists
$\textbf{v}\in\mathcal{N}$ such that
$\|\textbf{u}-\textbf{v}\|_{\ell^2}\leqslant\delta$.
    \eqref{shi-duoxiangshicha} yields
    $$
    \begin{aligned}
        |P(\textbf{u})|
        &\leqslant
        |P(\textbf{v})|+|P(\textbf{u})-P(\textbf{v})|\\
        &\leqslant
        \max\limits_{\textbf{w}\in\mathcal{N}}|P(\textbf{w})|
        +3mK\|\textbf{u}-\textbf{v}\|_{\ell^2}\\
        &\leqslant
        \max\limits_{\textbf{w}\in\mathcal{N}}|P(\textbf{w})|
        +3mK\delta\\
        &=
        \max\limits_{\textbf{w}\in\mathcal{N}}|P(\textbf{w})|
        +\frac{K}{2}.
    \end{aligned}
    $$
    Taking the supremum over
    $\|\textbf{u}\|_{\ell^2}\leqslant1$, we obtain
    $$
    K\leqslant
    \max\limits_{\textbf{w}\in\mathcal{N}}|P(\textbf{w})|
    +\frac{K}{2},
    $$
    and therefore
    \begin{equation}
        K\leqslant
        2\max\limits_{\textbf{u}\in\mathcal{N}}|P(\textbf{u})|.
        \label{shi-duoxiangshiwangjie}
    \qedhere
    \end{equation}
<!-- end proof-26 -->
<!-- lemma-17: body_md -->
\label{yinli-suijiduoxiangshi}
Let $n,m\in\mathbb{N}$ with $m\geqslant2$. There are 
$\left( \varepsilon_{\alpha} \right)_{\alpha \in \mathbb{N}_{0}^{n} ,\left| \alpha \right| =m} \in \left\{ -1,1 \right\}^{\left| \left\{ \alpha \in \mathbb{N}_{0}^{n}:\left| \alpha \right| =m \right\} \right|}$ such that the polynomial
$$
R(\textbf{u})\triangleq
\sum\limits_{\substack{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_0^n\\|\alpha|=m}}
\varepsilon_\alpha\frac{m!}{\alpha!}\textbf{u}^\alpha
$$
satisfies, simultaneously for $1\leqslant r\leqslant2$,
\begin{equation}
\sup\limits_{\|\textbf{u}\|_{\ell^r}\leqslant1}|R(\textbf{u})|
\leqslant16^{2(1-\frac{1}{r})}
(nm!\log m)^{1-\frac{1}{r}}.
\label{shi-suijiduoxiangshiguji}
\end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-17 -->
<!-- proof-27: body_md -->
Choose $\mathcal{N}$ as in
Lemma~\ref{yinli-duoxiangshiwang}.

\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{Step 1}\par\nopagebreak[4]

Let $(\varepsilon_\alpha)_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_0^n,\ |\alpha|=m}$
be a family of independent Rademacher random variables; that is,
$$
\mathbb{P}(\varepsilon_\alpha=1)
=
\mathbb{P}(\varepsilon_\alpha=-1)
=
\frac{1}{2}
$$
for every $\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_0^n$ with $|\alpha|=m$. For a fixed
$\|\textbf{u}\|_{\ell^2}\leqslant1$, put
$b_\alpha=\frac{m!}{\alpha!}\textbf{u}^\alpha$.
Since $\frac{m!}{\alpha!}\leqslant m!$,
\begin{equation}
\sum\limits_{|\alpha|=m}|b_\alpha|^2
\leqslant m!\sum\limits_{|\alpha|=m}
\frac{m!}{\alpha!}|\textbf{u}|^{2\alpha}
=m!\left(\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}|u_j|^2\right)^m\leqslant m!.
\label{shi-suijifangcha}
\end{equation}
Let $a_\alpha\in\mathbb{R}$, $|\alpha|=m$. For $|\alpha|=m$, set
$
X_\alpha\triangleq\varepsilon_\alpha a_\alpha.
$
Then the random variables $X_\alpha$ are independent and satisfy
$$
\mathbb{E}X_\alpha=0,
\qquad
-|a_\alpha|\leqslant X_\alpha\leqslant|a_\alpha|.
$$
Since there are only finitely many multi-indices $\alpha$ with
$|\alpha|=m$, \cite[Theorem~2, p.~16]{Hoeffding63} applies and gives,
for every $t>0$,
$$
\begin{aligned}
    \mathbb{P}\left(
    \sum\limits_{|\alpha|=m}\varepsilon_\alpha a_\alpha>t
    \right)
    &\leqslant
    e^{
    -\frac{2t^2}
    {\sum\limits_{|\alpha|=m}(2|a_\alpha|)^2}
    }=
    e^{
    -\frac{t^2}
    {2\sum\limits_{|\alpha|=m}a_\alpha^2}
    }.
\end{aligned}
$$
Applying the same estimate to the independent random variables
$-X_\alpha$ yields
$$
\mathbb{P}\left(
\sum\limits_{|\alpha|=m}\varepsilon_\alpha a_\alpha<-t
\right)
\leqslant
e^{
-\frac{t^2}
