{2\sum\limits_{|\alpha|=m}a_\alpha^2}
}.
$$
Hence
\begin{equation}
    \mathbb{P}\left(
    \left|\sum\limits_{|\alpha|=m}
    \varepsilon_\alpha a_\alpha\right|>t
    \right)
    \leqslant
    2e^{
    -\frac{t^2}
    {2\sum\limits_{|\alpha|=m}a_\alpha^2}
    }.
    \label{shi-suijiweigailv}
\end{equation}
Applying \eqref{shi-suijiweigailv} with
$a_\alpha=\mathrm{Re}b_\alpha$ and with
$a_\alpha=\mathrm{Im}b_\alpha$, respectively, and using
\eqref{shi-suijifangcha}, yields
\begin{equation}
	\begin{aligned}
		\mathbb{P}\bigl(|R(\textbf{u})|>t\bigr)
		&\leqslant
		\mathbb{P}\left(
		\left|\mathrm{Re}R(\textbf{u})\right|
		>\frac{t}{\sqrt{2}}
		\right)
		+
		\mathbb{P}\left(
		\left|\mathrm{Im}R(\textbf{u})\right|
		>\frac{t}{\sqrt{2}}
		\right)
		\\
		&\leqslant
		2e^{
			-\frac{(\frac{t}{\sqrt{2}})^2}
			{2\sum\limits_{\left| \alpha \right| =m}(\mathrm{Re}b_\alpha)^2}
		}
		+
		2e^{
			-\frac{(\frac{t}{\sqrt{2}})^2}
			{2\sum\limits_{\left| \alpha \right| =m}(\mathrm{Im}b_\alpha)^2}
		}
		\\
		&\leqslant
		2e^{
			-\frac{t^2}{4\sum\limits_{\left| \alpha \right| =m}|b_\alpha|^2}
		}
		+
		2e^{
			-\frac{t^2}{4\sum\limits_{\left| \alpha \right| =m}|b_\alpha|^2}
		}
		\\
		&\leqslant
		4e^{-\frac{t^2}{4m!}}.
	\end{aligned}\label{shi-cheche}
\end{equation}

Set
$$
t=2\sqrt{m!\bigl(\log8+2n\log(1+12m)\bigr)}.
$$
$\left| \mathcal{N} \right| \leqslant \left( 1+12m \right)^{2n}$ shows that
$$
\mathbb{P}\left(\max\limits_{\textbf{u}\in\mathcal{N}}|R(\textbf{u})|>t\right)
\leqslant4(1+12m)^{2n}e^{-\frac{t^2}{4m!}}
=\frac{1}{2}.
$$
Consequently, there is a choice of signs for which
\eqref{shi-duoxiangshiwangjie} gives
\begin{equation}
    \sup\limits_{\|\textbf{u}\|_{\ell^2}\leqslant1}|R(\textbf{u})|
    \leqslant4\sqrt{m!\bigl(\log8+2n\log(1+12m)\bigr)}
    \leqslant B\triangleq 16\sqrt{nm!\log m}.\label{shi-suijierfanshu}
\end{equation}
For the last inequality, $m\geqslant2$ gives
$1+12m\leqslant m^5$ and $\log8\leqslant3n\log m$;
thus the quantity inside the square root is at most $13nm!\log m$,
and $4\sqrt{13}<16$.

\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{Step 2}\par\nopagebreak[4]

For the same choice of signs, the multinomial theorem gives
$$
|R(\textbf{u})|
\leqslant
\sum\limits_{|\alpha|=m}\frac{m!}{\alpha!}|\textbf{u}^{\alpha}|
=
\left(\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}|u_j|\right)^m,
$$
and hence
$$
\sup\limits_{\|\textbf{u}\|_{\ell^1}\leqslant1}|R(\textbf{u})|
\leqslant1.
$$
Together with the estimate \eqref{shi-suijierfanshu}, this proves
\eqref{shi-suijiduoxiangshiguji} for $r=1$ and $r=2$.

Now let $1<r<2$ and set
$
\theta=2\left(1-\frac{1}{r}\right)\in(0,1).
$
Fix $\textbf{u}\in\mathbb{C}^n$ with $\|\textbf{u}\|_{\ell^r}=1$, and define
$$
\textbf{v}(w)=\bigl(v_1(w),\cdots,v_n(w)\bigr),
\qquad
0\leqslant\mathrm{Re}w\leqslant1,
$$
where
$$
v_j(w)=
\begin{cases}
    \displaystyle
    \frac{u_j}{|u_j|}
    e^{r\left(1-\frac{w}{2}\right)\log|u_j|},
    & u_j\neq0,\\[1ex]
    0,&u_j=0.
\end{cases}
$$
Since $r(1-\frac{\theta}{2})=1$, we have
$\textbf{v}(\theta)=\textbf{u}$. Moreover, for every $t\in\mathbb{R}$,
$$
\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}|v_j(\mathrm{i}t)|
=
\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}|u_j|^r
=
1,
\qquad
\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}|v_j(1+\mathrm{i}t)|^2
=
\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}|u_j|^r
=
1.
$$
By the cases $r=1$ and $r=2$ of
\eqref{shi-suijiduoxiangshiguji}, it follows that
$$
\sup\limits_{t\in\mathbb{R}}
|R(\textbf{v}(\mathrm{i}t))|
\leqslant1,
\qquad
\sup\limits_{t\in\mathbb{R}}
|R(\textbf{v}(1+\mathrm{i}t))|
\leqslant B.
$$
Since $\|\textbf{u}\|_{\ell^r}=1$, we have $|u_j|\leqslant1$ for
$1\leqslant j\leqslant n$, and therefore
$|v_j(w)|\leqslant1$ whenever
$0\leqslant\mathrm{Re}w\leqslant1$. Thus the function
$w\mapsto R(\textbf{v}(w))$ is bounded and continuous on the closed
strip $0\leqslant\mathrm{Re}w\leqslant1$ and holomorphic in its
interior. By Hadamard's three-lines theorem
\cite[Theorem~12.8, p.~257]{Rudin87},
$$
|R(\textbf{u})|
=
|R(\textbf{v}(\theta))|
\leqslant
B^\theta
=
16^{2(1-\frac{1}{r})}
(nm!\log m)^{1-\frac{1}{r}}.
$$
Since $\textbf{u}$ was arbitrary on the $\ell^r$ unit sphere, the
$m$-homogeneity of $R$ yields
$$
\sup\limits_{\|\textbf{u}\|_{\ell^r}\leqslant1}|R(\textbf{u})|
\leqslant
16^{2(1-\frac{1}{r})}
(nm!\log m)^{1-\frac{1}{r}}.
$$
Hence \eqref{shi-suijiduoxiangshiguji} holds for every
$1\leqslant r\leqslant2$.
<!-- end proof-27 -->
<!-- lemma-18: body_md -->
\label{yinli-shangjixianbijiao}
Fix $1<p\leqslant\infty$, $\textbf{z}\in X$, and
$N\in\mathbb{N}_0$. Suppose there exist $A,R>0$ such that
\begin{equation}
\sum\limits_{|\alpha|=m}|c_\alpha(G)\textbf{z}^\alpha|
\leqslant AR^m\|G\|
\label{shi-weibuzhishijiading}
\end{equation}
for every $m\geqslant2$ and every $G\in\mathcal{P}^m(X)$ depending only on finitely many coordinates $x_j$ with $j>N$.
Then
\begin{equation}
\limsup\limits_{n\to\infty}
\frac{\left(\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}
\bigl((Q_N\textbf{z})_j^*\bigr)^{q}\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}}
{(\log(n+1))^{\sigma}}
\leqslant R.
\label{shi-weibushangjixian}
\end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-18 -->
<!-- proof-28: body_md -->
Put $C\triangleq16$, $r\triangleq\min\{p,2\}$, and $\frac{1}{s}\triangleq\frac{1}{r}-\frac{1}{p}$, with $s=\infty$ when $p=r$. By \eqref{shi-fenleizhishu}, $0<\sigma=1-\frac{1}{r}\leqslant\frac{1}{2}$, $\frac{1}{r}=\frac{1}{s}+\frac{1}{p}$, and $\frac{1}{q}+\frac{1}{s}=1$. Fix $n,m\in\mathbb{N}$ with $m\geqslant2$. By \eqref{shi-suijiduoxiangshiguji} in Lemma~\ref{yinli-suijiduoxiangshi}, there exists a family
$
	(\varepsilon_\alpha)_{\substack{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_0^n\\|\alpha|=m}}
	\in
	\{-1,1\}^{\{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_0^n:\,|\alpha|=m\}}
$
	such that
	\begin{equation}
		\sup\limits_{\|\textbf{u}\|_{\ell^r}\leqslant1}
		\left|\sum\limits_{\substack{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_0^n\\|\alpha|=m}}
		\varepsilon_\alpha\frac{m!}{\alpha!}\textbf{u}^\alpha\right|
		\leqslant16^{2\sigma}(nm!\log m)^\sigma
		\leqslant C(nm!\log m)^\sigma.
		\label{shi-suijicanshuguji}
	\end{equation}
	Choose $N<j_1<\cdots<j_n$, put $J\triangleq\{j_1,\cdots,j_n\}$, and let $\textbf{a}=(a_1,\cdots,a_n)\in\mathbb{C}^n$ satisfy $\|\textbf{a}\|_{\ell^s}\leqslant1$. H\"older's inequality, including the cases $s=\infty$ and $p=\infty$, gives
	$$
	\left\|(a_kx_{j_k})_{k=1}^n\right\|_{\ell^r}
	\leqslant\|\textbf{a}\|_{\ell^s}
	\left\|(x_{j_k})_{k=1}^n\right\|_{\ell^p}
	\leqslant\|\textbf{x}\|_X,
	\qquad \textbf{x}\in X.
	$$
	Define
	$$
	G_{\textbf{a}}(\textbf{x})\triangleq
	\sum\limits_{\substack{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_0^n\\|\alpha|=m}}
	\varepsilon_\alpha\frac{m!}{\alpha!}
	\prod\limits_{k=1}^n(a_kx_{j_k})^{\alpha_k},
	\qquad \textbf{x}\in X.
	$$
	Then $G_{\textbf{a}}\in\mathcal{P}^m(X)$ depends only on the coordinates indexed by $J$, and \eqref{shi-suijicanshuguji} gives $\|G_{\textbf{a}}\|\leqslant C(nm!\log m)^\sigma$. For every $\beta\in\mathbb{N}_0^{(\mathbb{N})}$ with $|\beta|=m$,
	$$
	c_\beta(G_{\textbf{a}})=
	\begin{cases}
		\displaystyle
		\varepsilon_{(\beta_{j_1},\cdots,\beta_{j_n})}
		\frac{m!}{\beta!}\prod\limits_{k=1}^n a_k^{\beta_{j_k}},
		&\mathrm{supp}\beta\subset J,\\[5pt]
		0,&\mathrm{supp}\beta\not\subset J.
	\end{cases}
	$$
	The multinomial theorem and \eqref{shi-weibuzhishijiading} therefore yield
	\begin{equation}
		\begin{aligned}
		&\left(\sum\limits_{k=1}^n|a_kz_{j_k}|\right)^m
			=\sum\limits_{\substack{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_0^n\\|\alpha|=m}}
			\frac{m!}{\alpha!}\prod\limits_{k=1}^n|a_kz_{j_k}|^{\alpha_k}\\
			&\quad =\sum\limits_{\substack{\beta\in\mathbb{N}_0^{(\mathbb{N})}\\|\beta|=m}}
			|c_\beta(G_{\textbf{a}})\textbf{z}^\beta| \leqslant AR^m\|G_{\textbf{a}}\|
			\\&\quad\leqslant AC R^m(nm!\log m)^\sigma.
		\end{aligned}
		\label{shi-duijiaoceshiguji}
	\end{equation}
	
	Put $S\triangleq\left\|(z_{j_k})_{k=1}^n\right\|_{\ell^q}$. By H\"older's inequality, $$\sum\limits_{k=1}^n|a_kz_{j_k}|\leqslant\|\textbf{a}\|_{\ell^s}S\leqslant S.$$If $q=1$, the choice $\textbf{a}=(1,\cdots,1)$ gives $\|\textbf{a}\|_{\ell^\infty}=1$ and $\sum\limits_{k=1}^n|a_kz_{j_k}|=S$. If $q>1$ and $S>0$, choose $a_k=\frac{|z_{j_k}|^{q-1}}{S^{q-1}}$, $1\leqslant k\leqslant n$. Since $s=\frac{q}{q-1}$,
	$$
	\|\textbf{a}\|_{\ell^s}^s
	=\frac{\sum\limits_{k=1}^n|z_{j_k}|^q}{S^q}=1,
	\qquad
	\sum\limits_{k=1}^n|a_kz_{j_k}|
	=\frac{\sum\limits_{k=1}^n|z_{j_k}|^q}{S^{q-1}}=S.
	$$
	For $S=0$, both sides of the following equality vanish. Thus \eqref{shi-duijiaoceshiguji} implies
	$$
	\begin{aligned}
		\left(\sum\limits_{k=1}^n|z_{j_k}|^q\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}
		&=\sup\limits_{\substack{\textbf{a}\in\mathbb{C}^n\\\|\textbf{a}\|_{\ell^s}\leqslant1}}
		\sum\limits_{k=1}^n|a_kz_{j_k}|\leqslant R(AC)^{\frac{1}{m}}n^{\frac{\sigma}{m}}
		(m!)^{\frac{\sigma}{m}}(\log m)^{\frac{\sigma}{m}}.
	\end{aligned}
	$$
	
	For every $F\subset\mathbb{N}$ with $|F|=n$, choose $H\subset\{N+1,N+2,\cdots\}\setminus F$ with $|H|=|F\cap\{1,\cdots,N\}|$, and put $\widetilde F\triangleq(F\setminus\{1,\cdots,N\})\cup H$. Then $\widetilde F\subset\{N+1,N+2,\cdots\}$, $|\widetilde F|=n$, and
	$$
	\sum\limits_{j\in F}|(Q_N\textbf{z})_j|^q
	=\sum\limits_{j\in F\setminus\{1,\cdots,N\}}|z_j|^q
	\leqslant\sum\limits_{j\in\widetilde F}|z_j|^q.
	$$
	Since $\textbf{z}\in X\subset c_0$, \eqref{shi-chongpaishangquejie} yields
	\begin{equation}
		\begin{aligned}
			&\left(\sum\limits_{j=1}^n\bigl((Q_N\textbf{z})_j^*\bigr)^q\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}
			=\sup\limits_{\substack{F\subset\mathbb{N}\\|F|=n}}
			\left(\sum\limits_{j\in F}|(Q_N\textbf{z})_j|^q\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}\\
			&\quad=\sup\limits_{N<j_1<\cdots<j_n}
			\left(\sum\limits_{k=1}^n|z_{j_k}|^q\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}\leqslant R(AC)^{\frac{1}{m}}n^{\frac{\sigma}{m}}
			(m!)^{\frac{\sigma}{m}}(\log m)^{\frac{\sigma}{m}}.
		\end{aligned}
		\label{shi-weibuchongpaijie}
	\end{equation}
Finally, set $m=\max\{2,\lceil\log(n+1)\rceil\}$. By the Stolz--Ces\`aro theorem,
$$
\lim\limits_{k\to\infty}
\frac{\log(k!)-k\log k}{k}
=-\lim\limits_{k\to\infty}
k\log\left(1+\frac{1}{k}\right)=-1.
$$
Hence $\frac{(k!)^{\frac{1}{k}}}{k}\longrightarrow e^{-1}$. Since $\frac{m}{\log(n+1)}\longrightarrow1$, we obtain
$$
n^{\frac{1}{m}}\longrightarrow e,
\qquad
\frac{(m!)^{\frac{1}{m}}}{\log(n+1)}
\longrightarrow e^{-1},
\qquad
(AC)^{\frac{1}{m}}(\log m)^{\frac{\sigma}{m}}
\longrightarrow1.
$$
Dividing \eqref{shi-weibuchongpaijie} by $(\log(n+1))^\sigma$ and taking the upper limit as $n\to\infty$ therefore gives
$$
\limsup\limits_{n\to\infty}
\frac{\left(\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}
	\bigl((Q_N\textbf{z})_j^*\bigr)^q\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}}
{(\log(n+1))^\sigma}
\leqslant R\left(e\cdot e^{-1}\right)^\sigma=R.
$$
This establishes \eqref{shi-weibushangjixian}.
<!-- end proof-28 -->
<!-- proposition-7: body_md -->
\label{mingti-youjiexingshoulian}
For $X,p,q,\sigma$ as in \eqref{shi-fenleizhishu}, one has
\begin{equation}
\mathrm{dom}H_b(X)
=\{\textbf{z}\in X:M(\textbf{z})<\infty\}.
\label{shi-youjiexingshoulian}
\end{equation}
<!-- end proposition-7 -->
<!-- proof-29: body_md -->
Let $C$ be the constant in
Lemma~\ref{yinli-chongpaixishuguji}.
We first record the estimate for a bounded holomorphic function.
Let $r>0$, $h\in H^\infty(rB_X)$, and let $H_m$ be its homogeneous
Taylor polynomials from
\eqref{shi-qicitaile}.
By \eqref{shi-qicixishuyizhi} and
\eqref{shi-qicikexiguji},
$c_\alpha(H_m)=c_\alpha(h)$ for $|\alpha|=m$ and
$\|H_m\|\leqslant\frac{\|h\|_\infty}{r^m}$.
Thus \eqref{shi-chongpaixishuguji} gives, whenever
$\textbf{v}\in X$ and $CM(\textbf{v})<r$,
\begin{equation}
\begin{aligned}
\sum\limits_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_0^{(\mathbb{N})}}
|c_\alpha(h)\textbf{v}^\alpha|
&=|h(\textbf{0})|+
\sum\limits_{m=1}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{|\alpha|=m}
|c_\alpha(H_m)\textbf{v}^\alpha|\\
&\leqslant\|h\|_\infty
\sum\limits_{m=0}^{\infty}
\left(\frac{CM(\textbf{v})}{r}\right)^m
=\frac{\|h\|_\infty}{1-\frac{CM(\textbf{v})}{r}}<\infty.
\end{aligned}
\label{shi-youjiehanshuqiuhe}
\end{equation}
For $\textbf{z}\in X$ with $M(\textbf{z})<\infty$ and
$f\in H_b(X)$, choose $r>CM(\textbf{z})$.
By \eqref{shi-youjiexing},
$f|_{rB_X}$ is bounded. Applying
\eqref{shi-youjiehanshuqiuhe} at $\textbf{z}$
proves $\textbf{z}\in\mathrm{dom}H_b(X)$.

Conversely, let $\textbf{z}\in\mathrm{dom}H_b(X)$.
By \eqref{shi-dianchuzhishijie} in
Lemma~\ref{yinli-bituguji}, there exists $C\geqslant1$ such that
$$
\sum\limits_{|\alpha|=m}|c_\alpha(G)\textbf{z}^\alpha|
\leqslant C^m\|G\|,
\qquad
G\in\mathcal{P}^m(X),\quad m\in\mathbb{N}.
$$
Applying Lemma~\ref{yinli-shangjixianbijiao}  gives
$$
\limsup\limits_{n\to\infty}
\frac{\left(\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}(z_j^*)^q\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}}
{(\log(n+1))^\sigma}
\leqslant C<\infty.
$$
Hence
$$
M(\textbf{z})
=
\sup\limits_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
\frac{\left(\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}(z_j^*)^q\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}}
{(\log(n+1))^\sigma}
<\infty,
$$
which proves the reverse inclusion in \eqref{shi-youjiexingshoulian}.
<!-- end proof-29 -->
<!-- proof-30: body_md -->
\par\medskip\noindent

By \cite[Theorem~3.1, p.~19]{DMP09}, \eqref{shi-yikeheshoulian} follows directly.
We therefore fix $1<p\leqslant\infty$ and take $X,p,q,\sigma$ as in
\eqref{shi-fenleizhishu}. Then we prove
\begin{equation}
	\mathrm{dom}H(X)
	=\left\{\textbf{z}\in X:
	\lim\limits_{n\to\infty}
	\frac{\left(\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}(z_j^*)^q\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}}
	{(\log(n+1))^\sigma}=0\right\}.
	\label{shi-jingdiantongyikehua}
\end{equation}
The assertion is immediate for $\textbf{z}=\textbf{0}$.
Let $\textbf{0}\neq\textbf{z}\in\mathrm{dom}H(X)$ and fix
$r>0$. By \eqref{shi-weibupanju} in
Proposition~\ref{mingti-jubuhua}, there exist $N\in\mathbb{N}$
and $A>0$ such that
$$
\sum\limits_\alpha|c_\alpha(G)\textbf{z}^\alpha|
\leqslant A\sup\limits_{\textbf{x}\in rB_X}|G(\textbf{x})|,
\qquad G\in\mathscr{P}_N.
$$
Hence, for every $m\in\mathbb{N}$ and
$G\in\mathcal{P}^m(X)\cap\mathscr{P}_N$,
$$
\sum\limits_{|\alpha|=m}|c_\alpha(G)\textbf{z}^\alpha|
\leqslant Ar^m\|G\|.
$$
Since $\overline{\mathbb{C}^{(\mathbb{N})}}^{\,X} =X$,
Lemma~\ref{yinli-shangjixianbijiao}, with $R=r$, gives
\eqref{shi-weibushangjixian}. By \eqref{shi-shoubumodong},
$$
\limsup\limits_{n\to\infty}
\frac{\left(\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}(z_j^*)^q\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}}
{(\log(n+1))^\sigma}
\leqslant r.
$$
Since $r>0$ is arbitrary,
$$
\lim\limits_{n\to\infty}
\frac{\left(\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}(z_j^*)^q\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}}
{(\log(n+1))^\sigma}
=0.
$$

Conversely, let $\textbf{z}\in X$ satisfy
$$
\lim\limits_{n\to\infty}
\frac{\left(\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}(z_j^*)^q\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}}
{(\log(n+1))^\sigma}
=0.
$$
Fix $r>0$, and let $C$ be the constant in
Lemma~\ref{yinli-chongpaixishuguji}. By \eqref{shi-weibudengjia}, choose
$N\in\mathbb{N}$ such that
$
CM(Q_N\textbf{z})<\frac{r}{2}.
$
For $G\in\mathscr{P}_N$, write
$$
G=\sum\limits_{m=0}^{d}H_m,
\qquad
H_m\in\mathcal{P}^m(X)\cap\mathscr{P}_N,
\quad 0\leqslant m\leqslant d,
$$
for its homogeneous decomposition. By \eqref{shi-qicixishuyizhi},
\eqref{shi-qicikexiguji}, and \eqref{shi-chongpaixishuguji},
$$
\begin{aligned}
	&\sum\limits_\alpha|c_\alpha(G)\textbf{z}^\alpha|
	=\sum\limits_{m=0}^{d}
	\sum\limits_{|\alpha|=m}
	|c_\alpha(H_m)(Q_N\textbf{z})^\alpha|\\
	&\leqslant
	\|G\|_r
	\sum\limits_{m=0}^{d}
	\left(\frac{CM(Q_N\textbf{z})}{r}\right)^m\leqslant
	\|G\|_r\sum\limits_{m=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{2^m}
	=2\|G\|_r.
\end{aligned}
$$
Thus \eqref{shi-weibupanju} holds for every $r>0$, and
Proposition~\ref{mingti-jubuhua}, with $O=X$, yields
$
\textbf{z}\in\mathrm{dom}H(X).
$
Therefore \eqref{shi-jingdiantongyikehua} holds, and
\eqref{shi-xiaozhishushoulian}, \eqref{shi-dazhishushoulian}, and \eqref{shi-lingjixianshoulian} follow from
\eqref{shi-fenleizhishu}.
<!-- end proof-30 -->
<!-- theorem-5: body_md -->
\label{dingli-youjiexulieshoulian}
One has
\begin{equation}
\mathrm{dom}H(\ell^\infty)
=
\left\{\textbf{z}\in c_0:
\sup\limits_{n\geqslant1}
\frac{\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}(z_j^*)^2}{\log(n+1)}<\infty\right\}.
\label{shi-youjiexulieshoulian}
\end{equation}
Moreover,
$$
\mathrm{dom}H(\ell^\infty)
=\mathrm{dom}H_b(c_0).
$$
<!-- end theorem-5 -->
<!-- proof-31: body_md -->
Aron and Berner \cite[Proposition~1.1, p.~8]{AB78} prove that \begin{equation}
\{F|_{c_0}:F\in H(\ell^\infty)\}=H_b(c_0).
\label{shi-zhenghanshuxianzhi}
\end{equation}

Since
$\overline{\mathbb{C}^{(\mathbb{N})}}^{\,\ell^\infty}=c_0$,
\eqref{shi-bibaobaohan} gives
\begin{equation}
\mathrm{dom}H(\ell^\infty)\subset c_0.
\label{shi-shoulianlingjixian}
\end{equation}
Restriction from $\ell^\infty$ to $c_0$ leaves each $c_\alpha$
unchanged, because the finite-dimensional restrictions defining it
coincide. Together with \eqref{shi-shoulianlingjixian} and
\eqref{shi-youjiexingshoulian} for $p=\infty$,
this gives \eqref{shi-youjiexulieshoulian}.
<!-- end proof-31 -->
<!-- corollary-3: body_md -->
\label{tuilun-youjiexuliedengjia}
One has
\begin{equation}
\mathrm{dom}H_b(\ell^\infty)
=\mathrm{dom}H(\ell^\infty)
=\mathrm{dom}H_b(c_0).
\label{shi-sangeshoulianyudengjia}
\end{equation}
In particular,
$\mathrm{dom}H_b(\ell^\infty)$ is the set on the right-hand side of
\eqref{shi-youjiexulieshoulian}.
<!-- end corollary-3 -->
<!-- proof-32: body_md -->
Since $H_b(\ell^\infty)\subset H(\ell^\infty)$, the definition
\eqref{shi-shoulianyu} gives
$$
\mathrm{dom}H(\ell^\infty)
\subseteq\mathrm{dom}H_b(\ell^\infty).
$$
To prove the reverse inclusion, fix
$\textbf{z}\in\mathrm{dom}H_b(\ell^\infty)$.
Every continuous quadratic polynomial on $\ell^\infty$ belongs to
$H_b(\ell^\infty)$, so Theorem~\ref{dingli-ercizhangai} implies
$
\textbf{z}\in
\overline{\mathbb{C}^{(\mathbb{N})}}^{\,\ell^\infty}=c_0.
$
By Lemma~\ref{yinli-bituguji},  there is $K\geqslant1$ such that
\begin{equation}
\sum\limits_{|\alpha|=m}|c_\alpha(P)\textbf{z}^\alpha|
\leqslant K^m\|P\|_{\mathcal{P}^m(\ell^\infty)},
\qquad P\in\mathcal{P}^m(\ell^\infty),\quad m\in\mathbb{N}.
\label{shi-youjiexuliexishuguji}
\end{equation}
The constant $K$ is independent of $m$ and $P$.

For $m,n\in\mathbb{N}$ and $G\in\mathcal{P}^m(c_0)$ satisfying
$G=G\circ P_n$, define
$$
\widetilde G(\textbf{x})\triangleq G(P_n\textbf{x}),
\qquad\textbf{x}\in\ell^\infty.
$$
Then $\widetilde G\in\mathcal{P}^m(\ell^\infty)$ and
\begin{equation}
\|\widetilde G\|_{\mathcal{P}^m(\ell^\infty)}
=\|G\|_{\mathcal{P}^m(c_0)},
\qquad
c_\alpha(\widetilde G)=c_\alpha(G),\qquad \alpha\in\mathbb{N}_0^{(\mathbb{N})}.
\label{shi-youxianzuobiaoyantuo}
\end{equation}
Applying
\eqref{shi-youjiexuliexishuguji} to $\widetilde G$
and using \eqref{shi-youxianzuobiaoyantuo}, we obtain
$$
\sum\limits_{|\alpha|=m}|c_\alpha(G)\textbf{z}^\alpha|
\leqslant K^m\|G\|_{\mathcal{P}^m(c_0)}
$$
for every continuous homogeneous polynomial on $c_0$ depending on
finitely many coordinates. Thus Lemma~\ref{yinli-shangjixianbijiao} yields
$$
\limsup\limits_{n\to\infty}
\frac{\left(\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}(z_j^*)^2\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}}
{(\log(n+1))^{\frac{1}{2}}}
\leqslant K.$$
Together with $\textbf{z}\in c_0$,
Theorem~\ref{dingli-youjiexulieshoulian} therefore gives
$\textbf{z}\in\mathrm{dom}H(\ell^\infty)$.
This proves the reverse inclusion
$
\mathrm{dom}H_b(\ell^\infty)
\subseteq
\mathrm{dom}H(\ell^\infty).
$
The remaining equality in \eqref{shi-sangeshoulianyudengjia}
follows from Theorem~\ref{dingli-youjiexulieshoulian}.
<!-- end proof-32 -->
<!-- corollary-4: body_md -->
\label{tuilun-jingdianquanyu}
Let $X=\ell^p$ with $1\leqslant p<\infty$, or let $X=c_0$,
and let $O\subset X$ be a Reinhardt domain. If $X=\ell^1$, then
\begin{equation}
\mathrm{dom}H(O)=O.
\label{shi-yikehequanyu}
\end{equation}
If $X=\ell^p$ with $1<p\leqslant2$, then
\begin{equation}
\mathrm{dom}H(O)
=
\left\{\textbf{z}\in O:
\lim\limits_{n\to\infty}
\frac{\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}z_j^*}
{(\log(n+1))^{1-\frac{1}{p}}}=0\right\}.
\label{shi-xiaozhishuquanyu}
\end{equation}
If $X=\ell^p$ with $2<p<\infty$, then
\begin{equation}
\mathrm{dom}H(O)
=
\left\{\textbf{z}\in O:
\lim\limits_{n\to\infty}
\frac{\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}(z_j^*)^{\frac{2p}{p+2}}}
{(\log(n+1))^{\frac{p}{p+2}}}=0\right\}.
\label{shi-dazhishuquanyu}
\end{equation}
If $X=c_0$, then
\begin{equation}
\mathrm{dom}H(O)
=
\left\{\textbf{z}\in O:
\lim\limits_{n\to\infty}
\frac{\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}(z_j^*)^2}{\log(n+1)}=0\right\}.
\label{shi-lingjixianquanyu}
\end{equation}
In each case, for every $f\in H(O)$ and
$\textbf{z}\in\mathrm{dom}H(O)$,
\eqref{shi-juduishoulian} and \eqref{shi-hanshubiaoshi} hold.
<!-- end corollary-4 -->
<!-- proposition-8: body_md -->
\label{mingti-youjiexulieqiu}
One has
\begin{equation}
\mathrm{dom}H(B_{\ell^\infty})
=
\left\{\textbf{z}\in B_{c_0}:
\limsup\limits_{n\to\infty}
\frac{\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}(z_j^*)^2}{\log(n+1)}<1\right\}.
\label{shi-youjiexulieqiu}
\end{equation}
Moreover, for every $f\in H(B_{\ell^\infty})$ and every
$\textbf{z}$ in the set on the right of
\eqref{shi-youjiexulieqiu},
\eqref{shi-juduishoulian} and \eqref{shi-hanshubiaoshi} hold.
<!-- end proposition-8 -->
<!-- proof-33: body_md -->
\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{Step 1}\par\nopagebreak[4]

Since
$\overline{\mathbb{C}^{(\mathbb{N})}}^{\,\ell^\infty}=c_0$,
\eqref{shi-bibaobaohan}, applied to
$O=B_{\ell^\infty}$, gives
\begin{equation}
\mathrm{dom}H(B_{\ell^\infty})\subset B_{c_0}.
\label{shi-qiushoulianlingjixian}
\end{equation}
For every $f\in H(B_{\ell^\infty})$, we have
\begin{equation}
c_\alpha(f|_{B_{c_0}})=c_\alpha(f),
\qquad \alpha\in\mathbb{N}_0^{(\mathbb{N})}.
\label{shi-xianzhixishuyizhi}
\end{equation}
Consequently, whenever the monomial series of $f$ is absolutely
convergent at $\textbf{z}\in B_{c_0}$,
Proposition~\ref{mingti-jishubiaoshi} identifies its sum with $f(\textbf{z})$
by \eqref{shi-hanshubiaoshi}. 

By \cite[Theorem~2.2\,(a)]{BDFMS17}, together with
\cite[(14) and (16)]{BDFMS17}, we have, for
$X\in\{c_0,\ell^\infty\}$ and $\textbf{w}\in B_{c_0}$,
\begin{equation}
	\limsup\limits_{n\to\infty}
	\frac{\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}(w_j^*)^2}{\log(n+1)}<1
	\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
	\textbf{w}\in\mathrm{dom}H^\infty(B_X).
	\label{shi-yuzhichongfentiaojian}
\end{equation}

\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{Step 2}\par\nopagebreak[4]

Let $\textbf{z}\in\mathrm{dom}H(B_{\ell^\infty})$. By \eqref{shi-qiushoulianlingjixian}, $\textbf{z}\in B_{c_0}$. Since $H_b(B_{\ell^\infty})\subset H(B_{\ell^\infty})$, Lemma~\ref{yinli-bituguji} gives $A>0$ and $0<\rho<1$ such that
\begin{equation}
\sum\limits_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_0^{(\mathbb{N})}}
|c_\alpha(f)\textbf{z}^\alpha|
\leqslant A\|f\|_\rho,\qquad f\in H_b(B_{\ell^\infty}).
\label{shi-qiubituguji}
\end{equation}
Fix $m\geqslant2$, $n\in\mathbb{N}$, and $G\in\mathcal{P}^m(c_0)$ with $G=G\circ P_n$. Put $\widetilde G(\textbf{x})\triangleq G(P_n\textbf{x})$ for $\textbf{x}\in\ell^\infty$. Then $\widetilde G|_{B_{\ell^\infty}}\in H_b(B_{\ell^\infty})$. By \eqref{shi-youxianzuobiaoyantuo}, \eqref{shi-qiubituguji}, and homogeneity,
\begin{equation}
\begin{aligned}
\sum\limits_{|\alpha|=m}|c_\alpha(G)\textbf{z}^\alpha|
&=\sum\limits_{|\alpha|=m}|c_\alpha(\widetilde G)\textbf{z}^\alpha|
\leqslant A\|\widetilde G\|_\rho\\
&=A\rho^m\|\widetilde G\|_{\mathcal{P}^m(\ell^\infty)}
=A\rho^m\|G\|_{\mathcal{P}^m(c_0)}.
\end{aligned}
\label{shi-qiucishujie}
\end{equation}
By \eqref{shi-qiucishujie} and Lemma~\ref{yinli-shangjixianbijiao},
$$
\limsup\limits_{n\to\infty}
\frac{\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}(z_j^*)^2}{\log(n+1)}
\leqslant\rho^2<1.
$$
This proves the necessary condition in \eqref{shi-youjiexulieqiu}.

\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{Step 3}\par\nopagebreak[4]

By \cite[p.~455]{GMZ12}, a
function in $H(B_{c_0})$ extends holomorphically to $B_{\ell^\infty}$
if and only if it belongs to $H_b(B_{c_0})$. Therefore, for each $f\in H(B_{\ell^\infty})$ and $0<r<1$, we have
\begin{equation}
\sup\limits_{\textbf{w}\in rB_{c_0}}|f(\textbf{w})|<\infty.
\label{shi-xianzhijubuyoujie}
\end{equation}
Let $\textbf{z}\in B_{c_0}$ satisfy the upper-limit condition in
\eqref{shi-youjiexulieqiu}. Choose $r$ such that
\begin{equation}
\|\textbf{z}\|_{\ell^\infty}<r<1,
\qquad
\limsup\limits_{n\to\infty}
\frac{\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}(z_j^*)^2}{\log(n+1)}<r^2.
\label{shi-suofangbanjing}
\end{equation}
Fix $f\in H(B_{\ell^\infty})$, and define
$$
h(\textbf{w})\triangleq f(r\textbf{w}),\qquad\textbf{w}\in B_{c_0}.
$$
By \eqref{shi-xianzhijubuyoujie},
$h\in H^\infty(B_{c_0})$.
It is easy to see that
\begin{equation}
c_\alpha(h)=r^{|\alpha|}c_\alpha(f),
\qquad\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_0^{(\mathbb{N})}.
\label{shi-suofangxishu}
\end{equation}
Also, \eqref{shi-suofangbanjing} implies
$\frac{\textbf{z}}{r}\in B_{c_0}$ and
$$
\limsup\limits_{n\to\infty}
\frac{\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}\left(\frac{z_j^*}{r}\right)^2}
{\log(n+1)}
=\frac{1}{r^2}\limsup\limits_{n\to\infty}
\frac{\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}(z_j^*)^2}{\log(n+1)}<1.
$$
By \eqref{shi-yuzhichongfentiaojian},
$\frac{\textbf{z}}{r}\in\mathrm{dom}H^\infty(B_{c_0})$.
Consequently, \eqref{shi-suofangxishu} yields
$$
\sum\limits_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_0^{(\mathbb{N})}}
|c_\alpha(f)\textbf{z}^\alpha|
=\sum\limits_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_0^{(\mathbb{N})}}
\left|c_\alpha(h)\left(\frac{\textbf{z}}{r}\right)^\alpha\right|
<\infty.
$$
Since $f$ was arbitrary, this proves the reverse inclusion in
\eqref{shi-youjiexulieqiu}. Equation
\eqref{shi-xianzhixishuyizhi} and
Proposition~\ref{mingti-jishubiaoshi}, applied to
$f|_{B_{c_0}}$ at $\textbf{z}$, now give
\eqref{shi-hanshubiaoshi}.
<!-- end proof-33 -->
<!-- remark-5: body_md -->
\label{zhuji-linjieqingxing}
The strict inequality in \eqref{shi-youjiexulieqiu}
characterizes $\mathrm{dom}H(B_{\ell^\infty})$.
For bounded holomorphic functions, however, a complete characterization
is still not known. The available result is due to
\cite[Theorem~2.2\,(a)--(b)]{BDFMS17}:
$$
	\begin{aligned}
		\left\{\textbf{z}\in B_{c_0}:
		\limsup\limits_{n\to\infty}
		\frac{\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}(z_j^*)^2}{\log(n+1)}<1\right\}
		&\subseteq\mathrm{dom}H^\infty(B_{\ell^\infty})\\
		&\subsetneq
		\left\{\textbf{z}\in B_{c_0}:
		\limsup\limits_{n\to\infty}
		\frac{\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}(z_j^*)^2}{\log(n+1)}\leqslant1\right\}.
	\end{aligned}
$$
<!-- end remark-5 -->
<!-- remark-6: body_md -->
\label{zhuji-jubuhuashixiao}
It is easy to see that
$$
\mathrm{dom}H(O)
\subset O\cap\mathrm{dom}H(\ell^\infty).
$$
However, the equality \eqref{shi-jubuhua} cannot be
applied with $X=\ell^\infty$, since its hypothesis fails:
$\overline{\mathbb{C}^{(\mathbb{N})}}^{\,\ell^\infty}
=c_0\neq\ell^\infty$. Equations~\eqref{shi-youjiexulieshoulian} and~\eqref{shi-youjiexulieqiu} also provide a concrete example showing that \eqref{shi-jubuhua} cannot be applied when $X=\ell^\infty$.
<!-- end remark-6 -->
<!-- theorem-6: body_md -->
\label{dingli-l1-zuijiabanjing}
	Let $t\in\left(\frac{1}{2},1\right)$ be the unique solution of
	\begin{equation}
		-\log(1-t)=2t.
		\label{shi-l1-zuijiacanshu}
	\end{equation}
	Every function $f$ holomorphic in a neighbourhood of $\textbf{0}$
	in  $\ell^1$ satisfies
	\begin{equation}
		\sqrt{t(1-t)}\,r(f,\textbf{0})
		\leqslant |r|(f,\textbf{0})
		\leqslant r(f,\textbf{0}).
		\label{shi-l1-zuijiabanjing}
	\end{equation}
	Both constants are optimal.
<!-- end theorem-6 -->
<!-- proof-34: body_md -->
By $-\log(1-t)=2t$ and
$$
\sup\limits_{s>0}\frac{\log(1+s^2)}{2s}
=\frac{-\log(1-t)}{2\sqrt{\frac{t}{1-t}}}
=\sqrt{t(1-t)},
$$
we have
	\begin{equation}
		1+s^2\leqslant e^{2s\sqrt{t(1-t)}},
		\qquad s\geqslant0.
		\label{shi-l1-biaoliangzhishijie}
	\end{equation}
	We shall also use
	\begin{equation}
b^{2a}\geqslant a!b^{a}\geqslant a!b\left( b-1 \right) \cdots \left( b-a+1 \right) =\left( a! \right)^{2} \binom{b}{a},
		\qquad a,b\in\mathbb{N}_0,b\geqslant a.
		\label{shi-l1-zhengshujuguji}
	\end{equation}
	Finally, Stirling's formula and
	\eqref{shi-l1-zuijiacanshu} give
	\begin{equation}
\begin{aligned}
	&\begin{aligned}\lim\limits_{m\to \infty} \frac{\binom{m^{2}}{m}^{\frac{1}{m}}}{4^{\frac{1}{m}}e\, m\sqrt{t(1-t)} \left( \log 8+2m^{2}\log (1+24m) \right)^{\frac{1}{2m}}}&\end{aligned}\\ &\  =\lim\limits_{m\to \infty} \frac{\left\lceil \frac{m}{t} \right\rceil}{\left( m! \right)^{\frac{1}{m}} \binom{\left\lceil \frac{m}{t} \right\rceil}{m}^{\frac{1}{2m}}} =\frac{1}{\sqrt{t\left( 1-t \right)}}.\end{aligned}
		\label{shi-l1-caiyanggenjixian}
	\end{equation}

	
	\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{Step 1}\par\nopagebreak[4]
	
	We prove that, for all integers $k\geqslant m\geqslant2$,
	\begin{equation}
		\|P\|\leqslant\|P^{\#}\|
		\leqslant
		\frac{k^m}{m!\binom{k}{m}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\|P\|,
		\qquad P\in\mathcal{P}^m(\ell^1).
		\label{shi-l1-jingquexishushangjie}
	\end{equation}
The lower bound in \eqref{shi-l1-jingquexishushangjie} is immediate from
$$
|P(\textbf{z})|
\leqslant P^{\#}(|\textbf{z}|),
\qquad \textbf{z}\in B_{\ell^1}.
$$
To prove the upper bound, by the same truncation argument as in
Lemma~\ref{yinli-gujituiguang}, it suffices to consider
$P\in\mathcal{P}^m(\ell^1)$ satisfying $P=P\circ P_n$ for some
$n\in\mathbb{N}$. Fix
	$\textbf{x}=(x_1,\cdots,x_n)\in[0,+\infty)^n$ with
	$\sum\limits_{j=1}^n x_j=1$.
	For every $\beta\in\mathbb{N}_0^n$ with $|\beta|=k$, 
\begin{equation}
	\begin{aligned}
		&\frac{1}{k^{2m}}
		\sum\limits_{\substack{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_0^n\\|\alpha|=m}}
		|c_\alpha(P)|^2
		\prod\limits_{j=1}^n\beta_j^{2\alpha_j}=
		\sum\limits_{\substack{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_0^n\\|\alpha|=m}}
		\left|
		c_\alpha(P)
		\prod\limits_{j=1}^n
		\left(\frac{\beta_j}{k}\right)^{\alpha_j}
		\right|^2\\
		&\quad=
		\frac{1}{(2\pi)^n}
		\int\limits_{[0,2\pi]^n}
		\left|
		\sum\limits_{\substack{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_0^n\\|\alpha|=m}}
		c_\alpha(P)
		\prod\limits_{j=1}^n
		\left(
		\frac{\beta_j}{k}e^{\mathrm{i}\theta_j}
		\right)^{\alpha_j}
		\right|^2
		\,\mathrm{d}\theta_1\cdots\mathrm{d}\theta_n\\
		&\quad=
		\frac{1}{(2\pi)^n}
		\int\limits_{[0,2\pi]^n}
		\left|
		P\left(
		\sum\limits_{j=1}^n
		\frac{\beta_j}{k}e^{\mathrm{i}\theta_j}e_j
		\right)
		\right|^2
		\,\mathrm{d}\theta_1\cdots\mathrm{d}\theta_n\\
		&\quad\leqslant
		\frac{1}{(2\pi)^n}
		\int\limits_{[0,2\pi]^n}
		\|P\|^2
		\left\|
		\sum\limits_{j=1}^n
		\frac{\beta_j}{k}e^{\mathrm{i}\theta_j}e_j
		\right\|_{\ell^1}^{2m}
		\,\mathrm{d}\theta_1\cdots\mathrm{d}\theta_n\\
		&\quad=
		\frac{1}{(2\pi)^n}
		\int\limits_{[0,2\pi]^n}
		\|P\|^2
		\left(
		\sum\limits_{j=1}^n\frac{\beta_j}{k}
		\right)^{2m}
		\,\mathrm{d}\theta_1\cdots\mathrm{d}\theta_n\\
		&\quad=
		\frac{1}{(2\pi)^n}
		\int\limits_{[0,2\pi]^n}
		\|P\|^2
		\left(\frac{|\beta|}{k}\right)^{2m}
		\,\mathrm{d}\theta_1\cdots\mathrm{d}\theta_n
		=\|P\|^2,
	\end{aligned}\label{shi-714}
\end{equation}
	where the second equality follows from Parseval's identity.	For $|\alpha|=m$, inequality
	\eqref{shi-l1-zhengshujuguji} implies
\begin{equation}
	\begin{aligned}
		&\sum\limits_{\substack{\beta\in\mathbb{N}_0^n\\|\beta|=k}}
		\frac{k!}{\beta!}\textbf{x}^{\beta}
		\prod\limits_{j=1}^n\beta_j^{2\alpha_j}\geqslant
		(\alpha!)^2
		\sum\limits_{\substack{\beta\in\mathbb{N}_0^n\\
				|\beta|=k,\ \beta\geqslant\alpha}}
		\frac{k!}{\beta!}\textbf{x}^{\beta}
		\prod\limits_{j=1}^n\binom{\beta_j}{\alpha_j}\\
		&\quad=
		\alpha!k!\textbf{x}^{\alpha}
		\sum\limits_{\substack{\gamma\in\mathbb{N}_0^n\\|\gamma|=k-m}}
		\frac{\textbf{x}^{\gamma}}{\gamma!}
		=
		\alpha!\frac{k!}{(k-m)!}\textbf{x}^{\alpha}.\label{shi-713}
	\end{aligned}
\end{equation}
	Here $\beta\geqslant\alpha$ is understood $\beta_{j} \geqslant \alpha_{j} ,1\leqslant j\leqslant n$.
	
By \eqref{shi-713}, \eqref{shi-714} and $\sum\limits_{\substack{\beta\in\mathbb{N}_0^n\\|\beta|=k}}
\frac{k!}{\beta!}\textbf{x}^{\beta}=\| \textbf{x} \|_{\ell^{{}1}}^{k} =1$, we have
	$$
\begin{aligned}
	\|P\|^2
	&=
	\|P\|^2
	\sum\limits_{\substack{\beta\in\mathbb{N}_0^n\\|\beta|=k}}
	\frac{k!}{\beta!}\textbf{x}^{\beta}\geqslant
	\frac{1}{k^{2m}}
	\sum\limits_{\substack{\beta\in\mathbb{N}_0^n\\|\beta|=k}}
	\frac{k!}{\beta!}\textbf{x}^{\beta}
	\sum\limits_{\substack{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_0^n\\|\alpha|=m}}
	|c_\alpha(P)|^2
	\prod\limits_{j=1}^n\beta_j^{2\alpha_j}\\
	&=
	\frac{1}{k^{2m}}
	\sum\limits_{\substack{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_0^n\\|\alpha|=m}}
	|c_\alpha(P)|^2
	\sum\limits_{\substack{\beta\in\mathbb{N}_0^n\\|\beta|=k}}
	\frac{k!}{\beta!}\textbf{x}^{\beta}
	\prod\limits_{j=1}^n\beta_j^{2\alpha_j}\geqslant
	\frac{k!}{k^{2m}(k-m)!}
	\sum\limits_{\substack{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_0^n\\|\alpha|=m}}
	\alpha!|c_\alpha(P)|^2\textbf{x}^{\alpha},
\end{aligned}
	$$
Then the Cauchy--Schwarz inequality gives
	$$
	\begin{aligned}
		\bigl(P^{\#}(\textbf{x})\bigr)^2
		&\leqslant
		\left(
		\sum\limits_{\substack{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_0^n\\|\alpha|=m}}
		\alpha!|c_\alpha(P)|^2\textbf{x}^{\alpha}
		\right)
		\left(
		\sum\limits_{\substack{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_0^n\\|\alpha|=m}}
		\frac{\textbf{x}^{\alpha}}{\alpha!}
		\right)\leqslant
		\frac{k^{2m}(k-m)!}{m!k!}\|P\|^2,
	\end{aligned}
	$$
	which gives the upper
	bound in \eqref{shi-l1-jingquexishushangjie}.
	

	
	\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{Step 2}\par\nopagebreak[4]
	
	Let $f$ be holomorphic in a neighbourhood of $\textbf{0}$,
	and let $H_m$ be its homogeneous Taylor polynomials.
	By local boundedness of $f$ and \eqref{shi-qicikexiguji},
	$$
	\limsup\limits_{m\to\infty}\|H_m\|^{\frac{1}{m}}<+\infty.
	$$
	Apply \eqref{shi-l1-jingquexishushangjie} to $H_m$ with
	$k=\left\lceil\frac{m}{t}\right\rceil$ and using
	\eqref{shi-l1-caiyanggenjixian}, gives
	$$
	\begin{aligned}
		\limsup\limits_{m\to\infty}\|H_m\|^{\frac{1}{m}}
		&\leqslant
		\limsup\limits_{m\to\infty}\|H_m^{\#}\|^{\frac{1}{m}}\leqslant
		\frac{1}{\sqrt{t(1-t)}}
		\limsup\limits_{m\to\infty}\|H_m\|^{\frac{1}{m}}.
	\end{aligned}
	$$
Thus we have completed the proof of \eqref{shi-l1-zuijiabanjing}.
	
	\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{Step 3}\par\nopagebreak[4]
	
To prove the optimality of the lower bound, fix $ m\geqslant2$.
	For $\textbf{u}=\left( u_{i} \right)_{1\leqslant i\leqslant m^{2}}\in\mathbb{C}^{m^{2}}$ with
	$\|\textbf{u}\|_{\ell^1}\leqslant1$,
	\eqref{shi-l1-biaoliangzhishijie} give
	$$
	\begin{aligned}
		&\left(\frac{m^2}{t(1-t)}\right)^m
		\sum\limits_{\substack{F\subset\{1,\cdots,m^{2}\}\\|F|=m}}
		\prod\limits_{j\in F}|u_j|^2\leqslant
		\prod\limits_{j=1}^{m^{2}}
		\left(1+\frac{m^2|u_j|^2}{t(1-t)}\right)\leqslant
		e^{2m\sum\limits_{j=1}^{m^{2}}|u_j|}
		\leqslant e^{2m}.
	\end{aligned}
	$$
	Consequently,
	\begin{equation}
		(m!)^2
		\sum\limits_{\substack{F\subset\{1,\cdots,m^{2}\}\\|F|=m}}
		\prod\limits_{j\in F}|u_j|^2
		\leqslant
		(m!)^2
		\left(\frac{e\sqrt{t(1-t)}}{m}\right)^{2m}.
		\label{shi-l1-suijifangcha}
	\end{equation}
	
	Apply Lemma~\ref{yinli-duoxiangshiwang} and let $\mathcal{N}$ be the resulting finite subset
	of the closed $\ell^2$ unit ball in $\mathbb{C}^{m^{2}}$.
	For every $m$-homogeneous polynomial $P$ on $\mathbb{C}^{m^{2}}$,
	the polynomial
	$\textbf{u}\mapsto P(u_1^2,\cdots,u_{m^{2}}^2)$
	is $2m$-homogeneous. Moreover,
	$$
	\left\{
	(u_1^2,\cdots,u_{m^{2}}^2):
	\textbf{u}\in\mathbb{C}^{m^{2}},\
	\|\textbf{u}\|_{\ell^2}\leqslant1
	\right\}
	=
	\left\{
	\textbf{v}\in\mathbb{C}^{m^{2}}:
	\|\textbf{v}\|_{\ell^1}\leqslant1
	\right\}.
	$$

	The cited lemma therefore gives
	\begin{equation}
		\begin{gathered}
			|\mathcal{N}|\leqslant(1+24m)^{2m^{2}},\qquad	\|P\|
			=
			\sup\limits_{\|\textbf{u}\|_{\ell^2}\leqslant1}
			|P(u_1^2,\cdots,u_{m^{2}}^2)|
			\leqslant
			2\max\limits_{\textbf{u}\in\mathcal{N}}
			|P(u_1^2,\cdots,u_{m^{2}}^2)|.
		\end{gathered}
		\label{shi-l1-danchunxingwang}
	\end{equation}
	
	Let
	$
	(\varepsilon_F)_{\substack{F\subset\{1,\cdots,m^{2}\}\\|F|=m}}
	$
	be independent random variables satisfying
	$$
	\mathbb{P}(\varepsilon_F=1)
	=
	\mathbb{P}(\varepsilon_F=-1)
	=
	\frac{1}{2},
	\qquad
	F\subset\{1,\cdots,m^{2}\},\quad |F|=m.
	$$and put
	$$
	R_m(\textbf{u})\triangleq
	m!\sum\limits_{\substack{F\subset\{1,\cdots,m^{2}\}\\|F|=m}}
	\varepsilon_F\prod\limits_{j\in F}u_j.
	$$
	By \eqref{shi-suijiweigailv} and 
	\eqref{shi-l1-suijifangcha}, for each $\|\textbf{u}\|_{\ell^1}\leqslant1,\lambda>0$,
	$$
	\begin{aligned}
		\mathbb{P}\bigl(|R_m(\textbf{u})|>\lambda\bigr)
		&\leqslant
		\mathbb{P}\left(
		|\mathrm{Re}\,R_m(\textbf{u})|>\frac{\lambda}{\sqrt{2}}
		\right)
		+
		\mathbb{P}\left(
		|\mathrm{Im}\,R_m(\textbf{u})|>\frac{\lambda}{\sqrt{2}}
		\right)\\
		&\leqslant
		2e^{
			-\frac{(\frac{\lambda}{\sqrt{2}})^2}
			{2	\sum\limits_{\substack{F\subset\{1,\cdots,m^{2}\}\\|F|=m}}
	\left(
				\mathrm{Re}\left(
				m!\prod\limits_{j\in F}u_j
				\right)
				\right)^2}
		}
		+
		2e^{
			-\frac{(\frac{\lambda}{\sqrt{2}})^2}
			{2	\sum\limits_{\substack{F\subset\{1,\cdots,m^{2}\}\\|F|=m}}
				\left(
				\mathrm{Im}\left(
				m!\prod\limits_{j\in F}u_j
				\right)
				\right)^2}
		}\\
		&\leqslant
		4e^{-\frac{\lambda^2}
			{4(m!)^2
				\left(
				\frac{e\sqrt{t(1-t)}}{m}
				\right)^{2m}}}.
	\end{aligned}
	$$
	
	Take
	$$
	\lambda\triangleq
	2m!\left(\frac{e\sqrt{t(1-t)}}{m}\right)^m
	\sqrt{\log8+2m^{2}\log(1+24m)}.
	$$
Hence, by \eqref{shi-l1-danchunxingwang}, 
$$
\begin{aligned}\mathbb{P} \left( \frac{\| R_m\|}{2} >\lambda \right)&\leqslant \mathbb{P} \left( \max\limits_{\textbf{u} \in \mathcal{N}} |R_m(u_{1}^{2},\cdots ,u_{m^{2}}^{2})|>\lambda \right)\\ &\leqslant 4|\mathcal{N} |e^{-\log 8-2m^{2}\log (1+24m)}\\ &\leqslant 4(1+24m)^{2m^{2}}e^{-\log 8-2m^{2}\log (1+24m)}\\ &=\frac{1}{2} ,\end{aligned}
$$
	Thus there is a $	
	(\varepsilon_F)_{\substack{F\subset\{1,\cdots,m^{2}\}\\|F|=m}}
	$ such that
	$$
	\|R_m\|
	\leqslant
	4m!\left(\frac{e\sqrt{t(1-t)}}{m}\right)^m
	\sqrt{\log8+2m^{2}\log(1+24m)}.
	$$
	Note that
	$$
\| R_{m}^{\#}\| \geqslant R_{m}^{\#}\left( \left( \frac{1}{m^{2}} \right)_{1\leqslant i\leqslant m^{2}} \right) =\frac{m!\binom{m^{2}}{m}}{(m^{2})^{m}}.
	$$
	Together with \eqref{shi-l1-jingquexishushangjie}, this gives
	\begin{equation}
	\begin{aligned}\sqrt[m]{\frac{\frac{m!\binom{m^{2}}{m}}{(m^{2})^{m}}}{4m!\left( \frac{e\sqrt{t(1-t)}}{m} \right)^{m} \sqrt{\log 8+2m^{2}\log (1+24m)}}}&\leqslant \frac{\| R_{m}^{\#}\|^{\frac{1}{m}}}{\| R_{m}\|^{\frac{1}{m}}} \leqslant \frac{\left\lceil \frac{m}{t} \right\rceil}{\left( m! \right)^{\frac{1}{m}} \binom{\left\lceil \frac{m}{t} \right\rceil}{m}^{\frac{1}{2m}}} .\end{aligned}
		\label{shi-l1-jingqueliangbian}
	\end{equation}
Applying
	\eqref{shi-l1-caiyanggenjixian}, we conclude that
	\begin{equation}
		\lim\limits_{m\to\infty}
		\left(
		\frac{\|R_m^{\#}\|}{\|R_m\|}
		\right)^{\frac{1}{m}}
		=
		\frac{1}{\sqrt{t(1-t)}}.
		\label{shi-l1-jingquezengzhang}
	\end{equation}
	
	\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{Step 4}\par\nopagebreak[4]
	
	Put $n_1=0$ and $n_m=n_{m-1}+m^2$ for $m\geqslant2$,
	and define
	$$
	H_0=H_1=0,
	\qquad
	H_m(\textbf{z})\triangleq
	\frac{R_m(z_{n_{m-1}+1},\cdots,z_{n_m})}{\|R_m\|},
	\qquad m\geqslant2.
	$$
Note that
	$$
	\|H_m\|=1,
	\qquad
	\|H_m^{\#}\|=\frac{\|R_m^{\#}\|}{\|R_m\|},
	\qquad
	|H_m(\textbf{z})|
	\leqslant
	\|(P_{n_m}-P_{n_{m-1}})\textbf{z}\|_{\ell^1}^m.
	$$
	Set
	$$
	f(\textbf{z})\triangleq
	\sum\limits_{m=2}^{\infty}H_m(\textbf{z}).
	$$
	
	Fix $\textbf{x}\in\ell^1$. By \eqref{shi-weibushoulian},
	choose $M\geqslant2$ such that
	$\|Q_{n_{M-1}}\textbf{x}\|_{\ell^1}\leqslant\frac{1}{4}$.
	For $\|\textbf{y}-\textbf{x}\|_{\ell^1}<\frac{1}{4}$
	and $m\geqslant M$, 
$$
\begin{aligned}
	\|(P_{n_m}-P_{n_{m-1}})\textbf{y}\|_{\ell^1}
	&\leqslant
	\|(P_{n_m}-P_{n_{m-1}})\textbf{x}\|_{\ell^1}
	+\|(P_{n_m}-P_{n_{m-1}})(\textbf{y}-\textbf{x})\|_{\ell^1}\\
	&\leqslant
	\|Q_{n_{m-1}}\textbf{x}\|_{\ell^1}
	+\|\textbf{y}-\textbf{x}\|_{\ell^1}\leqslant
	\|Q_{n_{M-1}}\textbf{x}\|_{\ell^1}
	+\|\textbf{y}-\textbf{x}\|_{\ell^1}\leqslant\frac{1}{2}.
\end{aligned}
$$
	Hence $\sum\limits_{m=M}^{\infty} \left| H_{m}(\textbf{y} ) \right|\leqslant \sum\limits_{m=M}^{\infty} \frac{1}{2^{m}} <\infty$
	and \eqref{shi-quanchunjixian} gives $f\in H(\ell^1)$. One readily checks that  $H_m$ is the $m$-homogeneous Taylor polynomial of $f$ at $\textbf{0}$. By \eqref{shi-l1-jingquezengzhang},
	$$
	r(f,\textbf{0})=1,
	\qquad
	|r|(f,\textbf{0})=\sqrt{t(1-t)}.
	$$
	Thus the lower constant in \eqref{shi-l1-zuijiabanjing}
	cannot be increased.
	
		\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{Step 5}\par\nopagebreak[4]
		
To prove the optimality of the upper bound, consider
$$
g(\textbf{z})\triangleq
\sum\limits_{m=2}^{\infty}z_m^m\in H(\ell^1),
\qquad
\sup\limits_{\|\textbf{z}\|_{\ell^1}\leqslant1}
|g(\textbf{z})|\leqslant1.
$$
For $m\geqslant2$, the $m$-homogeneous Taylor polynomial of $g$
at $\textbf{0}$ is $H_m(\textbf{z})=z_m^m$. Hence
$
\|H_m\|=\|H_m^{\#}\|=1,
$
which gives
$
r(g,\textbf{0})=|r|(g,\textbf{0})=1.
$
This completes the proof of
Theorem~\ref{dingli-l1-zuijiabanjing}.
<!-- end proof-34 -->
<!-- theorem-7: body_md -->
\label{dingli-duoxiangshishoulianyu}
	For every $1\leqslant p\leqslant\infty$,
	\begin{equation}
		\mathrm{dom}\mathcal{P}^{1}(\ell^p)=\ell^p,
		\qquad
		\mathrm{dom}\mathcal{P}^{1}(c_0)=c_0.
		\label{shi-yiciduoxiangshishoulian}
	\end{equation}
	For every $m\in\mathbb{N}$,
	\begin{equation}
		\mathrm{dom}\mathcal{P}^{m}(\ell^1)=\ell^1.
		\label{shi-yikeduoxiangshishoulian}
	\end{equation}
	For every $m\geqslant2$,
	\begin{equation}
		\mathrm{dom}\mathcal{P}^{m}(c_0)
		=
		\mathrm{dom}\mathcal{P}^{m}(\ell^\infty)
		=
		\ell_{\frac{2m}{m-1},\infty}.
		\label{shi-youjieduoxiangshishoulian}
	\end{equation}
	For $1<p<\infty$ and $m\geqslant2$, define
	\begin{equation}
		\frac{1}{q_m}
		\triangleq
		\frac{1}{p}+
		\frac{m-1}{m}
		\left(1-\frac{1}{\min\{p,2\}}\right).
		\label{shi-duoxiangshiqm}
	\end{equation}
	Then
	\begin{equation}
		\begin{aligned}
			\mathrm{dom}\mathcal{P}^{m}(\ell^p)
			&=
			\ell_{q_m,\max\{p,m\}}
		\end{aligned}
		\label{shi-duoxiangshishoulianyu}
	\end{equation}
<!-- end theorem-7 -->
<!-- remark-7: body_md -->
If we additionally require that, at every point of monomial convergence, the corresponding monomial series converges to the original polynomial, then Proposition~\ref{mingti-jishubiaoshi} shows that all the above characterizations remain unchanged except for $\mathrm{dom}\mathcal{P}^{1}(\ell^{\infty})$. Indeed, by Proposition~\ref{mingti-jishubiaoshi}, every $\textbf{z}\in c_0$ belongs to the corresponding set of monomial convergence. Conversely, if $\textbf{z}\in\ell^\infty\setminus c_0$, the Hahn--Banach theorem gives $G\in(\ell^\infty)^*$ such that $G|_{c_0}=0$ and $G(\textbf{z})\neq0$. Since $e_i\in c_0$ for every $i\in\mathbb N$, we have
$
\sum\limits_{i=1}^{\infty}G(e_i)z_i=0.
$
Therefore, the corresponding set of monomial convergence for $\mathcal{P}^{1}(\ell^{\infty})$ is exactly $c_0$.
<!-- end remark-7 -->
<!-- remark-8: body_md -->
\label{rem-11}
Let $X=c_0$ or $X=\ell^p$ with $1\leqslant p\leqslant\infty$. The identities in \eqref{shi-yiciduoxiangshishoulian} are elementary, since
$$
\sum\limits_{i=1}^{\infty}
\left|c_{e_i}(G)z_i\right|
\leqslant
\|G\|\,\|\textbf{z}\|_X,
\qquad
G\in\mathcal{P}^{1}(X),\quad
\textbf{z}=(z_i)_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\in X.
$$
The identity \eqref{shi-yikeduoxiangshishoulian} follows directly
from \eqref{shi-yikeheshoulian}, since
$\mathcal{P}^m(\ell^1)\subset H(\ell^1)$; see also
\cite[Theorem~3.1, p.~19]{DMP09}.
For $m\geqslant2$, the identities in
\eqref{shi-youjieduoxiangshishoulian} were established in
\cite[Theorem~2.1 and equations~(15)--(16)]{BDFMS17}.
Moreover, when $2\leqslant m\leqslant p<\infty$, the sufficient
inclusion in \eqref{shi-duoxiangshishoulianyu} already follows
from \cite[Theorem~5.1\,(3)]{BDS19}.
<!-- end remark-8 -->
<!-- proof-35: body_md -->
\raggedbottom
By Remark~\ref{rem-11}, it remains to prove
\eqref{shi-duoxiangshishoulianyu}.
Fix $1<p<\infty$ and $m\geqslant2$, and put
\begin{equation}
\sigma\triangleq1-\frac{1}{\min\{p,2\}},
\qquad r\triangleq\max\{p,m\}.
\label{shi-duoxiangshi-canshu}
\end{equation}
Thus 
\begin{equation}
\frac{1}{q_m}=\frac{1}{p}+\frac{m-1}{m}\sigma,
\qquad 1<q_m<p\leqslant r,
\qquad r\geqslant m.
\label{shi-duoxiangshi-q-sigma}
\end{equation}
For an $m$-homogeneous polynomial $G$, write
$$
S_{\textbf{z}}(G)\triangleq
\sum\limits_{\substack{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_0^{(\mathbb{N})}\\|\alpha|=m}}
|c_\alpha(G)\textbf{z}^{\alpha}|,
$$
allowing the value $+\infty$.
We first prove that there exist $c,C>0$, depending only on
$m,p$, such that, for every $\textbf{z}\in\mathbb{C}^{(\mathbb{N})}$,
\begin{equation}
\begin{aligned}
c\left(\sum\limits_{n=1}^{\infty}
n^{\frac{r}{q_m}-1}(z_n^*)^r\right)^{\frac{m}{r}}
&\leqslant
\sup\limits_{\substack{
G\in\mathscr{P}_0\cap\mathcal{P}^{m}(\ell^p)\\\|G\|\leqslant1}}
S_{\textbf{z}}(G)&\leqslant
C\left(\sum\limits_{n=1}^{\infty}
n^{\frac{r}{q_m}-1}(z_n^*)^r\right)^{\frac{m}{r}}.
\end{aligned}
\label{shi-duoxiangshi-liangceguji}
\end{equation}

\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{Step 1}\par\nopagebreak[4]
Without loss of generality, we may assume
$z_1\geqslant z_2\geqslant\cdots\geqslant0$.
Put
$$
D_k\triangleq\{2^k,\cdots,2^{k+1}-1\},
\qquad k\in\mathbb{N}_0.
$$
Note that for every $k\in \mathbb{N}_0$, 
$$
2^{\frac{kr}{q_m}}z_{2^{k+1}}^r
\leqslant\sum\limits_{n\in D_k}n^{\frac{r}{q_m}-1}z_n^r
\leqslant2^{\frac{r}{q_m}-1}
\left(2^{\frac{k}{q_m}}z_{2^k}\right)^r.
$$
Summing the above inequalities over $k=0,1,2,\cdots$, we obtain
\begin{equation}
2^{1-\frac{r}{q_m}}
\sum\limits_{n=1}^{\infty}n^{\frac{r}{q_m}-1}z_n^r
\leqslant\sum\limits_{k=0}^{\infty}
\left(2^{\frac{k}{q_m}}z_{2^k}\right)^r
\leqslant\left(1+2^{\frac{r}{q_m}}\right)
\sum\limits_{n=1}^{\infty}n^{\frac{r}{q_m}-1}z_n^r.
\label{shi-duoxiangshi-erjinzhi}
\end{equation}

\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{Step 2}\par\nopagebreak[4]

We prove the upper estimate in \eqref{shi-duoxiangshi-liangceguji}.
Suppose first that $p\geqslant m$.
Put $\textbf{u} \triangleq \left( j^{\frac{m-1}{2m}}z_{j} \right)_{j\in \mathbb{N}}=\left( u_{j} \right)_{j\in \mathbb{N}}$ and
$\textbf{v} \triangleq \left( j^{-\frac{m-1}{2m}} \right)_{j\in \mathbb{N}}$.
By \eqref{shi-youjieduoxiangshishoulian},
$\textbf{v}\in\mathrm{dom}\mathcal{P}^m(c_0)$.
The closed graph argument in the proof of
Lemma~\ref{yinli-bituguji}, applied to $\mathcal{P}^m(c_0)$,
therefore gives $C_m>0$ such that
\begin{equation}
	S_{\textbf{v}}(Q)\leqslant C_m\|Q\|\label{shi-yy}
\end{equation} for every
$Q\in\mathcal{P}^m(c_0)$.

For $G\in\mathscr{P}_0\cap\mathcal{P}^m(\ell^p)$,
note that
$$
\widetilde{G}:c_0\to\mathbb{C},
\quad
\textbf{x}=(x_j)_{j\in\mathbb N}
\mapsto G\left((u_jx_j)_{j\in\mathbb N}\right)
$$
belongs to $\mathcal{P}^m(c_0)$.
Applying \eqref{shi-yy} and using
$c_\alpha(\widetilde{G})
=\textbf{u}^\alpha c_\alpha(G)$
for every $\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_0^{(\mathbb{N})}$,
we obtain
$$
\begin{aligned}S_{\textbf{z}}(G)&=S_{\textbf{v}}(\widetilde{G} )\leqslant C_{m}\| \widetilde{G} \| \leqslant C_{m}\sup\limits_{\textbf{x} \in B_{c_{0}}} \| G\| \left\| (u_{j}x_{j})_{j\in \mathbb{N}} \right\|_{\ell^{p}}^{m}\\ &\leqslant C_{m}\| G\| \| \textbf{u} \|_{\ell^{p}}^{m} =C_{m}\| G\| \left( \sum\limits_{j=1}^{\infty} j^{\frac{p\left( m-1 \right)}{2m}}z_{j}^{p} \right)^{\frac{m}{p}}\\ &=C_{m}\| G\| \left( \sum\limits_{j=1}^{\infty} j^{\frac{p}{q_{m}} -1}z_{j}^{p} \right)^{\frac{m}{p}} =C_{m}\| G\| \left( \sum\limits_{j=1}^{\infty} j^{\frac{r}{q_{m}} -1}z_{j}^{r} \right)^{\frac{m}{p}}\end{aligned}
$$

Now suppose that $1<p<m$.
Fix $G\in\mathscr{P}_0\cap\mathcal{P}^m(\ell^p)$ and choose
$N\in\mathbb{N}_0$ such that
$G=G\circ P_{2^{N+1}-1}$ and
$\mathrm{supp}\textbf{z}\subset\{1,\cdots,2^{N+1}-1\}$.
For $0\leqslant k_1\leqslant\cdots\leqslant k_m\leqslant N$, put
$$
A_{k_1,\cdots,k_m}\triangleq\left\{
\sum\limits_{j=1}^{m}e_{i_j}:
i_1\leqslant\cdots\leqslant i_m,\quad
i_j\in D_{k_j},\ 1\leqslant j\leqslant m
\right\}.\qquad \textbf{w}\triangleq
\sum\limits_{k\in\{k_1,\cdots,k_m\}}
2^{-\frac{k}{p}}\sum\limits_{i\in D_k}e_i.
$$
Then
$$
\left\{
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_0^{(\mathbb{N})}:
|\alpha|=m,\quad
\mathrm{supp}\,\alpha\subset\{1,\cdots,2^{N+1}-1\}
\right\}
=
\bigsqcup\limits_{0\leqslant k_1\leqslant\cdots\leqslant k_m\leqslant N}
A_{k_1,\cdots,k_m}.
$$
Similarly to \eqref{shi-shanjianzhiji}, we define
$$
A_{k_1,\cdots,k_m}^-
\triangleq\left\{
\sum\limits_{j=1}^{m-1}e_{i_j}:
i_1\leqslant\cdots\leqslant i_{m-1},\quad
i_j\in D_{k_j},\ 1\leqslant j\leqslant m-1
\right\}.
$$
One has
$$
|A_{k_1,\cdots,k_m}^-|
\leqslant\prod\limits_{j=1}^{m-1}|D_{k_j}|
=2^{\sum\limits_{j=1}^{m-1}k_j},\qquad\| \textbf{w} \|_{\ell^{p}}^{p} =\sum\limits_{k\in \left\{ k_{1},k_{2},\cdots ,k_{m} \right\}} \frac{\left| D_{k} \right|}{2^{k}} =\left| \left\{ k_{1},\cdots ,k_{m} \right\} \right| \leqslant m.
$$

Applying \eqref{shi-zhijixishuguji}  and 
\eqref{shi-duoxiangshi-q-sigma}, we obtain
$$
\begin{aligned}\sum\limits_{\alpha \in A_{k_{1},\cdots ,k_{m}}} |c_{\alpha}(G)\textbf{z}^{\alpha} |&=\sum\limits_{\alpha \in A_{k_{1},\cdots ,k_{m}}} \left( |c_{\alpha}(G)|\prod\limits_{j=1}^{m} \left| z_{i_{j}} \right| \right)\leqslant \sum\limits_{\alpha \in A_{k_{1},\cdots ,k_{m}}} \left( |c_{\alpha}(G)|\prod\limits_{j=1}^{m} \left| z_{2^{k_{j}}} \right| \right)\\ &=\sum\limits_{\alpha \in A_{k_{1},\cdots ,k_{m}}} \left( |c_{\alpha}(G)|\cdot \prod\limits_{j=1}^{m} 2^{-\frac{k_{j}}{p}}\cdot \prod\limits_{j=1}^{m} \left( 2^{\frac{k_{j}}{p}}z_{2^{k_{j}}} \right) \right)\\ &=\sum\limits_{\alpha \in A_{k_{1},\cdots ,k_{m}}} \left( |c_{\alpha}(G)|\cdot \textbf{w}^{\alpha} \prod\limits_{j=1}^{m} \left( 2^{\frac{k_{j}}{p}}z_{2^{k_{j}}} \right) \right)\\ &\leqslant e^{2m}|A_{k_{1},\cdots ,k_{m}}^{-}|^{\sigma}\left\| \textbf{w} \right\|_{\ell^{p}}^{m} \| G\| \prod\limits_{j=1}^{m} \left( 2^{\frac{k_{j}}{p}}z_{2^{k_{j}}} \right)\\ &\leqslant e^{2m}m^{\frac{m}{p}}\| G\| 2^{\sigma \sum\limits_{j=1}^{m-1} k_{j}}\prod\limits_{j=1}^{m} \left( 2^{\frac{k_{j}}{p}}z_{2^{k_{j}}} \right)\\ &=e^{2m}m^{\frac{m}{p}}\| G\| 2^{\sigma \sum\limits_{j=1}^{m-1} k_{j}}\prod\limits_{j=1}^{m} \left( 2^{\frac{k_{j}}{q_{m}} -\frac{\sigma \left( m-1 \right) k_{j}}{m}}z_{2^{k_{j}}} \right)\\ &=e^{2m}m^{\frac{m}{p}}\| G\| 2^{-\frac{\sigma}{m} \sum\limits_{j=1}^{m-1} (k_{m}-k_{j})}\prod\limits_{j=1}^{m} \left( 2^{\frac{k_{j}}{q_{m}}}z_{2^{k_{j}}} \right) .\end{aligned}
$$
Summing the preceding inequality over
$0\leqslant k_1\leqslant\cdots\leqslant k_m\leqslant N$
and using $z_{2^k}=0$ for $k>N$, we obtain
\begin{equation}
S_{\textbf{z}}(G)\leqslant e^{2m}m^{\frac{m}{p}}\|G\|
\sum\limits_{0\leqslant k_1\leqslant\cdots\leqslant k_m}
2^{-\frac{\sigma}{m}\sum\limits_{j=1}^{m-1}(k_m-k_j)}
\prod\limits_{j=1}^{m}
\left(2^{\frac{k_j}{q_m}}z_{2^{k_j}}\right).
\label{shi-duoxiangshi-fenkuai}
\end{equation}
We have
\begin{equation}
\begin{aligned}
	&\sum\limits_{0\leqslant k_1\leqslant\cdots\leqslant k_m}
	2^{-\frac{\sigma}{m}\sum\limits_{j=1}^{m-1}(k_m-k_j)}
	\prod\limits_{j=1}^{m}
	\left(2^{\frac{k_j}{q_m}}z_{2^{k_j}}\right)
	\\
	&\qquad=
	\sum\limits_{0=k_1\leqslant\cdots\leqslant k_m}
	\prod\limits_{j=1}^{m-1}
	2^{-\frac{\sigma j}{m}(k_{j+1}-k_j)}
	\sum\limits_{k=0}^{\infty}
	\prod\limits_{j=1}^{m}
	\left(2^{\frac{k+k_j}{q_m}}z_{2^{k+k_j}}\right)
	\\
	&\qquad\leqslant
	\frac{1}{m}
	\sum\limits_{0=k_1\leqslant\cdots\leqslant k_m}
	\prod\limits_{j=1}^{m-1}
	2^{-\frac{\sigma j}{m}(k_{j+1}-k_j)}
	\sum\limits_{j=1}^{m}
	\sum\limits_{k=0}^{\infty}
	\left(2^{\frac{k+k_j}{q_m}}z_{2^{k+k_j}}\right)^m
	\\
	&\qquad\leqslant
	\sum\limits_{0=k_1\leqslant\cdots\leqslant k_m}
	\prod\limits_{j=1}^{m-1}
	2^{-\frac{\sigma j}{m}(k_{j+1}-k_j)}
	\sum\limits_{k=0}^{\infty}
	\left(2^{\frac{k}{q_m}}z_{2^k}\right)^m
	\\
	&\qquad=
	\prod\limits_{j=1}^{m-1}
	\left(\sum\limits_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{-\frac{\sigma j}{m}k}\right)
	\sum\limits_{k=0}^{\infty}
	\left(2^{\frac{k}{q_m}}z_{2^k}\right)^m
	\\
	&\qquad=
	\prod\limits_{j=1}^{m-1}
	\left(1-2^{-\frac{\sigma j}{m}}\right)^{-1}
	\sum\limits_{k=0}^{\infty}
	\left(2^{\frac{k}{q_m}}z_{2^k}\right)^m,
\end{aligned}
\label{shi-duoxiangshi-xianglinchaguji}
\end{equation}
where the first equality follows by subtracting the smallest index from each $k_j$ and using
$\sum\limits_{j=1}^{m-1}(k_m-k_j)
=\sum\limits_{j=1}^{m-1}j(k_{j+1}-k_j)$,
and the first inequality follows from the arithmetic--geometric mean inequality.

Combining \eqref{shi-duoxiangshi-fenkuai},
\eqref{shi-duoxiangshi-xianglinchaguji}, and
\eqref{shi-duoxiangshi-erjinzhi}, with $r=m$, proves the upper
estimate in \eqref{shi-duoxiangshi-liangceguji}.

\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{Step 3}\par\nopagebreak[4]

We prove the lower estimate in \eqref{shi-duoxiangshi-liangceguji}.
Put $C_0\triangleq16^{2\sigma}(m!\log m)^\sigma$.
For each $n\in\mathbb{N}$, let $R$ be the polynomial given by
Lemma~\ref{yinli-suijiduoxiangshi}, namely,
$$
R(\textbf{u})=
\sum\limits_{\substack{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_0^n\\|\alpha|=m}}
\varepsilon_\alpha\frac{m!}{\alpha!}\textbf{u}^{\alpha},
\qquad\varepsilon_\alpha\in\{-1,1\}.
$$
Applying the classical inequality
$$
\|\textbf{u}\|_{\ell^{\min\{p,2\}}}
\leqslant
n^{\frac{1}{\min\{p,2\}}-\frac{1}{p}}
\|\textbf{u}\|_{\ell^p},
\quad \textbf{u}\in\mathbb{C}^n
$$
together with \eqref{shi-suijiduoxiangshiguji}, we obtain
$$
\begin{aligned}\sup\limits_{\| \textbf{u} \|_{\ell^{p}} \leqslant 1} |R(\textbf{u} )|&\leqslant \sup\limits_{\| \textbf{u} \|_{\ell^{\min\{p,2\}}} \leqslant n^{\frac{1}{\min\{p,2\}} -\frac{1}{p}}} |R(\textbf{u} )|\\ &=\sup\limits_{\| \textbf{u} \|_{\ell^{\min\{p,2\}}} \leqslant 1} \left| R\left( n^{\frac{1}{\min\{p,2\}} -\frac{1}{p}}\textbf{u} \right) \right|\\ &=n^{\frac{m}{\min\{p,2\}} -\frac{m}{p}} \sup\limits_{\| \textbf{u} \|_{\ell^{\min\{p,2\}}} \leqslant 1} \left| R\left( \textbf{u} \right) \right|\\ &\leqslant C_{0}n^{\frac{m}{\min\{p,2\}} -\frac{m}{p}}n^{\sigma}=C_{0}n^{m\left( 1-\frac{1}{q_{m}} \right)}.\end{aligned}
$$
For each $k\in\mathbb{N}_0$, choose such a polynomial $R_k$
in $n=2^k$ variables and define
$$
G_k(\textbf{x})\triangleq
\frac{R_k(x_{2^k},\cdots,x_{2^{k+1}-1})}
{C_0\,2^{km\left(1-\frac{1}{q_m}\right)}},
\qquad\textbf{x}\in\ell^p.
$$
Then $|G_k(\textbf{x})|\leqslant
\|P_{D_k}\textbf{x}\|_{\ell^p}^m$ and
\begin{equation}
	S_{\textbf{z}}(G_k)
	=\frac{\left(\sum\limits_{j\in D_k}z_j\right)^m}{C_0\,2^{km\left(1-\frac{1}{q_m}\right)}}
	\geqslant\frac{\left(2^kz_{2^{k+1}}\right)^m}{C_0\,2^{km\left(1-\frac{1}{q_m}\right)}}
	=\frac{1}{C_0}\left(2^{\frac{k}{q_m}}z_{2^{k+1}}\right)^m.
	\label{shi-duoxiangshi-kuai-zhixishu}
\end{equation}
and \begin{equation}
S_{\textbf{z}}(G_0)=\frac{z_1^m}{C_0}.
\label{shi-duoxiangshi-shoukuai}
\end{equation}

Let $\left(\frac{r}{m}\right)'=
\begin{cases}
	\frac{r}{r-m},&r>m,\\
	\infty,&r=m.
\end{cases}$.
For $N\in\mathbb{N}_0$ and
$\textbf{a}=(a_0,\cdots,a_N)\in\mathbb{C}^{N+1}$,
H\"older's inequality, $r\geqslant p$ and monotonicity of $q\mapsto \left\| \cdot \right\|_{\ell^{q}}$ gives
$$
\begin{aligned}\left|\sum\limits_{k=0}^{N} a_{k}G_{k}\left( \textbf{x} \right) \right|&\leqslant \sum\limits_{k=0}^{N} |a_{k}|\| P_{D_{k}}\textbf{x} \|_{\ell^{p}}^{m} \leqslant \| \textbf{a} \|_{\ell^{\left( \frac{r}{m} \right)^{\prime}}} \left( \sum\limits_{k=0}^{N} \| P_{D_{k}}\textbf{x} \|_{\ell^{p}}^{r} \right)^{\frac{m}{r}}\\ &\leqslant \| \textbf{a} \|_{\ell^{\left( \frac{r}{m} \right)^{\prime}}} \left( \sum\limits_{k=0}^{N} \| P_{D_{k}}\textbf{x} \|_{\ell^{p}}^{p} \right)^{\frac{m}{p}} \leqslant \| \textbf{a} \|_{\ell^{\left( \frac{r}{m} \right)^{\prime}}} \| \textbf{x} \|_{\ell^{p}}^{m}.\end{aligned}
$$

Thus 
$$\left\| \sum\limits_{k=0}^{N} a_{k}G_{k}\left( \cdot \right) \right\|\leqslant\|\textbf{a}\|_{\ell^{\left(\frac{r}{m}\right)'}},\qquad S_{\textbf{z}}\left(\sum\limits_{k=0}^{N} a_{k}G_{k}\left( \cdot \right) \right)=\sum\limits_{k=0}^{N} |a_{k}|S_{\textbf{z}}(G_{k}).$$
Finite-dimensional duality, followed by taking the supremum over
$N$, therefore yields
\begin{equation}
\begin{aligned}
	\sup\limits_{\substack{G\in\mathscr{P}_0\cap\mathcal{P}^{m}(\ell^p)\\\|G\|\leqslant1}}S_{\textbf{z}}(G)
	&\geqslant\sup\limits_{N\geqslant0}\sup\limits_{\|\textbf{a}\|_{\ell^{\left(\frac{r}{m}\right)'}}\leqslant1}S_{\textbf{z}}\left(\sum\limits_{k=0}^{N} a_{k}G_{k}\left( \cdot \right) \right)
	=\sup\limits_{N\geqslant0}\sup\limits_{\|\textbf{a}\|_{\ell^{\left(\frac{r}{m}\right)'}}\leqslant1}\sum\limits_{k=0}^{N}|a_k|S_{\textbf{z}}(G_k)\\
	&=\sup\limits_{N\geqslant0}\left(\sum\limits_{k=0}^{N}S^{\frac{r}{m}}_{\textbf{z}}(G_k)\right)^{\frac{m}{r}}
	=\left(\sum\limits_{k=0}^{\infty}S^{\frac{r}{m}}_{\textbf{z}}(G_k)\right)^{\frac{m}{r}}.
\end{aligned}
\label{shi-duoxiangshi-kuai-duiou}
\end{equation}
By \eqref{shi-duoxiangshi-kuai-zhixishu} and
\eqref{shi-duoxiangshi-shoukuai},
$$
\begin{aligned}
\sum\limits_{k=0}^{\infty}
\left(2^{\frac{k}{q_m}}z_{2^k}\right)^r
&=z_1^r+2^{\frac{r}{q_m}}
\sum\limits_{k=0}^{\infty}
\left(2^{\frac{k}{q_m}}z_{2^{k+1}}\right)^r\leqslant C_0^{\frac{r}{m}}\left(1+2^{\frac{r}{q_m}}\right)
\sum\limits_{k=0}^{\infty}S^{\frac{r}{m}}_{\textbf{z}}(G_k).
\end{aligned}
$$
Together with \eqref{shi-duoxiangshi-erjinzhi} and
\eqref{shi-duoxiangshi-kuai-duiou}, this proves the lower estimate
in \eqref{shi-duoxiangshi-liangceguji}.


\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{Step 4}\par\nopagebreak[4]

First, $\ell_{q_m,r}\subset\ell^p$. Indeed, for $\textbf{z}\in\ell_{q_m,r}$, since $r\geqslant p> q_m$ and $$\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}j^{\frac{r}{q_m}-1}\geqslant\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}\int\limits_{j-1}^{j}t^{\frac{r}{q_m}-1}\,\mathrm{d}t
=\int\limits_0^n t^{\frac{r}{q_m}-1}\,\mathrm{d}t
=\frac{q_m}{r}n^{\frac{r}{q_m}},$$ we have
$$
\begin{aligned}
	\sum\limits_{n=1}^{\infty}(z_n^*)^p
	&=\sum\limits_{n=1}^{\infty}n^{-\frac{p}{q_m}}
	\left(n^{\frac{r}{q_m}}(z_n^*)^r\right)^{\frac{p}{r}}
	\leqslant\sum\limits_{n=1}^{\infty}n^{-\frac{p}{q_m}}
	\left(\frac{r}{q_m}\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}j^{\frac{r}{q_m}-1}(z_n^*)^r\right)^{\frac{p}{r}}\\
	&\leqslant\sum\limits_{n=1}^{\infty}n^{-\frac{p}{q_m}}
	\left(\frac{r}{q_m}\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}j^{\frac{r}{q_m}-1}(z_j^*)^r\right)^{\frac{p}{r}}\\
	&\leqslant\left(\frac{r}{q_m}\sum\limits_{j=1}^{\infty}j^{\frac{r}{q_m}-1}(z_j^*)^r\right)^{\frac{p}{r}}
	\sum\limits_{n=1}^{\infty}n^{-\frac{p}{q_m}}<\infty.
\end{aligned}
$$


Arguing as in the proof of Lemma~\ref{yinli-gujituiguang},
we extend the upper estimate in \eqref{shi-duoxiangshi-liangceguji}
to $\textbf{z}\in\ell_{q_m,r}$ and
$G\in\mathcal{P}^m(\ell^p)$. Consequently,
$$
S_{\textbf{z}}(G)\leqslant C\|G\|
\left(\sum\limits_{j=1}^{\infty}
j^{\frac{r}{q_m}-1}(z_j^*)^r\right)^{\frac{m}{r}}<\infty,
$$
which proves
$\ell_{q_m,r}\subseteq\mathrm{dom}\mathcal{P}^m(\ell^p)$.



Conversely, let $\textbf{z}=\left( z_{i} \right)_{i\in \mathbb{N}}\in\mathrm{dom}\mathcal{P}^m(\ell^p)$.
By the same closed graph argument as in the proof of
Lemma~\ref{yinli-bituguji}, applied to the coefficient map
$G\mapsto(c_\alpha(G)\textbf{z}^{\alpha})_{|\alpha|=m}$
on the Banach space $\mathcal{P}^m(\ell^p)$,
there exists $K>0$ such that
\begin{equation}
	S_{\textbf{z}}(G)\leqslant K\|G\|,
	\qquad G\in\mathcal{P}^m(\ell^p).
	\label{shi-duoxiangshi-bitu}
\end{equation}

Since $S_{P_n\textbf{z}}(G)\leqslant S_{\textbf{z}}(G)$, the lower
estimate in \eqref{shi-duoxiangshi-liangceguji} implies
$$
c\left(\sum\limits_{j=1}^{\infty}
j^{\frac{r}{q_m}-1}\bigl((P_n\textbf{z})_j^*\bigr)^r
\right)^{\frac{m}{r}}\leqslant K.
$$
As $\textbf{z}\in\ell^p\subset c_0$,
$(P_n\textbf{z})_j^*\uparrow z_j^*$ for every $j\in \mathbb{N}$.
Monotone convergence yields
$$
\sum\limits_{j=1}^{\infty}j^{\frac{r}{q_m}-1}(z_j^*)^r
\leqslant\left(\frac{K}{c}\right)^{\frac{r}{m}}<\infty.
$$
Thus $\mathrm{dom}\mathcal{P}^m(\ell^p)\subseteq\ell_{q_m,r}$,
which completes the proof of \eqref{shi-duoxiangshishoulianyu}.
